We begin by introducing Verdier’s specialization functor, following
[
Ver81
]
. If
i:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
is a closed immersion, we consider the normal cone
C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}
over
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
and the deformation to the normal cone
D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}
. This construction works also for stacks, see
[
AP24
]
. More precisely, if we denote by
\mathcal{I}
the sheaf of ideals defining
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
, we have by definition that
D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
is the relative spectrum of the
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathcal{I}^{j}\boxtimes s^{-j}
(where we use the convention
\mathcal{I}^{j}=\mathcal{O}_{X}
for
j\leq 0
), such that
s
gives a map
s:D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
. We have then
s^{-1}(0)=C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}
, which is the relative spectrum over
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
of
\bigoplus_{j\geq 0}\mathcal{I}^{j}/\mathcal{I}^{j+1}
. One obtains the following commutative diagram with Cartesian squares:

(4.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

We define as in Verdier
[
Ver81
, Section 8]
the specialization functor:

(4.23)

\displaystyle Sp_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}:=\psi_{s}q^{\ast}:SH(\mathcal{X})\to SH(C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X})

Notice that a motivic version of Verdier’s specialization functor was also defined by Ayoub in
[
Ayo22
, Section 3.2]
, under the name of ’monodromic specialization system’.

Consider the commutative diagram with Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

where the composite function is
t:=pr_{2}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
. Consider the morphism:

(4.24)

\displaystyle 0^{\ast}Sp_{X^{\prime}}:=0^{\ast}\psi_{s}q^{\ast}\to\psi_{t}(i_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{\ast}q^{\ast}\simeq\psi_{t}(q^{\prime})^{\ast}i^{\ast}\simeq i^{\ast}

where the last isomorphism follows from
[
Ayo07b
, Lemme 3.5.10]
(namely, the nearby cycle acts as the identity on constant objects). From
[
Ver81
, Section 8 SP5, Section 9]
, this morphism is an isomorphism for schemes in the Betti case, hence it is also an isomorphism for stacks and
\mathcal{D}
by conservativity.

Consider now the case where
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}=\mathcal{X}_{0}=f^{-1}(0)
for
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
is a principal divisor: we obtain that
D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}
is the relative spectrum of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}[S,T]/(ST-f)
, and
C_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\mathcal{X}\simeq\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
. We have then that
C_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}X\to D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}X
is given by
(x,t)\mapsto(x,0,t)
, and
\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}X
by
(x,s)\mapsto(x,s,f(x)/s)
. In this case, from the functoriality of the deformation to the normal cone and the fact that
\psi_{f}
underlies a specialization system,
Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}:SH(\mathcal{X})\to SH(\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k})
underlies a specialization system over:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0\circ pr_{1}}

we will denote it also by
Sp_{f}
when it is convenient. Then the isomorphism
0^{\ast}Sp_{f}\simeq(i_{0})^{\ast}
from (
4.24
) is an isomorphism of specialization systems over
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\rightarrow\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\leftarrow\{0\}
.

Consider the open immersion:

\displaystyle v:

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\mathcal{X}

(4.25)

\displaystyle(x,t)\mapsto(x,f(x)/t,t)

such that
s\circ v=f/t
, and consider the commutative diagram with Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Spec}(k)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

We obtain then the following isomorphism of specialization systems:

(4.26)

\displaystyle(j_{0})^{\ast}Sp_{f}:=(j_{0})^{\ast}\psi_{\pi}q^{\ast}\simeq\psi_{f/t}(u_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{\ast}q^{\ast}

in particular,
Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}
is monodromic from
[
Ver81
, Proposition 7.1, Section 9]
and conservativity, and we have
\pi_{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\simeq 0^{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}
. Using (
4.24
), we find that the morphism of specialization systems:

(4.27)

\displaystyle(j_{0})_{!}(j_{0})^{\ast}\pi^{\ast}0^{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\simeq(j_{0})_{!}(j_{0})^{\ast}\pi^{\ast}\pi_{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\to(j_{0})_{!}(j_{0})^{\ast}Sp_{f}

is isomorphic to the morphism of specialization systems:

(4.28)

\displaystyle(j_{0})_{!}(i_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{\ast}q^{\ast}\to(j_{0})_{!}\psi_{f/t}(u_{\mathbb{G}_{m}})^{\ast}q^{\ast}

We find then, using the exact triangle defining
\Pi_{\ast}
and
\phi_{f/t}
, an isomorphism of specialization systems:

(4.29)

\displaystyle\phi^{mon}_{f}:=(j_{0})_{!}\phi_{f/t}q^{\ast}\simeq(j_{0})_{!}(j_{0})^{\ast}\Pi_{\ast}Sp_{f}\simeq\Pi_{\ast}Sp_{f}

where the last isomorphism follows from the fact that
Sp_{f}
is monodromic. We will now use this representation of the monodromic vanishing cycle functor to build the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism. Notice that, to obtain an homotopy-coherent formalism, one should adopt this as a definition of
\phi^{mon}
from scratch, but the definition
(j_{0})_{!}\phi_{f/t}q^{\ast}
is the classical one, closer to the usual intuition.

Now, we notice that the monoidality of the nearby cycle functor induces the monoidality of Verdier’s specialization. Given two closed immersions
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{Y}
, consider the closed immersion
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
, such that
C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}\times C_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y}\simeq C_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})
. We denote
s:D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
,
s_{\mathcal{X}}:D_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
,
s_{\mathcal{Y}}:D_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
,
q:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
,
q_{\mathcal{X}}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to\mathcal{X}
,
q_{\mathcal{Y}}:\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to\mathcal{Y}
. We have
s=s_{\mathcal{X}}\times_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}s_{\mathcal{Y}}
, which gives an isomorphism:

\displaystyle Sp_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}\boxtimes Sp_{\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle:=(\psi_{s_{\mathcal{X}}}(q_{\mathcal{X}})^{\ast})\boxtimes(\psi_{s_{\mathcal{Y}}}(q_{\mathcal{Y}})^{\ast})\simeq\psi_{s}\delta^{\ast}((q_{\mathcal{X}})^{\ast}\boxtimes_{\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}(q_{\mathcal{Y}})^{\ast})\simeq\psi_{s}\Delta^{\ast}((q_{\mathcal{X}})^{\ast}\boxtimes(q_{\mathcal{Y}})^{\ast})\simeq\psi_{s}q^{\ast}(-\boxtimes-)

(4.30)

\displaystyle=:Sp_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\times\mathcal{Y}^{\prime}}(-\boxtimes-)

where the first isomorphism comes from the monoidality of nearby cycles, and the third isomorphism comes from
(q_{\mathcal{X}}\times q_{\mathcal{Y}})\circ\Delta=q
. From the monoidality of the nearby cycle and the deformation to the normal cone, these isomorphisms satisfy the obvious commutativity and associativity condition.

Consider now the case of two closed immersions
\mathcal{X}_{0}=f^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{X}
,
\mathcal{Y}_{0}=g^{-1}(0)\to\mathcal{Y}
of principal divisors: in this case, (
4.2.3
) underlies an isomorphism of specialization system. We have that
D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
is the relative spectrum of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}[S,T_{\mathcal{X}},T_{\mathcal{Y}}]/(ST_{\mathcal{X}}-f,ST_{\mathcal{Y}}-g)
, and
D_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})\to\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
is the relative spectrum of the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}
-module
\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}[S,T]/(ST-(f+g))
. We obtain then a morphism over
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
:

\displaystyle p

\displaystyle:D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})\to D_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})

(4.31)

\displaystyle:(x,y,s,t_{\mathcal{X}},t_{\mathcal{Y}})\mapsto(x,y,s,t_{\mathcal{X}}+t_{\mathcal{Y}})

and a commutative diagram with Cartesian squares:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k\times\mu}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}

We have then a natural morphism:

(4.32)

\displaystyle k^{\ast}Sp_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}}:=k^{\ast}\psi_{s}q^{\ast}\to k^{\ast}(\mu\times k)_{\ast}\psi_{s\circ p}q^{\ast}=:k^{\ast}(\mu\times k)_{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}}\simeq\mu_{\ast}(Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\boxtimes Sp_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})

which is a specialization system in each variables, where we have used (
4.2.3
) and
k^{\ast}k_{\ast}\simeq Id
in the last isomorphism. From the commutativity of (
4.2.3
) and the deformation to the normal cone, this morphism is obviously commutative. If we consider furthermore a map
h:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, have a commutative diagram of stack:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times\mathcal{Y}_{0}\times\mathcal{Z}_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}\times\mathcal{Z}_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\mathcal{X}_{0}\times(\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})_{0}}(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\times\mathcal{Z})}

Using the fact that the exchange morphism with respect to pushforward are compatible with composition for the specialization system
\Psi
, and the associativity property of (
4.2.3
), we obtain that the morphism (
4.32
) satisfies the obvious associativity property.

Using the monoidality of the morphism of six functor formalisms
\Pi_{\ast}
, we obtain a morphism:

(4.33)

\displaystyle k^{\ast}\phi_{f\boxplus g}^{mon}\simeq k^{\ast}\Pi_{\ast}Sp_{(\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y})_{0}}\to\Pi_{\ast}\mu_{\ast}(Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}}\boxtimes Sp_{\mathcal{Y}_{0}})\simeq(\Pi_{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}})\boxtimes_{mon}(\Pi_{\ast}Sp_{\mathcal{X}_{0}})\simeq\phi_{f}^{mon}\boxtimes_{mon}\phi_{g}^{mon}

which underlies a morphism of specialization system, and satisfies commutativity and associativity. As this is a morphism of specialization system, it commutes with smooth pullbacks, hence, using the conservativity of smooth pullback and Betti realization, it suffices to prove that it is an isomorphism for schemes in the Betti case. This is then the morphism used by Saito in the preprint
[
Sai10
]
, which is proven there to be an isomorphism. Alternatively, in
[
BBD+15
, Appendix A]
, Shürmann shows that Saito’s morphism is isomorphic to the morphism of
[
Mas01
]
, which is shown to be an isomorphism. Notice that this proof is topological: for a fully algebraic proof, which do not use conservativity of the Betti realization, but use resolution of singularities, see
[
Des24
]
.

The isomorphism of the Theorem is obtained directly by summing (
4.33
) for each couple of critical values of
f,g
, and it inherits its functoriality properties.∎

4.2.4.
Square root of Tate twists and quadratic bundles

The following is well known for mixed Hodge modules (see
[
BBD+15
, Example 2.23]
), we just show that it holds also for perverse Nori motives.

Lemma 4.3
.

Given a choice
\sqrt{-1}
of square root of
-1
in
k
(assumed to be algebraically closed), there is a canonical isomorphism:

(4.34)

\displaystyle\phi_{x^{2}}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\boxtimes_{mon}\phi_{x^{2}}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\simeq\mathbbm{1}[-2](-1)\in\mathcal{A}_{mon}({\rm Spec}(k))[-2]

which gives a canonical square root of the Tate twist:

(4.35)

\displaystyle-\{-1/2\}:=-[-1](-1/2):=-\boxtimes_{mon}0^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{x^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}

Proof.

Consider the closed immersion
i:\{(0,0)\}\to(xy)^{-1}(0)
, the projection
\rho:(xy)^{-1}(0)\to\{(0,0)\}
and the closed immersion
\eta:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}
which acts as
x\mapsto(x,0)
. We can define:

(4.36)

\displaystyle i^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq\rho_{!}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\to\rho_{!}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq\pi_{!}\phi^{mon}_{0}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\simeq\pi_{!}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\simeq\mathbbm{1}[-2](-1)

the first isomorphism holding because
\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\in\mathcal{A}_{mon}((xy)^{-1}(0))
is supported on
(0,0)
, the second morphism is obtained by adjunction, the third isomorphism holds because
\eta
is a closed immersion. From
[
Dav17
, Appendix A]
, the constructible complexes and mixed Hodge module version of this morphism is an isomorphism (it is a simple application of dimensional reduction), and then the monodromic and perverse Nori motive version is an isomorphism too, by conservativity.

We consider the closed immersion
i^{\prime}:\{(0,0)\}\to(x^{2}+y^{2})^{-1}(0)
. Consider a choice of square root
\sqrt{-1}
of
-1
, we can build an automorphism (recall that
k
has characteristic
0\neq 2
):

\displaystyle\Phi:

\displaystyle\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}\to\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}

(4.37)

\displaystyle(x,y)\mapsto(x+\sqrt{-1}y,x-\sqrt{-1}y)

Which gives:

(4.38)

\displaystyle i^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\Phi^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{x^{2}+y^{2}}\Phi^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{x^{2}+y^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}

Using Thom-Sebastiani, we obtain the desired isomorphism:

(4.39)

\displaystyle\phi_{x^{2}}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\boxtimes_{mon}\phi_{x^{2}}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{x^{2}+y^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq i^{\ast}\phi^{mon}_{xy}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}_{k}}\simeq\mathbbm{1}[-2](-1)

Notice that
(-1/2)
preserves the heart
\mathcal{A}_{mon}
from the exactness of
\phi^{mon}_{f}
.
∎

From now on, we fix once and for all a choice
\sqrt{-1}
of square root of
-1
in
k
, fixing a choice of square root of the Tate twist.

Consider a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
on a stack
\mathcal{X}
, by which we means the data of a locally free sheaf
\mathcal{E}
on
\mathcal{X}
, and a non-degenerate quadratic form
q
on
\mathcal{E}^{\vee}
, inducing a regular function
q:\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
. As before, we denote by
\pi:\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathcal{X}
the projection, and by
s:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})
the
0
-section. We consider
P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}
, the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
-principal bundle of orientation of
(\mathcal{E},q)
. By definition, its section over
f:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
is the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
torsor of orientations of
(\mathcal{E},q)
, i.e. , trivializations
\det(f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
which are square root of the trivialization
\det(f^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}))^{2}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}^{\prime}}
induced by
\det(f^{\ast}(q))
.

We will use the following result, which is inspired by
[
BJM19
, Theorem 4.4, Theorem 2.20]
:

Lemma 4.4
.

Given a stack
\mathcal{X}
with a function
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
, there is an isomorphism of specialization system
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{c}\to\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}
over
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{X}\leftarrow\mathcal{X}
:

(4.40)

\displaystyle\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}\simeq s^{\ast}\phi_{f\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}\pi^{\ast}

It is compatible with exterior tensor product and with direct sum of quadratic bundles. A trivialization
(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{n},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2})
, giving a section of
P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}
, identifies this morphism with the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism:

(4.41)

\displaystyle\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}:=\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\boxtimes 0^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k}}\simeq(Id\times 0)^{\ast}\phi_{f\boxplus\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}(pr_{1})^{\ast}

Proof.

We begin by defining a canonical isomorphism (this isomorphism is standard for mixed Hodge modules on schemes):

(4.42)

\displaystyle s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}

such that:

(4.43)

\displaystyle-\otimes_{mon}s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\simeq-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}

underlies an isomorphism of involution of coefficient systems
\mathcal{D}_{mon,c}\to\mathcal{D}_{mon,c}
over
\mathcal{X}
, and is compatible with exterior products of quadratic bundles.

First, notice that
\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}
is supported on the image of
s
, hence
s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\in\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X})
, as
\mathcal{L}_{P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}
, hence it suffices to define smooth locally isomorphisms (
4.42
), and to ensure that they glue on overlaps. Consider a smooth morphism from a separated scheme of finite type
g:X\to\mathcal{X}
and a trivialization
g^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{d},\sum_{i=1}^{d}x_{i}^{2})
. By smooth base change, we obtain an isomorphism:

(4.44)

\displaystyle g^{\ast}s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\simeq\mathbbm{1}_{X}\{-d/2\}

Considering the canonical orientation
\det(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{d})\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}
of
(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{d},\sum_{i=1}^{d}x_{i}^{2})
, this gives a canonical section of
g^{\ast}(P_{(\mathcal{E},q)})
, hence an isomorphism
g^{\ast}\mathcal{L}_{P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}}\simeq\mathbbm{1}_{X}
, which gives:

(4.45)

\displaystyle g^{\ast}s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\simeq\mathcal{L}_{P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}

Given two such choices
g_{i}:X_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
,
i=1,2
, two smooth morphisms
g^{\prime}_{i}:X^{\prime}\to X_{i}
such that
g:=g^{\prime}_{1}\circ g_{1}=g^{\prime}_{2}\circ g_{2}:X^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
, we obtain two trivialization
g^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{X}^{d},\sum_{i=1}^{d}x_{i}^{2})
, and the two isomorphisms:

(4.46)

\displaystyle(g^{\prime}_{i})^{\ast}(g_{i})^{\ast}s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}\simeq(g^{\prime}_{i})^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{i}}\{-d/2\}

that we need to compare are obviously the isomorphisms induced by those orientations. On the perverse sheaf realization, the isomorphism (
4.44
) only depends on the orientation on the Milnor sphere induced by the trivialization, hence the isomorphism (
4.45
) is independent of this choice. By faithfulness of the perverse sheaf realization, those two isomorphisms agree. By descent, one obtains a global isomorphism, that does not depends on any extra choice.

If we consider
f:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
, and consider a smooth covering with trivialization
g:X\to\mathcal{X}
, we can built (
4.42
) for
\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
by using the covering and trivialization
g^{\prime}:X^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}^{\prime}
obtained by base change from
g
. Then (
4.42
) induces by base change an isomorphism of involution of coefficient systems over
\mathcal{X}
. If we consider two stacks
\mathcal{X}_{i}
,
i=1,2
and two quadratic bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
on
\mathcal{X}_{i}
, we can built (
4.42
) for the product
\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}
by using product of smooth covering and trivializations
g_{i}:X_{i}\to\mathcal{X}_{i}
. By Thom-Sebastiani, we obtain that (
4.42
) is compatible with exterior tensor product.

Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\circ\pi+q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\boxplus q}

We have a natural sequence of (iso)morphisms:

\displaystyle\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}

\displaystyle\simeq\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{mon}(s^{\ast}\phi_{q}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E})})

\displaystyle\simeq\Delta^{\ast}(\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\boxtimes_{mon}(s^{\ast}\phi_{q}^{mon,tot}))(pr_{1})^{\ast}

\displaystyle\simeq s^{\ast}(\pi\times Id)^{\ast}(\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\boxtimes_{mon}\phi_{q}^{mon,tot})(pr_{1})^{\ast}

\displaystyle\simeq s^{\ast}(\pi\times Id)^{\ast}\phi_{f\boxplus q}^{mon,tot}(pr_{1})^{\ast}

\displaystyle\to s^{\ast}\phi_{f\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}(\pi\times Id)^{\ast}(pr_{1})^{\ast}

(4.47)

\displaystyle\simeq s^{\ast}\phi_{f\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}\pi^{\ast}

where the first line is (
4.42
), the second line comes from the definition of the internal tensor product, the third from
(Id\times s)\circ\Delta\simeq(p\times Id)\circ s
, the fourth from the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism, the fifth by the functoriality of the specialization system
\phi^{mon,tot}
in the above diagram, and the last one from
pr_{1}\circ(\pi\times Id)\simeq\pi
. As proven above, the first isomorphism underlies an isomorphism of specialization system and is compatible with exterior tensor products, it is also the case for the Thom-Sebastiani of the fourth line from Theorem
4.2
, and for the other lines from the classical functoriality of the four functors, hence it is the case for (
4.2.4
). We mean that, given
g:\mathcal{X}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
, denoting
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime}):=g^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)
and
f^{\prime}:=f\circ g
, the following squares of morphisms are commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\ast}(s^{\ast}\phi_{f\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}\pi^{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{f^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}/2\})g^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((s^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi_{f^{\prime}\circ\pi^{\prime}+q^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}(\pi^{\prime})^{\ast})g^{\ast}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}(\phi_{f^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}/2\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{!}((s^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi_{f^{\prime}\circ\pi^{\prime}+q^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}(\pi^{\prime})^{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\})g_{!}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(s^{\ast}\phi_{f\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}\pi^{\ast})g_{!}}

and similarly for
g^{!},g_{\ast}
, where the direction of the vertical arrows are reversed. The compatibility with exterior tensor products says that, given two quadratic bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
on
\mathcal{X}_{i}
and
f_{i}:\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
for
i=1,2
, considering the quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q):=(\mathcal{E}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{1}\boxplus q_{2})
on
\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2}
, the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\phi_{f_{1}}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1},q_{1})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\})\boxtimes(\phi_{f_{2}}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((s_{1})^{\ast}\phi_{f_{1}\circ\pi_{1}+q_{1}}^{mon,tot}(p_{1})^{\ast})\boxtimes((s_{2})^{\ast}\phi_{f_{2}\circ\pi_{2}+q_{2}}^{mon,tot}(p_{2})^{\ast})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\ast}\phi_{(f_{1}\oplus f_{2})\circ\pi+q}^{mon,tot}p^{\ast}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical arrows are the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphisms.

Given a trivialization
(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{n},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2})
, the above diagram becomes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{X}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times 0}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\boxplus q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f\boxplus 0\boxplus q}

hence the morphism of the Lemma becomes simply the Thom-Sebastiani morphism, hence is an isomorphism. In the general situation, given a smooth cover with trivialization
g:X\to\mathcal{X}
, we obtain from the functoriality with respect to smooth pullbacks that
g^{\ast}
applies to the morphism of the Lemma is a isomorphism, hence by conservativity of smooth pullbacks it is an isomorphism.

Consider two quadratic bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
on the same stack
\mathcal{X}
, and denote
(\mathcal{E},q):=(\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{1}\oplus q_{2})
. A choice of orientation of
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
for
i=1,2
gives a choice of orientation for
(\mathcal{E},q)
, which gives a natural isomorphism:

(4.48)

\displaystyle P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\simeq P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1},q_{1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}

Consider the following square of isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f\circ\pi+q}\pi^{\ast}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((s_{1})^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f\circ\pi_{1}+q_{1}}\pi^{\ast})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{mon,tot}_{f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1},q_{1})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}/2\}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}_{2}}/2\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the left arrow is obtained by considering the quadratic bundle
(\pi_{1})^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})
on
\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}_{1})
. The fact that it commutes can be checked on a smooth cover: using a smooth cover of
\mathcal{X}
such that both
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
have a trivialization, giving a trivialization of
(\mathcal{E},q)
, this follows directly from the associativity of the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism. Then the above square is commutative, meaning that the isomorphism of the Lemma is compatible with direct sum of quadratic bundles.
∎

4.3.
Grothendieck groups

We want to consider consider
K(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{X}))
(resp
K(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X}))
, the Grothendieck group of the triangulated category
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{c}(\mathcal{X})
(resp
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X})
), i.e. the free group generated by isomorphism class of objects, divided by the relation
[B]=[A]+[C]
for each exact triangle
A\to B\to C\to A[1]
. It is identified with the Grotendieck group of the perverse heart
K(\mathcal{A}_{c}(\mathcal{X}))
(resp.
K(\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X}))
), and then, in the presence of a weight structures, because each object in the heart has a weight filtration, it is a direct sum over
w\in\mathbb{Z}
of the Grothendieck group of pure objects of weight
w
. We denote by
\mathbb{L}
(resp.
\mathbb{L}^{1/2}
), called the Tate motive, or motive of the affine line
\mathbb{A}^{1}
, the class of
\mathbbm{1}[-2](-1)
(resp.
\mathbbm{1}[-1](-1/2)
).

There is a slight subtlety in dealing with Grothendieck group of objects over stacks, because (proper) pushforwards along non-representable maps (and in particular, taking the cohomology (with compact support)) does not preserve
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{c}(-)
(resp
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}(-)
). For any triangulated category
\mathcal{D}_{c}
with a Bondarko weight structure, we consider:

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{c,-}:=\lim_{w\to+\infty}\mathcal{D}_{c,\leq w}

(4.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{D}_{c,+}:=\lim_{w\to+\infty}\mathcal{D}_{c,\geq w}

We have then, from the functoriality of the four functors with weights and with objects that
f_{!},f^{\ast}
,
\boxtimes
preserves
\mathcal{D}^{-}_{c,-}
(and similarly
f_{\ast},f^{!}
,
\boxtimes
preserves
\mathcal{D}^{+}_{c,+}
, and
\mathbb{D}
exchanges
\mathcal{D}^{-}_{c,-}
and
\mathcal{D}^{+}_{c,+}
). Recall that for an object
F\in\mathcal{D}_{c,w}
, we have that
{}^{p}\mathcal{H}^{n}(F)
is pure of weight
w+n
. In particular, the weights of the perverse cohomology of an object of
\mathcal{D}^{-}_{c,-}
(resp
\mathcal{D}^{+}_{c,+}
) are bounded from above (resp bounded from below). Moreover, notice that any pure objects
F\in\mathcal{A}_{c}(\mathcal{X})
(resp
F\in\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X})
) of weight
w
can written as
F(\left\lfloor w/2\rfloor\right)(-\left\lfloor w/2\rfloor\right)
where
F(\left\lfloor w/2\rfloor\right)
is pure of weight
0
or
1
(resp as
F(w/2)(-w/2)
, where
F(-w/2)
is pure of weight
0
). This means that the Grothendieck group of
\mathcal{D}^{-}_{c,-}
(resp
\mathcal{D}^{-}_{mon,c,-}
) can be identified with the completed Grothendieck group
K(\mathcal{A}_{c}[[\mathbb{L}^{-1}]])
(resp
K(\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}[[\mathbb{L}^{-1/2}]]))
.

In particular, given a
k
-stack of finite type
p:\mathcal{X}\to Spec(k)
, we have that
H_{c}(\mathcal{X}):=p_{!}p^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{pt}\in\mathcal{D}^{-}_{c,-}
, which gives
[H_{c}(\mathcal{X})]\in K(\mathcal{A}_{c}(pt))[[\mathbb{L}^{-1}]])
. For constructible complexes,
D^{b}_{c}(pt,\mathbb{Q})
is the bounded derived category of complexes of
\mathbb{Q}
-vector space, hence
K(\mathcal{A}_{c}(pt))=\mathbb{Z}
and
\mathbb{L}=1
, and one find the Euler characteristic in the scheme case, but there is a problem in the stack case. This is a well known problem, see for example
[
Joy05
]
. This problem disappear when one uses a refinement, as we obtain then
\mathbb{L}\neq 1
.

For mixed Hodge modules,
MHM(pt)
is the Abelian category of mixed Hodge structure, and its Grothendieck group is isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}[q,y]
, with
q
(resp
y
) the parameter taking into account the weight filtration
W
(resp the Hodge filtration
F
). This isomorphism is given by the Hodge polynomial, which gives, for
M^{\bullet}
a complex of mixed Hodge structure:

(4.50)

\displaystyle[M^{\bullet}]=\sum_{i,j,k}(-1)^{k}q^{i/2}y^{j}\dim({\rm Gr}_{i}^{W}{\rm Gr}^{j}_{F}M^{k})\in\mathbb{Z}[q^{\pm 1/2},y^{\pm 1}]

in particular,
\mathbb{L}
is identified with
qy
. There are two usual one-parameter specializations the Hodge polynomial, the weight polynomial, for
y=1
, related count of points over finite fields via the Weil conjectures, and the
\chi_{y}
genus, for
q=1
, which is topological for smooth and proper varieties from the degeneration of the Hodge to de Rahm spectral sequence. The Euler characteristic (in the scheme case) is then given by
y=q=1
. If the Hodge structures are of Tate type, hence the Hodge polynomial is a polynomial in
\mathbb{L}=qy
(as one obtains often in toric localization contexts), the weight polynomial and
\chi_{y}
genus coincide, but one must take care that they are in general different.

The objects of the Abelian category of monodromic mixed Hodge modules
MMHM
are mixed Hodge structures
M
with a semisimple action
T_{s}
and a nilpotent action
N:M\to M(-1)
. This nilpotent action is not visible in the Grothendieck group, as any object is the extension of objects with
N
acting trivially. One can split
M\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{C}=\bigoplus_{0\leq\lambda<1}M_{\lambda}
, where
T_{s}
acts on
M_{\lambda}
with eigenvalue
e^{2i\pi\lambda}
. Denoting
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}=0
if
\lambda=1
and
\mathcal{E}_{\lambda}=1
if
\lambda\neq 1
, for
M^{\bullet}
a complex of mixed Hodge structure, the monodromic Hodge polynomial is defined by:

(4.51)

\displaystyle[M^{\bullet}]=\sum_{i,j,k,\lambda}(-1)^{k}q^{(i+\epsilon_{\lambda})/2}y^{j-\lambda}\dim({\rm Gr}_{i}^{W}{\rm Gr}^{j}_{F}M^{k}_{\lambda})\in\mathbb{Z}[q^{\pm 1/2},y^{\mathbb{Q}}]

and gives a an isomorphism of ring
K(D^{b}(MMHM(pt))\simeq\mathbb{Z}[q^{\pm 1/2},y^{\mathbb{Q}}]
from
[
Sai10
, Definition 2.18]
. It is obviously compatible with the inclusion of mixed Hodgge structure into monodromic mixed Hodge structure. The specialization
q=1
gives the Hodge spectrum
\chi_{y}^{mon}
.

\mathcal{M}_{perv}(Spec(k))
is the Abelian category of Nori motives, and its Grothendieck group is by definition the group ofNori motives, and similarly the Grothendieck group of monodromic perverse Nori over
{\rm Spec}(k)
is the group of monodromic Nori motives.

Consider
\mathcal{X}
an algebraic stack,
j:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{X}
the inclusion of a Zariski-open subscheme, and
i:\mathcal{Z}\to X
the inclusion of its closed complements. There is a natural exact triangle in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
:

(4.52)

\displaystyle j_{!}j^{\ast}\to Id\to i_{!}i^{\ast}\to j_{!}j^{\ast}[1]

Then, given
p:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{S}
, we have an equality in the Grothendieck group:

(4.53)

\displaystyle[p_{!}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{X}})]=[(p\circ j)_{!}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}]+[(p\circ i)_{!}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{Z}}]\in K(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{-}(\mathcal{S}))

using also homotopy invariance, we see that there is a natural map from the Grothendieck group of (monodromic) varieties over
\mathcal{S}
(with cut and paste relation) to
K(\mathcal{A}_{c}(\mathcal{S})[[\mathbb{L}^{-1}]])
(resp over
K(\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{S})[[\mathbb{L}^{-1/2}]])
).

For
X
a separated scheme of finite type, there is from the work of Denef-Loeser
[
DL98
]
and Looijenga
[
Loo00
]
a Grothendieck group
\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}
of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant varieties on
X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}
, and, for
f:X\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, nearby and vanishing cycles
\Psi_{f},\Phi_{f}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X_{0}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}
. From
[
IS21
, Proposition 3.5.1]
, there are morphisms
\chi_{X,c}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}:\mathcal{M}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}_{X\times\mathbb{G}_{m}}\to K(\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}(X))
. From
[
IS21
, Theorem 5.2.1]
, one has
\chi_{X,c}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\Psi_{f})=[\psi^{mon}_{f}\mathbbm{1}_{X_{\eta}}]
, and then directly
\chi_{X,c}^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}(\Phi_{f})=[\phi^{mon}_{f}\mathbbm{1}_{X}]
, where one uses Ayoub definition of nearby cycles. This result was well known for the case of
D^{b}_{c}(-,\mathbb{Q})
and
D^{b}MHM(-)
before
[
IS21
]
, but this was new for perverse Nori motives. The same follow for stacks by descent: in particular, the class in the Grothendieck group of Nori motives of the cohomology of the DT sheaf that wee will consider below will correspond to the Nori realization of the motivic DT invariants defined in
[
BJM19
]
, as was known in the Hodge case and pointed in
[
Dav19
, Appendix A]
.

Using the Mayer-Vietoris exact triangle, given
\mathcal{X}
, a constructible subset
S
of
\mathcal{X}(k)
, and an element
F\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{X})
, we can define uniquely its motive on
S
, that we denote by a slight abuse of notation
[\mathbb{H}_{c}(S,F|_{S})]
, and is additive under disjoint union (notice that, in general,
S
is not a stack, hence
F|_{S}
do not make sense, which explains why the notation is abusive). Indeed, writing
S
as a disjoint union of locally closed substacks
S_{i}\to\mathcal{X}
, it suffices to define:

(4.54)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(S,F|_{S})]:=\sum_{i}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(S_{i},F|_{S_{i}})]

and this does not depends on the choice of the
S_{i}
by Mayer-Vietoris. We have the useful ”motivic decomposition formula”:

Lemma 4.5
.

Let
k
be an algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
, and
\eta:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
be a geometric injection (resp. bijection) of algebraic
k
-stacks of finite type (i.e. , such that
\eta(k):\mathcal{X}(k)\to\mathcal{Y}(k)
is a injection (resp. bijection) of groupoids). Then, for any
F\in\mathcal{D}_{(mon)}(\mathcal{Y})
:

(4.55)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Y},F)]=[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\eta^{\ast}F)]+[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Y}-\eta(\mathcal{X}),F|_{\mathcal{Y}-\eta(\mathcal{X})})]

(resp. :

(4.56)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Y},F)]=[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{X},\eta^{\ast}F)]

)

Proof.

We begin by the case when
\eta
is a geometric bijection. Consider an open immersion
u:\mathcal{U}\to\mathcal{Y}
, its closed complement
z:\mathcal{Z}\to\mathcal{Y}
, and the Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{Z}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces z}

Applying
\eta_{!}
to the right and
\eta^{\ast}
to the left to the localization exact triangle of
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{X}\leftarrow\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}
, we obtain:

(4.57)

\displaystyle\eta_{!}(u^{\prime})_{!}(u^{\prime})^{\ast}\eta^{\ast}\to\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}\to\eta_{!}(z^{\prime})_{!}(z^{\prime})^{\ast}\eta^{\ast}

which gives, using base change:

(4.58)

\displaystyle u_{!}(\eta_{U})_{!}(\eta_{U})^{\ast}u^{\ast}\to\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}\to z_{!}(\eta_{Z})_{!}(\eta_{Z})^{\ast}z^{\ast}

and then passing to the Grothendieck group:

(4.59)

\displaystyle[\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}]=[u_{!}(\eta_{U})_{!}(\eta_{U})^{\ast}u^{\ast}]+[z_{!}(\eta_{Z})_{!}(\eta_{Z})^{\ast}z^{\ast}]

notice that, if
\eta
is a geometric bijection, then
\eta_{U},\eta_{Z}
are still geometric bijections. If
[(\eta_{U})_{!}(\eta_{U})^{\ast}]=Id
,
[(\eta_{Z})_{!}(\eta_{Z})^{\ast}]=Id
, then, using
[Id]=[u_{!}u^{\ast}]+[z_{!}z^{\ast}]
from the localization exact triangle, one obtains
[\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}]=Id
.

The main tool used here is
[
Bri12
, Lemma 3.2]
(notice that the arguments used here are entirely algebraic, and does not use
k=\mathbb{C}
, they works for any algebraically closed field of characteristic
0
). It gives that, if
\eta
is a geometric bijection, there is a collection
\mathcal{X}_{i}
(resp
\mathcal{Y}_{i}
) of locally closed substacks of
\mathcal{X}
(resp
\mathcal{Y}
) such that
\eta
induces isomorphisms
\eta_{i}:\mathcal{X}_{i}\to\mathcal{Y}_{i}
. Notice that the statement that
[\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}]=Id
is Zariski-local on
\mathcal{Y}
, hence we can suppose that
\mathcal{Y}
is of finite type, which implies that the collection is finite. We show that
[\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}]=Id
by recursion on the number
n
of strata, the case
n=1
(
\eta
is an isomorphism) being trivial. Take
\mathcal{Y}_{1}=\mathcal{U}_{1}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{1}
. From the above reduction, it suffices to show
[(\eta^{\prime})_{!}(\eta^{\prime})^{\ast}]=Id
for
\eta^{\prime}
the geometric bijections obtained by restricting to
\mathcal{U}_{1}\cap\mathcal{Z}_{1}
,
(\mathcal{Y}-\mathcal{U}_{1})\cap\mathcal{Z}_{1}
,
\mathcal{U}_{1}\cap(\mathcal{Y}-\mathcal{Z}_{1})
and
(\mathcal{Y}-\mathcal{U}_{1})\cap(\mathcal{Y}-\mathcal{Z}_{1})
. But all of those four stacks have
n-1
strata, hence we obtain
[\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}]=Id
by recursion, which gives in particular (
4.56
).

If
\eta
is a geometric injection, we can write the subset
\mathcal{Y}(k)-\eta(\mathcal{X}(k))
, which is constructible by Chevalley’s theorem, as a disjoint union of locally closed immersions
\eta_{i}
of substacks of
\mathcal{Y}
. We can then form a geometric bijection
\eta^{\prime}
from the disjoint union of
\eta
and the
\eta_{i}
, and, applying the above result for
\eta^{\prime}
, we obtain the claimed result for
\eta
.
∎

5.
Recollection on DT theory on stacks

In this section, as in the preceding section, we work over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
0
. All our stacks are assumed to be quasi-separated Artin
1
-stacks locally of finite type over
k
, with affine stabilizers. We will recall the theory of d-critical stacks, as introduced in
[
Joy13
]
, and the gluing of the sheaf of vanishing cycles of d-critical stacks, as done in
[
BBD+15
]
(scheme case) and
[
BBBBJ15
]
(stack case). In those articles, the theory is developed in two steps: first on schemes, by considering critical charts given by schemes, open/étale restrictions of them, and stabilization by adding a trivial quadratic factor, and then extending the construction to stacks by smooth descent. To glue our hyperbolic localization formula, we will have to work with more general critical charts, given by stacks, so we express the results of
[
Joy13
]
,
[
BBD+15
]
and
[
BBBBJ15
]
in this more general setting, using smooth descent.

5.1.
D-critical stacks and stacky critical charts

In
[
Joy13
, Section 2.8]
, Joyce introduce the concept of d-critical stack, which is the main tool to glue vanishing cycles in cohomological DT theory. We will adapt slightly the theory developed in
[
Joy13
, Section 2.8]
, in order to consider critical charts defined by functions on smooth stacks instead of smooth schemes. This extension is mostly straightforward, because the whole theory of
[
Joy13
]
behaves well with respect to smooth maps, hence we will obtains our results directly by smooth descent from the results in the scheme case.

For any Artin stack
\mathcal{X}
over
k
, Joyce defines in
[
Joy13
, Corollary 2.52]
a lisse-étale sheaf
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
of
k
-algebras on
\mathcal{X}
. We give the main characterization of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
that we will use:

Lemma 5.1
.

i)

For any smooth map
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
from a
k
-stack, and any closed embedding
i:\mathcal{R}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}
into a smooth
k
-stack, denote by
I_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}
the sheaf of ideals in
i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})
of functions on
\mathcal{U}
near
i(\mathcal{R})
which vanishes on
i(\mathcal{R})
. There is a natural exact sequence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathcal{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})}{I^{2}_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{i^{-1}(T^{\ast}_{\mathcal{U}})}{I_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}\cdot i^{-1}(T^{\ast}_{\mathcal{U}})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

We denote sections of
\mathcal{S}_{X}|_{\mathcal{R}}
by
f+I^{2}_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}
, with
df|_{\mathcal{R}}=0
.

ii)

The construction of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
is functorial, namely, for
\phi:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
, there is a naturally defined morphism of sheaves of
k
-algebras
\phi^{\star}:\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
, compatible with compositions, such that
Id^{\star}=Id
. Given a commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

where the left horizontal arrows are smooth and the right horizontal arrows are closed immersions. The natural diagram with exact lines:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{Y}}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{-1}(\frac{(i^{\prime})^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}})}{I^{2}_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{-1}(\frac{i^{-1}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U}^{\prime})}{I_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}\cdot(i^{\prime})^{-1}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U}^{\prime})})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{\mathcal{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})}{I^{2}_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{i^{-1}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U})}{I_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}\cdot i^{-1}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi^{\star}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}}

is commutative. In particular, taking
\phi=Id
(hence
\phi^{\star}=Id
) but
\tilde{\phi}
nontrivial, one obtains a stacky analogue of
[
Joy13
, Theorem 2.1 ii)]
expressing how
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
is glued.

Proof.

In
[
Joy13
, Theorem 2.1, Proposition 2.3]
, these are the defining property of
\mathcal{S}_{X}
and
\phi^{\star}
, for
X
a scheme and
\phi:X\to Y
a morphism of schemes, where one restricts to open immersions
R\to X
, and closed immersions of schemes
i:R\to U
. In
[
Joy13
, Corollary 2.52]
, for
\mathcal{X}
a stack,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
is defined by smooth descent from the scheme case. Namely, by definition, for any smooth presentation
X\to\mathcal{X}
by a scheme, Joyce defines
(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{X}:=\mathcal{S}_{X}
, and for
\phi:X\to X^{\prime}
a morphism of smooth presentation by schemes, Joyce use the gluing morphism:

(5.1)

\displaystyle\phi^{-1}((\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{X^{\prime}}):=\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{X^{\prime}})\overset{\phi^{\star}}{\to}\mathcal{S}_{X}=:(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{X}

The result of the lemma follows then formally by smooth descent.

Consider a smooth map
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
from a
k
-stack, and a closed embedding
i:\mathcal{R}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{U}
into a smooth
k
-stack. Consider a smooth covering
U\to\mathcal{U}
by a scheme, and consider the closed immersion
\tilde{i}:R\to U
obtained by base change. From the definition
[
Joy13
, Corollary 2.52]
,
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{R}
fits into an exact sequence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\tilde{i}^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{U})}{I^{2}_{R,U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\frac{\tilde{i}^{-1}(T^{\ast}U_{k})}{I_{R,U}\cdot\tilde{i}^{-1}(T^{\ast}U)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{R,U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}

Consider now a smooth covering
U^{\prime}\to U\times_{\mathcal{U}}U
by a (smooth) scheme, the induced closed immersion
i^{\prime}:R^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
of schemes, and the smooth morphism covering
R^{\prime}\to R\times_{\mathcal{R}}R
. Still from the definition, the exact sequence associated to
R\times_{\mathcal{R}}R\to\mathcal{X}
is isomorphic to the pullbacks of the first exact sequence along the two projections
p_{i}:R\times_{\mathcal{R}}R\to R
, hence, by smooth descent, one obtains an exact sequence as claimed. For two smooth cover
U,U^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
, using a smooth cover
U^{\prime\prime}\to U\times_{\mathcal{U}}U^{\prime}
and a similar argument, one obtains that this does not depends on the choice of the smooth presentation.

We build
\phi^{\star}
by smooth descent: given smooth presentations
X\to\mathcal{X}
,
Y\to\mathcal{Y}
by schemes, with a morphism
\tilde{\phi}:X\to Y
over
\phi
, we define:

(5.2)

\displaystyle\phi^{\ast}|_{X}:\tilde{\phi}^{-1}((\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{Y}})|_{Y}):=\tilde{\phi}^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{Y})\overset{\tilde{\phi}^{\star}}{\to}\mathcal{S}_{X}=:(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{X}

from the compatibility of
\phi^{\star}
with composition in the scheme case (
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.3]
), one obtains that these are compatible with the gluing morphisms (
5.1
) hence defines a morphism
\phi^{\star}:\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}
. The corresponding result in the scheme case implies the compatibility with compositions and the fact that
Id^{\star}=Id
.

Given a diagram as in the proposition, we consider a smooth morphism of schemes
\tilde{U}\to\tilde{U}^{\prime}
inducing smooth coverings of
\mathcal{U}
and
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
. We define smooth covering
\tilde{R}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
,
\tilde{R}\to\mathcal{R}
by base change. it suffices to check the commutativity of the diagram of the Lemma after a pullback to
\tilde{R}
, but, using the definitions, it is the commutative diagram of introduced in
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.3]
for the schemes
\tilde{R},\tilde{U},\tilde{R}^{\prime},\tilde{U}^{\prime}
.
∎

In particular, there is a natural map
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
, sending
f
to
f|_{\mathcal{R}}
for each
f\in\frac{i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})}{I^{2}_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}}
. Notice that each function
f\in i^{-1}(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}})
satisfying
df|_{\mathcal{R}}=0
is locally constant on
\mathcal{R}^{red}
(but not necessarily on
\mathcal{R}
). Hence, defining
\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\mathcal{X}}
to be the kernel of
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}\to\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}
, there is a natural splitting
\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}=\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\mathcal{X}}\oplus k_{\mathcal{X}}
, see
[
Joy13
, Theo 2.1 a), Cor 2.52 b)]
. As in the scheme case (
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.3]
), one obtains directly that
\phi^{\star}
maps
\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{Y}}^{0})\to\mathcal{S}_{\mathcal{X}}^{0}
.

We reformulate then Joyce’s definition, which is obtained by combining
[
Joy13
, Def 2.5, Def 2.53]
, (notice that we allows us to consider stacky critical charts, when Joyce consider only schematic ones, but our definition of d-critical stack is the same):

Definition 5.2
.

i)

For
\mathcal{X}
a stack and a global section
s\in H^{0}(\mathcal{S}^{0})
, a (stacky) critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
is the data of a smooth map
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
, a closed immersion
i:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{U}
into a smooth stack
\mathcal{U}
, and a function
f:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
such that
f+I^{2}_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U}}=s|_{\mathcal{R}}
and
{\rm Crit}(f)=\mathcal{R}
(notice that one has a priori by definition of
s
that
\mathcal{R}\subset{\rm Crit}(f)
).

ii)

Given a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and a smooth map
\phi:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
, one obtains by base change a closed immersion
i^{\prime}:\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
into a smooth stack
\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
, a smooth map
\mathcal{R}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
, and a function
f^{\prime}:=f\circ\Phi:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, giving a critical chart
(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
, which is called a smooth restriction of the critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
.

iii)

s
is said to be a
d
-critical structure if there is a collection of (stacky) critical charts
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
such that the smooth maps
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
are jointly surjective (one says in this case that these (stacky) critical charts cover
\mathcal{X}
).

Lemma 5.3
.

The definition of a d-critical structure on a stack given above coincide with Joyce’s definition
[
Joy13
, Definition 2.53]
.

Proof.

In
[
Joy13
, Definition 2.53]
, a global section
s
of
\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\mathcal{X}}
on a
k
-Artin stack
\mathcal{X}
is said to be d-critical structure if, for each smooth map
X\to\mathcal{X}
from a
k
-scheme (or equivalently, for a single smooth cover, using
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.8]
), there is near each each point
x\in X(k)
a Zariski-open subset
R
containing
x
and a critical chart
(R,U,f,i)
. Notice that it implies in particular that
\mathcal{X}
is covered by d-critical charts in our sense. Now, consider a covering of
\mathcal{X}
by d-critical charts
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
: we consider a cover
U^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
by a scheme, and consider the smooth restriction
(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
, such that the
\phi:R^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
cover
\mathcal{X}
. Then
R^{\prime}
is a global critical locus,
\phi^{\star}(s)
is a d-critical structure on the scheme
R^{\prime}
in the sense of
[
Joy13
, Definition 2.5]
, hence
s
is a d-critical structure on
s
in the sense of
[
Joy13
, Definition 2.53]
.
∎

Moreover, the structure of
d
-critical stack is extremely powerful, and gives a way to build a lot of critical charts, thanks to this proposition:

Proposition 5.4
.

(Joyce,
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.7, Proposition 2.8]
)

•

Consider a d-critical scheme
(R,s)
with a closed immersion
i:R\to U
into a smooth
k
-scheme
U
, a point
x\in R(k)
with
\dim T_{x}R=\dim(U)
, and a function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
such that
s=f+I_{R,U}^{2}
. There is a Zariski-open subset
R^{\prime}
containing
x
, a Zariski open subset
U^{\prime}
of
U
containing
R^{\prime}
, such that denoting
i^{\prime}:R^{\prime}\to U^{\prime}
the closed embedding and
f^{\prime}=f|_{R^{\prime}}
,
(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
is a critical chart.

•

Given a smooth morphism
\phi:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
, if
(\mathcal{Y},s)
is a d-critical stack, then
(\mathcal{X},\phi^{\star}(s))
is a d-critical stack (the converse being true if
\phi
is moreover surjective). We call
\phi:(\mathcal{X},\phi^{\star}(s))\to(\mathcal{Y},s)
a smooth morphism of d-critical stacks, and we obtain a 2-category of d-critical stacks, with smooth morphisms.

Proof.

The first part is
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.7]
. In
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.8]
, the second result is proven for schemes, but, given two smooth (resp. and surjective) presentation
X\to\mathcal{X}
,
Y\to\mathcal{Y}
and a smooth map
\tilde{\phi}:X\to Y
over
\phi
, one has that
(X,\tilde{\phi}^{\star}(s|_{Y})=\phi^{\star}(s)|_{X})
is a d-critical scheme if (resp. if and only if)
(Y,s|_{Y})
is a d-critical scheme. But, from the definition,
(\mathcal{X},\phi^{\star}(s))
(resp
(\mathcal{Y},s)
) is a d-critical stack if and only if
(X,\phi^{\star}(s)|_{X})
(resp.
(Y,s|_{Y})
) is a d-critical scheme, which gives the claim.
∎

5.2.
Canonical bundle on d-critical stack

Consider a smooth stack
\mathcal{U}
, a function
f:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
with critical locus
i:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{U}
, and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
over
\mathcal{U}
. The critical locus of
f\circ p+q:\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
is simply given by the
0
section of
\mathcal{R}
. Consider now a smooth morphism
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
. We have obviously:

(5.3)

\displaystyle f\circ p+q\in f\circ p+(\mathcal{I}_{\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})})^{2}

hence from Lemma
5.1

ii)
applied to
p
, we have that, if
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
, then
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q,s\circ i)
is also a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
. We will call such operations stabilization by quadratic bundle.

For
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
a smooth morphism of stacks, we consider the canonical line bundle
K_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}}:=\det(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}})
. In
[
Joy13
, Theo 2.28, Theorem 2.56]
, for
(\mathcal{X},s)
a d-critical stack, Joyce builds also a line bundle
K_{\mathcal{X},s}
on
\mathcal{X}^{red}
, the orientation bundle, and defines an orientation data to be a choice of a square root
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}
(with a choice of isomorphism
(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})^{\otimes 2}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X},s}
). We will use the following characterization:

Lemma 5.5
.

The line bundle
K_{\mathcal{X},s}
on
\mathcal{X}^{red}
build in
[
Joy13
, Theo 2.28, Theorem 2.56]
is uniquely characterized by the following properties:

i)

For each critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, there is a natural isomorphism:

(5.4)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\otimes 2})|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}

Given an orientation data
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}
, the
\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}
bundle
Q_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i}
over
\mathcal{R}^{red}
is defined to be the bundle of local isomorphisms:

(5.5)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}})|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-1}_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}

which are square roots of the above isomorphism.

ii)

Given a smooth restriction of critical charts
(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, the isomorphism (
5.4
) for
(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f,i)
is obtained by smooth restriction from the isomorphism for
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
. In the presence of an orientation data
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}
, this gives a canonical isomorphism:

(5.6)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime}}|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\simeq Q_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i}

iii)

Given a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
on
\mathcal{U}
, the following isomorphism:

(5.7)

\displaystyle i^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\otimes 2})|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\simeq(s\circ i)^{\ast}(K_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}^{\otimes 2})|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}

is the isomorphism induced by
\det(q):\det(\mathcal{E})^{2}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}
. In the presence of an orientation data
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}
, this gives a canonical isomorphism:

(5.8)

\displaystyle Q_{\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q,s\circ i}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\simeq Q_{\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i}

Proof.

Notice that
[
Joy13
, Theo 2.28, Theorem 2.56]
characterize uniquely
K_{\mathcal{X},s}
in terms of the above properties for schematic critical charts, hence those properties characterize
K_{\mathcal{X},s}
too.

i)

Consider a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, and take a smooth presentation
\tilde{U}\to\mathcal{U}
by a smooth scheme, and consider the smooth restriction of critical charts
(\tilde{R},\tilde{U},\tilde{f},\tilde{i})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
. From
[
Joy13
, Theorem 2.56 b), Theorem 2.28 i)]
, there are natural isomorphisms:

(5.9)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{\tilde{R}^{red}}\simeq K_{\tilde{R},s|_{\tilde{R}}}\otimes(K_{\tilde{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{\tilde{R}^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{\tilde{U}}^{\otimes 2})|_{\tilde{R}^{red}}\otimes(K_{\tilde{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{R^{red}}

Consider a smooth covering of the diagonal
\tilde{U}^{\prime}\to\tilde{U}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{U}
: from
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.30]
, the pullbacks of this isomorphisms along the two projections
\tilde{R}^{\prime}\to\tilde{R}
agree with the similar isomorphism for
\tilde{R}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{X}
, hence by smooth descent one obtains an isomorphism (
5.4
). Considering two covering
\tilde{U},\tilde{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
, using the same argument for a smooth covering
\tilde{U}^{\prime\prime}\to\tilde{U}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{U}^{\prime}
, one obtains that this isomorphism does not depends on the choice of the smooth presentation.

ii)

We wean that the isomorphism:

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\otimes 2})|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})|^{\otimes-2}_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/\mathcal{U}}^{\otimes-2})|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\otimes 2})|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}/\mathcal{R}})|^{\otimes-2}_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}|^{\otimes-2}_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

(5.10)

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\otimes 2})|_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}}|^{\otimes-2}_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

(where the second line is the isomorphism of
i)
for
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
) is the isomorphism of
i)
for
(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
. Consider a smooth cover
\tilde{U}\to\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
, which gives by composition a smooth morphism
\tilde{U}\to\mathcal{U}
: we can build the isomorphism of
i)
for
(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
and
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
using those presentations, hence the result follows directly.

iii)

Consider a smooth cover by schemes
g_{i}:U_{i}\to\mathcal{U}
such that
(g_{i})^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)
is isomorphic to trivial quadratic forms (this can be done étale locally on scheme, and then smooth locally on stacks). The claimed result is obtained for
(g_{i})^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)
by
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.25 b), Theorem 2.28
ii)
]
. Building the isomorphism of
i)
with the smooth covers
g_{i}
and
g^{\prime}_{i}:\mathbb{V}_{U_{i}}((g_{i})^{\ast}(\mathcal{E}))\to\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})
, we obtain the claimed result.

∎

The above characterization gives directly that, for
f:(\mathcal{X},f^{\star}(s))\to(\mathcal{Y},s)
a smooth morphism of
d
-critical stacks, one obtains a functorial isomorphism
K_{\mathcal{X},f^{\star}(s)}\simeq K_{\mathcal{Y},s}|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}}|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}^{\otimes 2}
. We obtain then a
2
-category of oriented d-critical stacks, whose objects are oriented d-critical stacks, 1-morphisms are smooth morphisms of d-critical stacks
\phi:(\mathcal{X},\phi^{\star}(s))\to(\mathcal{Y},s)
with the data of an isomorphism of square roots:

(5.11)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},f^{\star}(s)}^{1/2}\simeq K_{\mathcal{Y},s}^{red}|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}}|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}

and 2-morphisms are 2-morphisms of the category of stacks, such that the two isomorphisms of square roots agree.

Lemma 5.6
.

There is a symmetric monoidal product on the
2
-category of d-critical stacks with smooth morphisms, given by
(\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1})\times(\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}):=(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
, where
s\boxplus t:=(p_{1})^{\star}s+(p_{2})^{\star}t
. If
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
are critical charts for
\mathcal{X}_{i}
,
(\mathcal{R}_{1}\times\mathcal{R}_{2},\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2},i_{1}\times i_{2})
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
. Moreover, there is a symmetric monoidal isomorphism
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}\boxtimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}
, compatible with the isomorphisms (
5.4
). It gives a symmetric monoidal structure to the 2-category of oriented d-critical stacks with smooth morphisms. In particular, given oriented d-critical stacks
(\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i},K_{\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i}}^{1/2})
, taking the product
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2},K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}^{1/2}\boxtimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}^{1/2})
, there is a symmetric monoidal isomorphism:

(5.12)

\displaystyle Q_{(\mathcal{R}_{1}\times\mathcal{R}_{2},\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2},i_{1}\times i_{2})}\simeq Q_{\mathcal{R}_{1},\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1},i_{1}}\boxplus Q_{\mathcal{R}_{2},\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2},i_{2}}

Proof.

In the scheme case, look at
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.11]
for a similar statement. Notice that, from the cofiber sequence
(f_{1}\times f_{2})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mu}\to\mathbb{L}_{f_{1}}\boxtimes\mathbb{L}_{f_{2}}\to\mathbb{L}_{f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}
, we have
{\rm Crit}(f_{1}\boxplus f_{2})={\rm Crit}(f_{1})\times{\rm Crit}(f_{2})
. Hence, if
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
are critical charts for
(\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i})
,
(\mathcal{R}_{1}\times\mathcal{R}_{2},\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2},i_{1}\times i_{2})
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
. This implies that
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
is a d-critical stack if
(\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i})
are. The product is obviously monoidal.

By definition, a
d
-critical stack is covered by schematic critical charts. From the results of
[
Joy13
, Section 2.3]
, two schematic critical charts can be related by étale restrictions and stabilization by quadratic form. It means that
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
can be covered by products of critical charts on the
(\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i})
, and two such product can be related by products of smooth restrictions and stabilization by quadratic forms. It means the conditions of Lemma
5.5
for
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2})
restricted to charts and stabilization by quadratic form being products of charts and stabilization by quadratic form for
K_{\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i}}
determines uniquely
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}\boxtimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}
. But the conditions of Lemma
5.5
are clearly monoidal with respect to product of critical charts, hence this defines canonically a monoidal isomorphism
K_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\boxplus s_{2}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}\boxtimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}
.
∎

5.3.
Shifted symplectic stacks and Darboux theorem

The main motivation for the introduction of
d
-critical stacks is that, from the ”Darboux theorem” of
[
BBJ19
]
,
[
BBBBJ15
]
, they forms a classical truncation of
-1
shifted symplectic stacks. We will recall this now.

In
[
PTVV13
, Definition 1.12]
, the authors define for each derived Artin
k
-stack
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, and integers
p,n
, the space
\mathcal{A}^{p}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},n)
of
p
-forms of degree
n
on
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, and the space
\mathcal{A}^{p,cl}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},n)
of closed
p
-forms of degree
n
on
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, and a natural map
\mathcal{A}^{p,cl}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},n)\to\mathcal{A}^{p}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},n)
sending a closed form to the underlying form. In particular, in derived geometry, the fact of being closed is not a property, but an extra structure. By
[
PTVV13
, Proposition 1.14]
, there is a natural equivalence:

(5.13)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{p}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},n)\simeq{\rm Map}_{L_{qcoh}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}(\mathcal{O}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}},\wedge^{p}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}[n])

In particular, a
2
-form
\omega
of degree
n
induces a map
\mathcal{O}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}\to\wedge^{2}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}[n]
, i.e. a morphism:

(5.14)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}[n]

and it is said to be nondegenerate if this is an isomorphism. By
[
PTVV13
, Definition 1.18]
, a
n
-shifted symplectic structure
\omega
is a closed
2
-form of degree
n
whose underlying
2
-form of degree
n
is nondegenerate. In particular, a
-1
-shifted symplectic structure on a derived scheme induces a symmetric perfect obstruction theory on the classical truncation.

Consider a function
f:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
on a smooth stack
\mathcal{U}
: one obtains from
[
PTVV13
, Corollary 2.11]
a canonical
-1
-shifted symplectic structure
\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}
on
\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f):=\mathcal{U}\overset{h}{\times}_{0,T^{\ast}\mathcal{U},df}\mathcal{U}
, obtained by considering it as an intersection of
0
-Lagrangians in the
0
-symplectic stack
T^{\ast}\mathcal{U}
, and applying
[
PTVV13
, Corollary 2.10]
.

In
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18]
(based on
[
BBJ19
, Theorem 6.6]
), the authors build, for any derived Artin stack
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
with a
-1
-shifted symplectic structure
\omega
, a natural
d
-critical structure
s\in H^{0}(\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\mathcal{X}})
on its classical truncation
\mathcal{X}
. From
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 2.10]
,
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
is covered by smooth maps
\underline{\phi}:\underline{R}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, such that there is a smooth scheme
U
, a function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and a map
\underline{j}:\underline{R}\to\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}
inducing an isomorphism of classical schemes such that
\underline{j}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}})\sim\underline{\phi}^{\ast}(\omega)
. According to
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18 a)]
, denoting by
\phi:R\to\mathcal{X}
the classical truncation of
\underline{\phi}:\underline{R}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
,
s
is uniquely defined by the condition that
s|_{R}=f+I_{R,U}^{2}
for any such data, i.e.
(R,U,f,i)
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
. According to
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18 b)]
,
K_{\mathcal{X},s}
is naturally isomorphic to
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}
.

The structure of d-critical stack is sufficient to define the DT sheaf, hence we will mainly work at this level. However, for stacks appearing in moduli problems, in general it is more natural to give them a
-1
-shifted symplectic structure using general results of
[
PTVV13
]
,
[
BD18
]
(look for example at Section
7.3
), and then applies the Darboux theorem to obtain a d-critical structure, then we will have to work a bit at the
-1
-shifted symplectic level to obtain some compatibility results.

5.4.
The DT sheaf on critical charts

5.4.1.
Definition

Consider a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, i.e. a smooth stack
\mathcal{U}
with a function
f:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, and the closed immersion of the critical locus
i:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{U}
. Consider
\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{U})[-2d_{\mathcal{U}}]
: one can check using a smooth presentation and the Betti representation that it is supported on
\mathcal{R}
, hence its restriction to
\mathcal{R}
is still in the shift of the heart of the perverse
t
-structure, we define then:

(5.15)

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{U},f}:=i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}\in\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{R})

Remark 5.7
.

We see from the definition that
P_{\mathcal{U},f}
is obtained by applying successively three functors to
\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm Spec}(k)}
: firstly, the functor
(\mathcal{U}\to{\rm Spec}(k))^{\ast}
, secondly
\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}
, and lastly
i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}
: let’s denote these temporarily by
a_{3},a_{2},a_{1}
for simplicity. We will build various compatibility isomorphisms, and check compatibility between these isomorphisms (namely, compatibility with respect to smooth pullbacks, products, stabilization and hyperbolic localization). We denote by
\alpha_{b,c}:bc\simeq cb
an isomorphism of commutation between the functors
b
and
c
.
Each of these isomorphism will be expressed as the commutation between a functor
f
and
a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}
obtained by a sequence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ba_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}ba_{2}a_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}ba_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}b}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{b,a_{1}}a_{2}a_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}\alpha_{b,a_{2}}a_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}\alpha_{b,a_{3}}}

We will show compatibility, namely that for
b,c
two functors, the following square is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces bca_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ba_{1}a_{2}a_{3}c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces cba_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}bc}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ca_{1}a_{2}a_{3}b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}cb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{c,b}a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{1}a_{2}a_{3}\alpha_{c,b}}

To prove that, it will suffice to prove that for each
1\leq i\leq 3
, the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces bca_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ba_{i}c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces cba_{i}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{i}bc}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces ca_{i}b}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{i}cb}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b\alpha_{c,a_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{b,a_{i}}c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{c,b}a_{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{c,a_{i}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{i}\alpha_{c,b}}

We say that the isomorphism of commutation between
a_{i}
and
b
and between
a_{i}
and
c
are compatible. In general, it will follows from the functoriality of the six functor formalism, or from functoriality results established in previous sections.

5.4.2.
Smooth restrictions of critical loci

Recall that smooth restriction of stacky critical loci
\phi:(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{U},f)
is the data the data of a smooth morphism
\phi:\mathcal{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
such that
f^{\prime}=f\circ\phi
. Because
\phi
is smooth, we have a Cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

We have then a natural sequence of isomorphisms:

\displaystyle\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U},f}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/2\}\phi^{\ast}\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/2\}\phi_{f^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}\phi^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i^{\prime})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}/2\}\phi_{f^{\prime}}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}

(5.16)

\displaystyle=:P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}

where the first and third isomorphism are obtained by functoriality of pullbacks, and the second one by functoriality of the specialization system
\phi^{mon,tot}
with respect to smooth pullbacks. By functoriality of the pullback, and by compatibility of specialization system with respect to composition of smooth pullbacks, this isomorphism is compatible with composition of smooth restriction of equivariant critical loci. It means that for
\phi^{\prime}:(\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime},f^{\prime\prime})\to(\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime})
a smooth restriction of critical loci, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi^{\prime}}/2\}\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U},f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\phi}^{\prime})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi^{\prime}}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\phi\circ\phi^{\prime}})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi\circ\phi^{\prime}}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U},f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime\prime},f^{\prime\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

5.4.3.
Product of critical loci

Given two critical loci
(\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1})
and
(\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2})
, one consider their product
(\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2})
. One has then a natural isomorphism:

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}:=
