\displaystyle(i_{1}\times i_{2})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}}/2\}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(i_{1}\times i_{2})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}}/2\}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}(\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}\boxtimes\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{2}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(i_{1}\times i_{2})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}}/2\}(\phi^{mon,tot}_{f_{1}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}})\boxtimes(\phi^{mon,tot}_{f_{1}}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{2}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle((i_{1})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}/2\}\phi_{f_{1}}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}})\boxtimes((i_{2})^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}_{2}}/2\}\phi_{f_{2}}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}_{2}})

(5.17)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}}\boxtimes P_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}}

where the first and third isomorphisms follows from functoriality of pullbacks with respect to exterior products, and the second isomorphism is the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism of Theorem
4.2
. From the functoriality of pullbacks with respect to exterior products, and the functoriality of the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism of Theorem
4.2
with respect to smooth pullbacks (coming from the fact that it is an isomorphism of specialization systems), we obtain that for any smooth restriction
\phi_{i}:(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{i},f^{\prime}_{i})\to(\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i})
, the following square of isomorphisms commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2}})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi_{1}\times\phi_{2}}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{1}\boxplus f^{\prime}_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\tilde{\phi}_{1})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi_{1}}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}})\boxtimes((\tilde{\phi}_{2})^{\ast}\{d_{\phi_{2}}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1},f^{\prime}_{1}}\boxtimes P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

Moreover, because pullbacks are symmetric monoidal functors, and the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism of Theorem
4.2
satisfies commutativity and associativity, we obtain that the above morphism satisfies commutativity and associativity.

5.4.4.
Stabilization by quadratic bundles

As said above, given a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
on
\mathcal{U}
, one consider the critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i)
obtained by stabilization by
(\mathcal{E},q)
. We obtain the following sequence of isomorphism:

\displaystyle P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q}

\displaystyle:=(s\circ i)^{\ast}\{d_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{U})}/2\}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f\circ p+q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}

\displaystyle\simeq i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2+d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}s^{\ast}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f\circ p+q}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}

\displaystyle\simeq i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2+d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\}(\phi^{mon,tot}_{f}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}\{-d_{\mathcal{E}}/2\})

\displaystyle\simeq i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}\phi^{mon,tot}_{f}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)})|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}

(5.18)

\displaystyle=:P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}

where the third line comes from Lemma
4.4
. From the compatibility with smooth pullbacks and exterior tensor products obtained in Lemma
4.4
, we obtain then that the above isomorphism commutes also with smooth restrictions and exterior products. Namely, given
\phi:(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, denoting
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime}):=\phi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)
, the following square of isomorphisms commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}(P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}(\mathcal{E}^{\prime})},f^{\prime}\circ p^{\prime}+q^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})|_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime red}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

given two critical charts
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
,
i=1,2
, and quadratic bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
on
\mathcal{U}_{i}
, denoting the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}}(\mathcal{E}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{E}_{2}),(f_{1}\boxplus f_{2})\circ(p_{1}\times p_{2})+(q_{1}\boxplus q_{2})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1}\boxplus\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{1}\boxplus q_{2})}|_{(\mathcal{R}_{1}\times\mathcal{R}_{2})^{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}}(\mathcal{E}_{1}),f_{1}\circ p_{1}+q_{1}}\boxtimes P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}_{2}}(\mathcal{E}_{2}),f_{2}\circ p_{2}+q_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1},q_{1})}|_{\mathcal{R}_{1}^{red}})\boxtimes(P_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}|_{\mathcal{R}_{2}^{red}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

The compatibility with direct sum of quadratic bundles gives that, given two quadratic bundles
(\mathcal{E}_{i},q_{i})
on the same stack
\mathcal{U}
, denoting
(\mathcal{E},q):=(\mathcal{E}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{1}\oplus q_{2})
, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}_{1}),f\circ\pi_{1}+q_{1}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{1},q_{1})}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E}_{2},q_{2})}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

i.e. this isomorphism is compatible with composition of stabilizations by quadratic form.

Moreover, given a trivialization
(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{X}}^{n},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2})
, this isomorphism is from Lemma
4.4
simply the isomorphism considered in
[
BBD+15
]
, obtained from Thom-Sebastiani:

(5.19)

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{U}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k},f\boxplus\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}\simeq P_{\mathcal{U}}\boxtimes_{mon}P_{\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}\simeq P_{\mathcal{U}}

5.5.
Gluing of the DT sheaf

The next proposition (which is a formal consequence of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8]
) ensure that one can work with stacky critical charts, and arbitrary stabilization by quadratic bundle in cohomological DT theory:

Proposition 5.8
.

Consider an oriented d-critical stack
(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})
, the object
P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X})
built in
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8]
(building from
[
BBD+15
, theorem 6.9]
) is uniquely characterized by the following properties:

i)

For any critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, there is a natural isomorphism:

(5.20)

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{\mathcal{R}}\{d_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{U}}/2\}\simeq P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}

.

ii)

Given a smooth restriction of critical charts
\phi:(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{\mathcal{R}}\{d_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{U}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}(P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\{d_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}/\mathcal{U}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the horizontal arrows are the isomorphisms from
i)
, and the right vertical arrow is the tensor product of the isomorphism of Section
5.4.2
and Lemma (
5.5
)
ii)
.

iii)

Given a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
, the following diagram of isomorphism is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{\mathcal{R}}\{d_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q,s\circ i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{\mathcal{E},q}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q,s\circ i)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the upper horizontal and left vertical arrows arrows are the isomorphism of
i)
for
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and its stabilization
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ p+q,s\circ i)
, the lower horizontal arrow is the isomorphism from Section
5.4.4
, and the right vertical arrow the isomorphism of Lemma
5.5

iii)
.

Proof.

In
[
BBD+15
, theorem 6.9]
(the scheme case), the authors works with perverse sheaves and mixed Hodge modules, whence in
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8]
(the stack case), the authors works with perverse sheaves. Thanks to the development of the formalism of mixed Hodge modules and perverse Nori motives on Artin stacks in
[
Tub24
]
, and the developement of the formalism of monodromic perverse Nori motives and Thom-Sebastiani in Section
4.2
, this construction extends automatically to the level of monodromic mixed Hodge modules and monodromic perverse Nori motives on stacks (more precisely, perverse Nori motives satisfies all the properties required in
[
BBD+15
, Section 2.5]
for the construction to work).

i)

consider a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
of
(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})
, and a smooth cover
\phi:U^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
by a scheme. Consider the smooth restriction
\phi:(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
. From
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8 a)]
and
[
BBD+15
, Theorem 6.9 i)]
, there is a natural isomorphism:

(5.21)

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{R^{\prime}}\{d_{R^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}}/2\}\simeq P_{U^{\prime},f^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})}

Consider any smooth cover
U^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{U}}U^{\prime}
: from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8 b), Proposition 4.5 a)]
, the two pullbacks along
R^{\prime\prime}\to R^{\prime}
of the last isomorphism agree with the last isomorphism for
(R^{\prime\prime},U^{\prime\prime},f^{\prime\prime},i^{\prime\prime})
, hence are equal, which means that the last isomorphism descend to the isomorphism of
i)
. Given an other choice of smooth cover
\phi:\tilde{U}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
, using a smooth cover
U^{\prime\prime}\to U^{\prime}\times_{\mathcal{U}}\tilde{U}^{\prime}
, a similar argument shows that this isomorphism is independent of the choice of
U^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
.

ii)

Consider a smooth cover
U^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{U}^{\prime}
by a scheme, and the induced smooth morphism
U^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
. By the definition of the isomorphism of
i)
, the pullback of the square of
ii)
along
R^{\prime\prime}\to\mathcal{R}^{\prime}
is the identity, hence by conservativity of smooth pullbacks the square of
ii)
is commutative.

iii)

Consider a smooth morphism
\phi:U^{\prime}\to\mathcal{U}
from a scheme and a trivialization of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime}):=\phi^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{\mathcal{U}}^{n},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2})
. The square of
iii)
for
(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})
and
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
is then:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}|_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}}\{d_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}/\mathcal{X}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U^{\prime},f^{\prime}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k},f\boxplus\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(R^{\prime},U^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k},f\boxplus\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2},i^{\prime}\times 0)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U^{\prime}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}_{k},f^{\prime}\boxplus\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}Q_{(R^{\prime},U^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

It is an isomorphism from
[
BBD+15
, Theorem 6.9 ii), Theorem 5.4 a)]
. From
ii)
, the left vertical and upper horizontal arrows are obtained from pullback along
R^{\prime}\to\mathcal{R}
from the corresponding arrows of the square of
iii)
for
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and
(\mathcal{E},q)
. From Section (
5.4.4
), the isomorphism of stabilization by quadratic forms commutes with smooth pullbacks, and, using the fact that the trivialization of
(\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime})
gives a section of
P_{\mathcal{E}^{\prime},q^{\prime}}
, we obtain that the lower horizontal and right vertical arrows are obtained by pullback from the corresponding arrows of the square of
iii)
. By covering
\mathcal{U}
by such trivialization, using faithfullness of smooth pullbacks, one obtains that the square of
iii)
is commutative.

∎

From the above characterization, one obtains directly:

Corollary 5.9
.

The construction of
P_{\mathcal{X}}
from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 4.8]
enhance naturally to a symmetric monoidal functor from the 2-category of oriented d-critical stacks with smooth morphisms. Namely, given a smooth morphism of oriented
d
-critical charts
\phi:(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})\to(\mathcal{Y},t,K_{\mathcal{Y},t}^{1}/2)
, there is a natural isomorphism in
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X})
:

(5.22)

\displaystyle\phi^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{Y},t,K_{\mathcal{Y},t}^{1}/2}\simeq P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}

compatible with composition, and given oriented d-critical stacks
(\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i},K_{\mathcal{X}_{i},s_{i}}^{1/2})
, there is a natural isomorphism in
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2})
:

(5.23)

\displaystyle P_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\times s_{2},K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}^{1/2}\otimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}^{1/2}}\simeq P_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1},K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}^{1/2}}\boxtimes_{mon}P_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2},K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}^{1/2}}

satisfying the obvious commutativity, associativity, and compatibility with smooth pullbacks.

Proof.

The functoriality with smooth morphisms follows directly from the unique characterization given above, as
\phi^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{Y},t,K_{\mathcal{Y},t}^{1}/2}
satisfies it with an obvious choice of isomorphism in
i)
, which is obviously compatible with composition. As recalled in the proof of Lemma
5.6
,
(\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\times s_{2})
can be covered by critical charts being products, and any two such critical charts can be compared by products of smooth restrictions and products of stabilization by quadratic bundles. Hence the restriction of
i),ii),iii)
to such product characterize
P_{\mathcal{X}_{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{1}\times s_{2},K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}^{1/2}\otimes K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}^{1/2}}
uniquely. Using the isomorphism from Section
5.4.3
, one obtains that
P_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1},K_{\mathcal{X}_{1},s_{1}}^{1/2}}\boxtimes_{mon}P_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2},K_{\mathcal{X}_{2},s_{2}}^{1/2}}
satisfies
i)
with a natural choice of isomorphism, which is compatible with smooth restriction, hence satisfies
ii)
, from Section
5.4.3
, and is compatible with stabilization by quadratic forms, hence satisfies
iii)
, from Section
5.4.4
. It gives a natural choice of isomorphism (
5.23
). From Section
5.4.3
, the isomorphisms of
i)
are satisfies commutativity and associativity and are compatible with smooth pullbacks, hence (
5.23
) does it too.
∎

Because the authors of
[
BBD+15
]
and
[
BBBBJ15
]
have to build an object in a
1
-category, the proof contains three main steps, of increasing complexity:

•

Defining locally the perverse sheaf of vanishing cycles on a critical chart (with a careful treatment of orientation). This corresponds to point
i)
of the above proposition

•

Defining comparison isomorphisms locally on intersection of critical charts. This is done by comparing critical charts by étale (hence, smooth) restrictions and stabilization by quadratic forms (hence, stabilization by trivialized quadratic bundles), and then defining locally on intersections of critical charts gluing isomorphisms from the equations of point
ii)
and
iii)
.

•

Showing that these isomorphisms glue into global isomorphism on intersections of two critical charts, and that they satisfies cocycle condition on intersection of three critical charts.

The third step is certainly the harder and deeper part of the proof, using deep results on
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-deformation of isomorphisms of critical charts,
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-homotopy invariance, and where orientation data becomes crucial. Fortunately, once this is done, any construction or computation on cohomological DT theory can virtually be expressed as the construction of an (iso)-morphism between such objects, hence (as long as one manage to stay in the Abelian category
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}
) one has to consider only the two first steps, namely:

•

Defining locally the (iso)morphism, working on a critical chart.

•

Showing that these (iso)morphisms agrees locally (hence globally, because they are morphisms in a
1
-category) on intersection of critical charts. it suffices to prove that they are compatible with smooth restrictions of critical charts, and stabilization by quadratic form.

Notice that the authors of
[
BBD+15
]
and
[
BBBBJ15
]
works only with schematic critical charts. However, in order to study the
\Theta
correspondence, this cannot works, as the
\Theta
-correspondence is trivial for schemes, hence the
{\rm Grad}
of a d-critical stack cannot be covered by
{\rm Grad}
of schematic critical charts. This is why we had to extend slightly the formalism of
[
Joy13
]
to consider stacky critical charts, and stabilization by quadratic bundles: see Proposition
6.8
. We could have consider only critical charts of the form
([R/\mathbb{G}_{m}],[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f,i)
, but we felt that adopting a more flexible formalism should be more natural and useful (for example, to use also étale critical charts of the quotient form, using étale-local structure results on stacks from
[
AHR20
]
,
[
AHR19
]
). Notice that stabilization by quadratic bundles was also used in
[
BJM19
]
to glue motives of vanishing cycles: for this, one has to work in the Nisnevich topology, and then one cannot trivialize quadratic bundles as in the Zariski topology. Stabilization by quadratic bundle was also used crucially in the homotopy-coherent formalism of
[
HHR24
]
.

6.
Hyperbolic localization in DT theory

In this section, we work over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
0
. All our stacks are assumed to be quasi-separated Artin
1
-stacks locally of finite type over
k
, with affine stabilizers. Given an oriented d-critical stack
(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})
, we will often abuse the notation by denoting
P_{\mathcal{X}}
for
P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}
, when the d-critical structure and the orientation are clear from the context.

6.1.
Hyperbolic localization on critical loci

6.1.1.
Local definition of the isomorphism

Consider a critical locus
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
. As recalled from Halpern-Leistner in Lemma
3.9
,
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})
(resp.
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{U})
) is also smooth, with cotangent complex
(\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}})^{0}
(resp.
(\eta^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}})^{\leq 0}
. We have the following result:

Lemma 6.1
.

{\rm Grad}(i):{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})
is the closed immersion of the critical locus of
{\rm Grad}(f)
.

Proof.

Denote by
T^{\ast}\mathcal{U}:=\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}}^{\vee})
the cotangent stack of
\mathcal{U}
. We have that
{\rm Grad}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U})\simeq T^{\ast}{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})
, and the section
0,d{\rm Grad}(f):{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})\to T^{\ast}{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})
identifies with
{\rm Grad}(0),{\rm Grad}(df):{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})\to{\rm Grad}(T^{\ast}\mathcal{U})
. Then, as
{\rm Crit}(f):=\mathcal{U}\times_{0,T^{\ast}\mathcal{U},df}\mathcal{U}
, we obtain directly, from the compatibility of the mapping construction with fiber product:

(6.1)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}({\rm Crit}(f))={\rm Crit}({\rm Grad}(f))

Notice that the same reasoning, applied to the derived critical locus
\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f):=\mathcal{U}\overset{h}{\times}_{0,T^{\ast}\mathcal{U},df}\mathcal{U}
, gives also at the derived level:

(6.2)

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f))=\underline{{\rm Crit}}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(f))

∎

In particular,
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f),{\rm Grad}(i))
is a critical locus. We consider the locally constant function
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}:{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})\to\mathbb{Z}
defined by:

(6.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}:=d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}-d^{-}_{\mathcal{U}}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}

where
d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}
(resp.
d^{-}_{\mathcal{U}}
) denotes the virtual dimension of
\mathbb{L}^{<0}_{\mathcal{U}}
(resp.
\mathbb{L}^{>0}_{\mathcal{U}}
) (see Section
3.3.2
). We choose this sign convention such that
d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}
counts the virtual dimension of the positive weight part of the tangent stack. Notice that, when we consider the derived critical locus
\underline{\mathcal{R}}
,
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}
is the virtual dimension of
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{R}}}^{<0}
.

Given a smooth restriction of critical loci
\phi:(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, we define similarly the locally constant function
{\rm Ind}_{\phi}:{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})\to\mathbb{Z}
:

(6.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\phi}:=d^{+}_{\phi}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}-d^{-}_{\phi}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})}

where
d^{+}_{\phi}
(resp.
d^{-}_{\phi}
) denotes the virtual dimension of
\mathbb{L}^{<0}_{\phi}
(resp.
\mathbb{L}^{>0}_{\phi}
). one obtains directly
d^{\pm}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}=d^{\pm}_{\mathcal{U}}\circ\tilde{\phi}+d^{\pm}_{\phi}
, which gives:

(6.5)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}\circ\tilde{\phi}+{\rm Ind}_{\phi}

Given
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
,
i=1,2
, we have also:

(6.6)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{1}}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}}\boxplus{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}}

Proposition 6.2
.

For any critical locus
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, there is a canonical isomorphism in
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{R})
:

(6.7)

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U},f}\simeq P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f)}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}/2\}

It is compatible with products of critical loci, namely for two critical chart
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
,
i=1,2
, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2}),{\rm Grad}(f_{1}\boxplus f_{2})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{1}\times\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{1}\boxplus f_{2}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((p_{1})!(\eta_{1})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}})\boxtimes((p_{2})_{!}(\eta_{2})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}_{1}),{\rm Grad}(f_{1})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1}}/2\})\boxtimes(P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}_{2}),{\rm Grad}(f_{2})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2}}/2\})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical arrows come from (
5.4.3
).

Proof.

We consider the following sequence of isomorphisms:

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U},f}:=

\displaystyle(p_{\mathcal{R}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}i^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}\{d_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}(p_{\mathcal{U}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{U}})^{\ast}\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}\{(d_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}+d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}+d^{-}_{\mathcal{U}})/2\}\phi_{{\rm Grad}(f)}^{mon,tot}(p_{\mathcal{U}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{U}})^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}\{(d_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}-d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}+d^{-}_{\mathcal{U}})/2\}\phi_{{\rm Grad}(f)}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}

(6.8)

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f)}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}/2\}

where the second line follows from Lemma
3.15
applied to the closed immersion
i:\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{U}
, using the fact that
\phi_{f}^{mon,tot}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}
is supported on
\mathcal{R}
, the third line from Theorem
3.7
applied to the specialization system
\phi^{mon,tot}
, the fourth line from Proposition
3.12
, and the last line from the definition, and the formula (
6.3
).

Consider two critical loci
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
for
i=1,2
. From the Lemma
3.15
, the commutation between the restriction to the critical locus and hyperbolic localization is compatible with products. From Theorem
4.2
, the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphism is an isomorphism of specialization system, hence commutes with the morphism of commutation between monodromic vanishing cycles and hyperbolic localization, obtained from the specialization system exchange morphisms. From Proposition
3.12
, the Białynicki-Birula isomorphism for smooth stacks is compatible with products. One obtains then that the above square is commutative.
∎

6.1.2.
Hyperbolic localization and smooth restrictions

Consider a smooth restriction of critical loci
\phi:(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
. From Proposition
3.12
, there is a natural isomorphism
(p_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}})^{\ast}(\tilde{\phi})^{\ast}\simeq{\rm Grad}(\tilde{\phi})^{\ast}\{-2d^{+}_{\phi}\}(p_{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{R}}^{\prime})^{\ast}
, which can be rewritten from definition (
6.3
):

(6.9)

\displaystyle(p_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{R}^{\prime}})^{\ast}\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}\simeq{\rm Grad}(\tilde{\phi})^{\ast}\{d^{0}_{\phi}/2\}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\phi}/2\}(p_{\mathcal{R}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}

We have the following compatibility lemma:

Lemma 6.3
.

The hyperbolic localization isomorphism of Proposition
6.2
is compatible with smooth restriction of critical loci, namely for
\phi:(\mathcal{R}^{\prime},\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime},i^{\prime})\to(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
a smooth restriction, the following diagram of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\tilde{\phi})^{\ast}\{d^{0}_{\phi}/2\}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\phi}/2\}p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U},f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\tilde{\phi})^{\ast}\{d^{0}_{\phi}/2\}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\phi}/2\}P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f)}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p^{\prime})_{!}(\eta^{\prime})^{\ast}\tilde{\phi}^{\ast}\{d_{\phi}/2\}P_{\mathcal{U},f}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}),{\rm Grad}(f^{\prime})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}/2\}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\phi}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p^{\prime})_{!}(\eta^{\prime})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}),{\rm Grad}(f^{\prime})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime},f^{\prime}}/2\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the horizontal arrows come from Proposition
6.2
, and the vertical arrows from (
5.4.2
) and (
6.9
).

Proof.

From Proposition
3.12
, the isomorphism of commutation between hyperbolic localization and smooth pullback is compatible with composition, hence the following diagram is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\phi)^{\ast}\{-2d^{+}_{\phi}\}p_{!}\eta^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\phi)^{\ast}\{-2d^{+}_{\phi}\}\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}\{-2d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p^{\prime})_{!}(\eta^{\prime})^{\ast}\phi^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})}\{-2d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}}\}\{-2d^{+}_{\phi}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(p^{\prime})_{!}(\eta^{\prime})^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}^{\prime})}\{-2d^{+}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

As
\phi^{mon,tot}
is a specialization system, the morphisms of commutation between
\phi^{mon,tot}
, hyperbolic localization and smooth pullbacks are compatible from Lemma
3.14
. From Lemma
3.15
, the isomorphisms of commutation between smooth pullbacks, restriction to the critical locus, and hyperbolic localization, are compatible. Hence the result follows.
∎

6.1.3.
Hyperbolic localization and quadratic bundles

Consider a critical locus
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
on
\mathcal{U}
, and the critical locus
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i)
obtained by stabilization by
(\mathcal{E},q)
. We have that
\iota^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
(resp
\eta^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
) is naturally
\mathbb{Z}
-graded (resp.
\mathbb{Z}
-filtered), as recalled from
[
HL20
, Section 1]
in Section
3.3.2
. From the non-degenerate quadratic form
q
, one obtains a canonical splitting of the
\mathbb{Z}
-filtration of
\eta^{\ast}\mathcal{E}
, and then an isomorphism
\eta^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)^{I}\simeq p^{\ast}\iota^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)^{I}
. We denote:

\displaystyle({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{I},{\rm Grad}(q)^{I})

\displaystyle:=\iota^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)^{I}

(6.10)

\displaystyle({\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{I},{\rm Filt}(q)^{I})

\displaystyle:=\eta^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)^{I}\simeq p^{\ast}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{I},{\rm Grad}(q)^{I})

In particular
{\rm Grad}(q)^{0},{\rm Filt}(q)^{0}
and
{\rm Filt}(q)
are nondegenerate, i.e.
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0}),({\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Filt}(q)^{0})
and
({\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Filt}(q)^{0})
are quadratic bundles. A simple computation gives:

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{V}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0})

(6.11)

\displaystyle{\rm Filt}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{X}}(\mathcal{E}))

\displaystyle\simeq\mathbb{V}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}({\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{\leq 0})

We obtain that the
{\rm Grad}
of
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i)
is the critical chart:

(6.12)

\displaystyle({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),\mathbb{V}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0}),{\rm Grad}(f)\circ{\rm Grad}(\pi)^{0}+{\rm Grad}(q)^{0},{\rm Grad}(s)^{0}\circ{\rm Grad}(i))

obtained by stabilization from
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f),{\rm Grad}(i))
by the quadratic bundle
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0})
. Moreover,
{\rm Grad}(q)
(resp.
{\rm Filt}(q))
) induces a perfect pairing between
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{<0}
and
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{>0}
(resp.
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{<0}
and
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E})^{>0}
), in particular
d^{-}_{\mathcal{E}}=d^{+}_{\mathcal{E}}
, hence:

(6.13)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}}+{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{E}}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}}

And

(6.14)

\displaystyle\det({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E}))\simeq\det({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0})\otimes\det({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{<0})\otimes\det({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{>0})\simeq\det({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0})

where the last isomorphism is induced by the perfect pairing. It gives a canonical identification between orientations of
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E}),{\rm Grad}(q))
and
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0}))
, i.e. an isomorphism:

(6.15)

\displaystyle P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E}),{\rm Grad}(q))}\simeq P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0})}

and furthermore an isomorphism:

(6.16)

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}(-\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{\mathcal{E},q})\simeq p_{!}(\eta^{\ast}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{E}),{\rm Filt}(q)}\simeq(p_{!}\eta^{\ast})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E}),{\rm Grad}(q))}\simeq(p_{!}\eta^{\ast})\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0})}

The compatibility between hyperbolic localization and stabilization by quadratic forms is obtained as follows:

Lemma 6.4
.

Given a critical locus
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and a quadratic bundle
(\mathcal{E},q)
on
\mathcal{U}
, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{p}_{!}\tilde{\eta}^{\ast}P_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})),{\rm Grad}(f\circ\pi+q)}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{p}_{!}\tilde{\eta}^{\ast}(P_{\mathcal{U},f}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{\mathcal{E},q}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f)}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U}}/2\}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0})}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})^{red}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical arrows are obtained by the isomorphisms of Section
5.4.4
, and the horizontal arrows by the isomorphisms of Proposition
6.2
and the isomorphism (
6.16
).

Proof.

Using Halpern-Leistner’s Lemma
3.3
, one can cover
\mathcal{U}
by smooth maps
\phi:[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to\mathcal{U}
by quotients of affine scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, such that
{\rm Grad}([U/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})
(and furthermore,
{\rm Grad}([R/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})
) are jointly surjective. Then using compatibility of hyperbolic localization and the action of quadratic bundle with smooth pullbacks, one is reduced to the case where
\mathcal{U}=[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
,
U={\rm Spec}(A)
, where
A
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
k
-algebra
A
, and we have to work in the neighborhood of a point
u
in
U
which is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed. Up to Zariski-local restriction, one can assume that
\mathcal{E}
is a free module generated by
\mathbb{Z}
-graded elements. Now,
q\in\mathcal{E}\otimes\mathcal{E}
induces a non-degenerate pairing between the positive and negative part, one can take a basis
e_{i}
of the positive part, and the dual basis
f_{j}
of the negative part, such that
q=\sum e_{i}\otimes f_{i}+q^{0}
, with
q^{0}
of degree
0
. Up to taking an étale extension of
A^{0}
, one can diagonalize
q^{0}
, such that
q=\sum e_{i}\otimes f_{i}+\sum g_{j}\otimes g_{j}
.

Then, we can assume that
\mathcal{U}=[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
and that, considering the smooth map
\phi^{\prime}:U\to[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
:

(6.17)

\displaystyle(\phi^{\prime})^{\ast}(\mathcal{E},q)\simeq(\mathcal{O}_{U}^{n+2m},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}y_{j}z_{j})

where the
x_{i}
(resp.
y_{j}
, resp.
z_{j}
) are homogeneous of weight
0
(resp.
>0
, resp.
<0
). We have that
{\rm Grad}([U/\mathbb{G}_{m}])=\bigsqcup_{\lambda\in\mathbb{Z}}[U^{0}_{\lambda}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
. Up to replacing the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action by some power, we just have to check that the pullback of the square of the Lemma along
R^{0}\to[R^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
commutes. Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{+}\times\mathbb{A}^{n+m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U\times\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{+}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p:=p_{U}\times p_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta:=\eta_{U}\times\eta_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{U}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{U}}

Then, denoting
f^{\prime}:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
, it follows from the last statement of Lemma
4.4
that the pullback of the square of the Lemma to
R^{0}
is the square of isomorphisms:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{p}_{!}\tilde{\eta}^{\ast}P_{U\times\mathbb{A}^{n+2m},f^{\prime}\boxplus\sum_{i}x_{i}^{2}+\sum_{j}y_{j}z_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U^{0}\times\mathbb{A}^{n},f^{\prime}\boxplus\sum_{i}x_{i}^{2}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{U\times\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}}/2\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\tilde{p}_{U})_{!}(\tilde{\eta}_{U})^{\ast}P_{U,f^{\prime}})\boxtimes((\tilde{p}_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}})_{!}(\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}})^{\ast}P_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m},\sum_{i}x_{i}^{2}+\sum_{j}y_{j}z_{j}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U^{0},(f^{\prime})^{0}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{U}/2\}\boxtimes P_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\sum_{i}x_{i}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces((\tilde{p_{U}})_{!}(\tilde{\eta_{U}})^{\ast}P_{U,f^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{U^{0},(f^{\prime})^{0}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{U}/2\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical arrows are the Thom-Sebastiani isomorphisms of subsection
5.4.3
, and the horizontal isomorphisms are obtained as those of Proposition
6.2
. Then, from Proposition
6.2
, the upper square commutes, and it suffices to prove that the following square commutes:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{p}_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}})_{!}(\tilde{\eta}_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m}})^{\ast}P_{\mathbb{A}^{n+2m},\sum_{i}x_{i}^{2}+\sum_{j}y_{j}z_{j}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces P_{\mathbb{A}^{n},\sum_{i=1}^{n}x_{i}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}_{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbbm{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

where the upper horizontal arrow is obtained by hyperbolic localization, and the vertical arrows by Lemma
4.3
.
Using Thom-Sebastiani again, it suffices to prove that the hyperbolic localization isomorphism
P_{\mathbb{A}^{2},xy}\simeq\mathbbm{1}
is the isomorphism
4.36
. Consider the commutative diagram with Cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{(0,0)\}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

By definition, the isomorphism of commutation with hyperbolic localization of Proposition
6.2

is given by (noticing that

(6.18)

\displaystyle i^{\ast}\phi_{xy}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\simeq(p_{0})_{!}\hat{i}_{!}\hat{i}^{!}(\eta_{0})^{\ast}\phi_{xy}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\overset{\simeq}{\leftarrow}(p_{0})_{!}(\eta_{0})^{\ast}\phi_{xy}^{mon}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\overset{\simeq}{\to}\phi_{0}^{mon}p_{!}\eta^{\ast}\mathbbm{1}_{\mathbb{A}^{2}}\simeq\mathbbm{1}[-2](-1)

Consider the following diagram:
