Using
p_{0}=\rho\circ\eta_{0}
, the upper path corresponds to the hyperbolic localization isomorphism (
6.18
); whence the bottom path corresponds to
4.36
. Using
i=\hat{i}\circ\eta_{0}
, the left triangle commutes by functoriality and the left square commutes by compatibility of adjunctions morphisms with composition; the right triengle commutes by the compatiblity of the morphisms of specialization system with adjunctions, and the right square commutes because
\phi_{0}^{mon}=Id
. We obtained then the desired equality of isomorphisms. Notice that we could have instead defined directly
4.36
using hyperbolic localization, but the definition using dimensional reduction is the usual one, closer to what is done in
[
GDK72
, Exposé XV]
.
∎

6.2.
Existence and comparison of critical charts

6.2.1.
D-critical version

A
d
-critical structure on a stack
\mathcal{X}
gives by definition a smooth covering of
\mathcal{X}
by critical charts
(R,U,f,i)
, where
R,U
are schemes, and the results of
[
Joy13
, Section 2.3]
gives a way to compare such charts, using stabilization by quadratic forms, still using critical charts given by schemes. However, the hyperbolic localization diagram for a scheme is trivial, so such ’d-critical atlas’ is not suitable to study hyperbolic localization. One must be able to cover
\mathcal{X}
by critical charts
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
such that the
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
are jointly surjective, and to be able to compare them in a similar way. Thanks to Halpern-Leistner’s Lemma
3.3
, it suffices to build
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant critical charts, and to compare them in a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant way. Torus-equivariant analogues of
[
Joy13
, Section 2.3]
were partially given in
[
Joy13
, Section 2.6]
, we just complete them here by following closely Joyce’s arguments:

Proposition 6.5
.

(Joyce,
[
Joy13
, Section 2.3, Section 2.6]
)
Consider an algebraic space
X
with a torus action
\mu:T\times X\to X
, with a
T
-equivariant
d
-critical structure
s
of weight
\chi:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
(i.e. such that
\mu^{\star}s=\chi\cdot(p_{2})^{\star}s\in H^{0}(\mathcal{S}^{0}_{T\times X})
).

i)

X
is covered étale-locally by
T
-equivariant affine critical charts (i.e. critical charts
(R,U,f,i)
such that
R,U
are affine with
T
-action,
R\to X
is a
T
-equivariant open immersion,
i:R\to U
is a
T
-equivariant closed immersion, and
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi
).

ii)

Given
(R_{i},U_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
,
i=1,2
two étale affine
T
-equivariant critical charts and a
T
-fixed point
(x_{1},x_{2})\in R_{1}\times_{X}R_{2}
, there exist a diagram of
T
-equivariant critical charts:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R,U,f,i)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R^{\prime}_{1},U^{\prime}_{1},f^{\prime}_{1},i^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R^{\prime}_{1},U^{\prime}_{1}\times E_{1},f^{\prime}_{1}\boxplus q_{1},i^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R^{\prime}_{2},U^{\prime}_{2}\times E_{2},f^{\prime}_{2}\boxplus q_{2},i^{\prime}_{2}\times 0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R^{\prime}_{2},U^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{2},i^{\prime}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{1},U_{1},f_{1},i_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(R_{2},U_{2},f_{2},i_{2})}

with a
T
-fixed point
x\in R
over
(x_{1},x_{2})
, where the dotted arrows corresponds to stabilization by
T
-equivariant quadratic forms (i.e. ,
E_{i}
is an affine space with linear
T
-action, and
q_{i}
is a
T
-equivariant non-degenerate quadratic form of weight
\chi
), and the plain arrows are
T
-equivariant étale morphisms of critical charts

Proof.

i)

Consider
x\in X(k)
: by
[
AHR20
, Theorem 4.1]
(notice that the conditions are trivially satisfied, as
X
is an algebraic space), there is a
T
-equivariant pointed étale map
\phi:(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})\to(X,x)
, where
X^{\prime}
is affine, in particular its
T
-action is automatically ’good’ in the sense of
[
Joy13
, Definition 2.41]
. Considering
s^{\prime}:=\phi^{\star}(s)
, one can applies
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.44]
to
(X^{\prime},s^{\prime})
near
X^{\prime}
to obtain a Zariski
T
-equivariant critical chart for
(X^{\prime},s^{\prime})
near
x^{\prime}
, which gives by compositions an étale
T
-equivariant critical chart for
(X,s)
over
x
.

ii)

This is a torus-equivariant analogue of the comparison given in
[
Joy13
, Section 2.3]
. We first applies
[
AHR20
, Theorem 4.1]
to
(R_{1}\times_{X}R_{2},(x_{1},x_{2}))
to obtain a
T
-equivariant pointed étale map
(\tilde{R},\tilde{x}\to(R_{1}\times_{X}R_{2},(x_{1},x_{2}))
with
\tilde{R}
affine, which is stabilizer preserving at
\tilde{x}
, and consider the
T
-equivariant
d
-critical structure
\tilde{s}
of weight
\chi
on
\tilde{R}
obtained by pullback, and the
T
-fixed point
\tilde{x}
.

According to
[
Sta18
, Tag 04B1, 04D1]
, given a smooth (resp. étale) morphism of affine scheme
\tilde{R}={\rm Spec}(\tilde{A})\to R_{i}={\rm Spec}(A_{i})
, and a closed immersion
R_{i}\to U_{i}={\rm Spec}(B_{i})
, and
\tilde{x}\in\tilde{R}
there exists a distinguished open neighborhood
\check{R}
of
\tilde{x}
(resp. one can take
\check{R}=\tilde{R}
) in
\tilde{R}
, an affine scheme
\check{U}_{i}={\rm Spec}(\check{B}_{i})
with a smooth (resp. étale) morphism
\check{U}_{i}\to U_{i}
, and a closed immersion
\check{R}\to\check{U}
. One can check easily that a
T
-equivariant version of this Lemma holds. Indeed,
\tilde{A},A_{i},B_{i}
have a
\Gamma:={\rm Hom}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
-grading corresponding to the
T
-action, and it suffices to give a version of the proof using only homogeneous elements. The proof of
[
Sta18
, Tag 04B1, 04D1]
(every smooth affine map is locally standard smooth, resp. every étale map is standard smooth) can easily be adapted to the
\Gamma
-graded setting. It gives that
\tilde{R}
is covered by open subsets
\check{R}=D(g)
, for
g\in\tilde{A}
homogeneous (such that
D(g)
is
T
-invariant), such that
\tilde{A}_{g}
can be written as
A_{i}[x_{1},...,x_{n}]/(\bar{f}_{1},...,\bar{f}_{c})
, with
x_{1},...,x_{n},\bar{f}_{1},...,\bar{f}_{c}
homogeneous (resp. with
c=n
), such that
\det((\partial x_{i}/\partial\bar{f}_{i})_{1\leq i\leq c})
is invertible in
\tilde{A}_{g}
. Now, one can choose homogeneous lifts
f_{1},...,f_{c}\in B_{i}[x_{1},...,x_{n}]
of
\bar{f}_{1},...,\bar{f}_{c}\in A_{i}[x_{1},...,x_{n}]
. As the
f_{i}
are homogeneous,
\Delta:=\det((\partial x_{i}/\partial f_{i})_{1\leq i\leq c})
is homogeneous: consider
x_{n+1}
, of weight opposite to
\Delta
, and the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded ring
\check{B}_{i}:=B_{i}[x_{1},...,x_{n+1}]/(f_{1},...,f_{c},x_{n+1}\Delta-1)
. Then,
\check{U}_{i}:={\rm Spec}(\check{A}_{i})
is
T
-equivariant,
\phi_{i}:\check{U}_{i}\to U_{i}
is smooth (resp. étale) and
T
-equivariant, and
\check{i}_{i}:\check{R}\to\check{U}_{i}
is a
T
-equivariant closed immersion.

We applies the étale version of the last paragraph, and define
\check{R}_{i}:=\check{R}=\tilde{R}
and
\check{f}_{i}:=f_{i}\circ\phi_{i}
, and we obtain two Zariski critical charts
(\check{R}_{i},\check{U}_{i},\check{f}_{i},\check{i}_{i})
for
(\tilde{R},\tilde{s})
, and a
T
-fixed point
\tilde{x}\in\check{R}_{1}\cap\check{R}_{2}
. Using
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.44]
, up to restricting
\check{U}_{i}
near
\tilde{x}
, and then
\check{R}_{i}
, to a
T
-invariant open subset
U^{\prime}_{i}
(that can be chosen to be distinguished, hence affine), one has
T
-equivariant closed immersions of
T
-equivariant critical charts
\Phi:(R^{\prime}_{i},U^{\prime}_{i},f^{\prime}_{i},i^{\prime}_{i})\to(\hat{R},\hat{U},\hat{f},\hat{i})
(i.e. ,
\Phi:U^{\prime}_{i}\to\hat{U}
is a
T
-equivariant closed immersion, such that
f^{\prime}_{i}=\hat{f}\circ\Phi
, and such that
{\rm Crit}(\hat{f})=\Phi({\rm Crit}(f^{\prime}_{i}))
). As
\tilde{x}
is
T
-fixed, one applies then the following Lemma
6.6
twice to obtain
T
equivariant étale maps
(\hat{R}_{i},\hat{U}_{i},\hat{f}_{i},\hat{i}_{i})\to(\hat{R},\hat{U},\hat{f},\hat{i})
and
(\hat{R}_{i},\hat{U}_{i},\hat{f}_{i},\hat{i}_{i})\to(R^{\prime}_{i},U^{\prime}_{i}\times E_{i},f^{\prime}_{i}\boxplus q_{i},i^{\prime}_{i})
, with a
T
-fixed point
\hat{x}_{i}
over
x_{i}
. We take then:

(6.19)

\displaystyle(R,U,f,i):=(\hat{R}_{1}\times_{\hat{R}}\hat{R}_{2},\hat{U}_{1}\times_{\hat{U}}\hat{U}_{2},\hat{f}_{1}\times_{\hat{f}}\hat{f}_{2},\hat{i}_{1}\times_{\hat{i}}\hat{i}_{2})

and the
T
-fixed point
x=(\hat{x}_{1},\hat{x}_{2})\in R
, which is over
(x_{1},x_{2})
. Schematically, this gives:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{1}\times E_{1},f^{\prime}_{1}\boxplus q_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U,f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{2}\times E_{2},f^{\prime}_{2}\boxplus q_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{1},f^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\hat{U}_{1},\hat{f}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\hat{U}_{2},\hat{f}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1},f_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\hat{U},\hat{f})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{2},f_{2})}

where dashed arrows denotes
T
-equivariant closed embeddings, dotted arrows stabilization by
T
-equivariant quadratic forms, and plain arrows
T
-equivariant étale restriction of critical charts.

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

(Equivariant analogue of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.23]
)
Consider a
T
-equivariant closed immersion
\Phi:(R_{1},U_{1},f_{1},i_{1})\to(R_{2},U_{2},f_{2},i_{2})
of
T
-equivariant affine étale critical charts on a
T
-equivariant
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s)
. Given a
T
-fixed point
x\in R_{1}=R_{2}
, there is a commutative diagram of
T
-equivariant critical charts:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{1},f^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1}\times E,f_{1}\boxplus q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{1},f_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(U_{2},f_{2})}

with a
T
-fixed point
x^{\prime}
in
R^{\prime}_{1}=R^{\prime}_{2}
over
x
, where dashed arrows denotes
T
-equivariant closed embeddings, dotted arrows stabilization by
T
-equivariant quadratic forms, and plain arrows
T
-equivariant étale restriction of critical charts.

Proof.

We begin by adapting the arguments of the proof of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.23]
to the
T
-equivariant setting. Because
T
is a torus, as proven in the proof of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.44]
, one can, up to restricting
U_{2}
to an affine
T
-invariant distinguished open neighborhood
\tilde{U}_{2}
of
\Phi(x)
(and denote
\tilde{U}_{1}=\Phi^{-1}(\tilde{U}_{2})
), find a
T
-equivariant étale map
\tilde{\gamma}\times\tilde{\beta}:\tilde{U}_{2}\to E^{\prime}\times E
where
E^{\prime},E
are affine spaces with linear
T
-action, such that
\tilde{U}_{1}=\tilde{\beta}^{-1}(0)
, and then
\tilde{\gamma}\circ\Phi:\tilde{U}_{1}\to E^{\prime}
is étale, and there is a
T
-fixed point over
x
. Then we define
\check{j}:\check{U}_{2}\to U_{2}
to be the
T
-equivariant étale neighborhood of
i_{2}(x)
in
\tilde{U}_{2}
given by
(\tilde{U}_{1}\times E^{\prime})\times_{E\times E^{\prime}}\tilde{U}_{2}
, which is then still affine. We denote by
\check{\alpha}:\check{U}_{2}\to U_{1}
the composition:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\check{U}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{U}_{1}\times E^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{U}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}

and
\check{\beta}:\check{U}_{2}\to E\times E^{\prime}\to E
: then
\check{\alpha}\times\check{\beta}:\check{U}_{2}\to U_{1}\times E
is étale. We denote
\check{f}_{1}:=f_{1}\circ\alpha:\check{U}_{2}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
,
\check{f}_{2}:=f_{2}\circ\check{j}:\check{U}_{2}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, and
\check{h}:=\check{f}_{2}-\check{f}_{1}:\check{U}_{2}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
, which is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi
by construction. Denote by
(\check{z}_{1},...,\check{z}_{n})
the étale coordinates coming from
\check{\beta}:\check{U}_{2}\to E={\rm Spec}(V)
: as
T
is a torus, we can assume that each
\check{z}_{b}
corresponds to a one dimensional representation of
T
of character
\chi_{b}
. As shown in the proof of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.23]
,
\check{h}
lies in the ideal
(\check{z}_{1},...,\check{z}_{n})^{2}
. Then, we can write
\check{h}=\sum_{b,c}Q^{b,c}\check{z}_{b}\check{z}_{c}
, where
Q^{b,c}=Q^{c,b}:\check{V}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
has weight
\chi-\chi_{b}-\chi_{c}
. Ax
x
is
T
-fixed,
q^{b,c}=Q^{b,c}(x)
defines a
T
-equivariant quadratic form on
V
of weight
\chi
. We have then a
T
-equivariant quadratic form
q
of
V
of weight
\chi
, which is non-degenerate by the argument below
[
Joy13
, equation 5.10]
: we restrict to the open subset where
Q
is still non-degenerate, which is automatically affine and
T
-invariant.

We cannot adapt directly the end of the proof of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.23]
, because the ’Gram Schmidt algorithm’ used here to diagonalize the quadratic form breaks the
T
-equivariance. Denote by
V=\bigoplus_{\psi}V_{\psi}
the weight decomposition of
V
, where the sum is over the characters
\psi:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Notice that
q
induces a non-degenerate pairing between
V_{\psi}
and
V_{\chi-\psi}
: for each couple
\{\psi,\chi-\psi\}
, with
\psi\neq\chi/2
, we choose a representative, giving a set of cocharacter
\Lambda
, such that
V=\bigoplus_{\psi\in\Lambda}(V_{\psi}\oplus V_{\chi-\psi})\oplus V_{\chi/2}
where the last term can be empty (in particular, if
\chi
is not the square of a character). For each
\psi\in\Lambda
, we consider the étale coordinates
x_{b}:=\check{z}_{b}
of weight
\psi
, and replace the étale coordinates
\check{z}_{b}
of weight
\chi-\psi
by the étale coordinates
y_{b}:=\sum_{c}(Q^{bc})^{-1}x_{b}
, and keep unchanged the étale coordinates
\check{z}_{c}
of weight
\chi/2
. We obtain then
h=\sum_{b}x_{b}y_{b}+\sum_{c,d}Q^{cd}\check{z}_{c}\check{z}_{d}
, where the last terms contains only coordinates of weight
\chi/2
(notice that, if
\chi
is not divisible by
2
, there are no such coordinates), and the
Q^{cd}
are
T
-invariant.

We can then applies the procedure of
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.23, 2.24]
to the étale coordinates
\check{z}_{c}
of weight
\chi/2
, because the formulas involves elements of the same weight. This allows, up to taking an étale
T
-equivariant cover
j^{\prime}:U^{\prime}_{2}\to\check{U}_{2}
(in order to take square roots of the
T
-invariant functions
Q^{cc}
), to obtain new étale coordinates
z_{c}
of weight
-\chi/2
replacing the
\check{z}_{c}
, such that
h\circ j^{\prime}=q\circ\beta=\sum_{b}x_{b}y_{b}+\sum_{c}(z_{c})^{2}
, and that there is a
T
-fixed point over
x
. Considering
\alpha,\beta,f^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{1}
obtained by composing
\check{\alpha},\check{\beta},\check{f}_{2},\check{f}_{1}
with
j^{\prime}
, and
U^{\prime}_{1}:=\beta^{-1}(0)
, with an
T
-equivariant étale map to
U_{1}
, one obtains the
T
-equivariant diagram of the Lemma.
∎

If
s
is
T
-invariant, i.e.
\chi=0
, one obtains by definition of
\mathcal{S}_{[X/T]}
that
s
descends to a d-critical structure
\bar{s}
on
[X/T]
: in particular, we obtain an isomorphism:

(6.20)

\displaystyle K_{X,s}\simeq K_{[X/T],\bar{s}}|_{X^{red}}\otimes K_{X/[X/T]}|_{X^{red}}^{\otimes 2}

which gives a
T
-equivariant structure on
K_{X,s}
. For future reference, it will be useful to notice that one can define also such an equivariant structure for
\chi\neq 0
:

Corollary 6.7
.

Consider an algebraic space
X
with a torus action
\mu:T\times X\to X
, with a
T
-equivariant
d
-critical structure
s
of weight
\chi:T\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
. Then
K_{X,s}
enhance naturally to a
T
-equivariant line bundle, such that, on a
T
-equivariant étale affine critical chart
(R,U,f,i)
, one has a canonical isomorphism
K_{X,s}|_{R^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{U}^{\otimes 2})|_{R^{red}}\otimes\chi^{-\dim(U)}
upgrading the one of Lemma
5.5

i)
.

Proof.

From Proposition
6.5

i)
, one obtains local definition of the equivariant structure on
K_{X,s}
on an étale cover of
X^{red}
. As
K_{X,s}
is a line bundle on a reduced algebraic space, it suffices to show that the equivariant structure at the stalk of each point is independent of the choice of
T
-equivariant critical chart. Given
x\in R(k)
, we have the isomorphism:

(6.21)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{x}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{U}^{\otimes 2})|_{x}\simeq K_{R}^{\otimes 2}|_{x}\simeq K_{X}^{\otimes 2}|_{x}

where the second isomorphism comes from
\det(hess_{f})
: as
f
, and then
hess_{f}
, is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi
,
\det(hess_{f})
is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi^{\dim(U)-\dim(R)}
, such that we have a
T
-equivariant isomorphism:

(6.22)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{R^{red}}|_{x}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{U}^{\otimes 2})|_{x}\otimes\chi^{-\dim(U)}\simeq K_{R}^{\otimes 2}|_{x}\otimes\chi^{-\dim(R)}\simeq K_{X}^{\otimes 2}|_{x}\otimes\chi^{-\dim(X)}

where the right hand side is independent of the critical chart, hence the
T
-equivariant structures defined above glue.
∎

Proposition 6.8
.

Consider a quasi-separated d-critical Artin stack
(\mathcal{X},s)
, with affine stabilizer, locally of finite type over
k
.

i)

\mathcal{X}
is covered by critical charts of the form
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, such that the smooth maps
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
are jointly surjective.

ii)

Given
(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},i_{i})
,
i=1,2
two critical charts, there is a family of diagrams of critical charts:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{U},f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1},f^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{1}}(\mathcal{E}_{1}),f^{\prime}_{1}\circ\pi_{1}+q_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{2}}(\mathcal{E}_{2}),f^{\prime}_{2}\circ\pi_{2}+q_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{U}^{\prime}_{2},f^{\prime}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{U}_{1},f_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathcal{U}_{2},f_{2})}

where the dotted arrows corresponds to stabilization by quadratic bundles, and the plain arrows are smooth morphisms of critical charts, such that the
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}_{1})\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}_{2})
are jointly surjective.

Proof.

i)

Using Halpern-Leistner’s Lemma
3.3
, one has a smooth cover of
\mathcal{X}
by quotients
[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
of affine scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action such that the
{\rm Grad}([X/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
cover
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
. Then
X
inherits a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant
d
-critical structure (i.e. , with weight
\chi=0
). Using
[
Joy13
, Proposition 2.43]
(Proposition
6.5
i) here), one can find
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant critical charts
(R,U,f,i)
covering
X
for the Zariski topology (with
f

\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant), which gives critical charts
([R/\mathbb{G}_{m}],[U/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f,i)
covering
[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
for the Zariski topology, such that; from
[
HL14
, Corollary 1.1.7]
, the
({\rm Grad}([R/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
are covering
({\rm Grad}([X/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
, i.e. also
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
, hence we are done.

ii)

Using Halpern-Leistner’s Lemma
3.3
applied to
\mathcal{U}_{1},\mathcal{U}_{2}
, one can find smooth restrictions of critical charts
([R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],[U_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f_{i},i_{i})\to(\mathcal{R}_{i},\mathcal{U}_{i},f_{i},s_{i})
with
U_{i}
affine covering
\mathcal{U}_{i}
such that the
({\rm Grad}([R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\to\mathcal{R}_{i}
are jointly surjective, hence we can suppose that the two charts are of the form
([R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],[U_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f_{i},i_{i})
with
U_{i}
affine. Apply now Halpern-Leistner’s Lemma
3.3
to
[R_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\times_{\mathcal{X}}[R_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
to cover it by some
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
such that the
{\rm Grad}([\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
cover
{\rm Grad}([R_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}{\rm Grad}([R_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
.

Consider
(y_{1},y_{2})\in{\rm Grad}([R_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}{\rm Grad}([R_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])(k)
, and take a point
\tilde{y}\in{\rm Grad}([\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])(k)
over
(y_{1},y_{2})
. One can replace the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
R_{i},\tilde{R}
by some power of it, such that
y_{i},\tilde{y}
corresponds to the cocharacter
1
(this leads us to replace the critical charts by an étale cover). It means in particular that the points
x_{i}:=\iota(y_{i})\in[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}](k),\tilde{x}:=\iota(\tilde{y})\in[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}](k)
underlying
y_{i},\tilde{y}
have stabilizer
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
, and that the smooth morphism
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induces an isomorphism of stabilizers at
\tilde{x}\to x_{i}
. Notice that these morphisms do not have to come from a
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
-equivariant morphism
\tilde{R}\to R_{i}
, but we can argue as in the proof of
[
AJ24
, Theorem 1.13]
. Namely, the fact that
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
comes from a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant morphism
\tilde{R}\to R_{i}
is equivalent to say that the following square is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}

denote by
p,q:[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
the two compositions morphisms, which corresponds to two
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-torsors
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{G}
on
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
. Notice that, because
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
induces an isomorphism of stabilizer at
\tilde{x}
, there is an isomorphism
p\simeq q
over the gerbe of
\tilde{x}
, which gives a section at the gerbe of
\tilde{y}
of the stack of isomorphisms between the torsors
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
. But the latter is a smooth and affine stack (because
\mathbb{G}_{m}
is itself smooth and affine) over
[\tilde{R}/G]
: From
[
AHR19
, Theorem 7.18]
, there is then an extension of this section étale locally near
\tilde{x}
. In other term, up to precomposing by an étale map, we can assume that
[\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[R_{i}/G]
comes from a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
\tilde{R}\to R_{i}
. Applying this procedure for
i=1
and further for
i=2
, and shrinking
\tilde{R}
étale locally near
\tilde{x}
, we have obtained an affine
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant scheme
\tilde{R}
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant smooth maps
\tilde{R}\to R_{i}
such that, considering the smooth morphisms
{\rm Grad}([\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\to{\rm Grad}([R_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}{\rm Grad}([R_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
, there is a point
\tilde{y}
over
(y_{1},y_{2})
, with image
\tilde{x}\in R_{1}
. We will use repeatedly below the fact that the lift of a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed point along a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
Y\to Z
is automatically
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed, and then gives a lift of the corresponding point along
{\rm Grad}(Y)\to{\rm Grad}(Z)
.

We can now use the smooth version of the torus-equivariant analog of
[
Sta18
, Tag 04B1]
discussed in the proof of Proposition
6.5
above, giving smooth
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant maps
\check{R}:=\check{R}_{1}\cap\check{R}_{2}\to R_{i}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-fixed point
\check{x}
lying over the
x_{i}
, such that the two composed maps
[\check{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[R_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to\mathcal{X}
are isomorphic, and
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant closed embeddings into smooth schemes
\check{R}\to\check{U}_{i}
over
R_{i}\to U_{i}
. Now, consider the critical charts
([\check{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],[\check{U}_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\check{f}_{i},\check{i}_{i})
of
(\mathcal{X},s)
induced by smooth restriction along
[\check{U}_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to[U_{i}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
: these are two critical charts for the
d
-critical structure induced on
[\check{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
by pullback from those of
\mathcal{X}
. In other terms,
(\check{R},\check{U}_{i},\check{f}_{i},\check{i}_{i})
are two
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant critical charts for the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant (i.e. , with weight
0
)
d
-critical structure on
\check{R}
. Applying Proposition
6.5

ii)
, one finds the following diagram of critical charts of
\mathcal{X}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U^{\prime}_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f^{\prime}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U^{\prime}_{1}\times E_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f^{\prime}_{1}\circ\pi_{1}+q_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U^{\prime}_{2}\times E_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f^{\prime}_{2}\circ\pi_{2}+q_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U^{\prime}_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f^{\prime}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([\check{U}_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\check{f}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([\check{U}_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\check{f}_{2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U_{1}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces([U_{2}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],f_{2})}

with a point
x\in[R/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
lying over
\check{x}
, hence over
\tilde{x}
, where the vertical arrows from the first to the second line, and from the second line to the third line, are étale and representable. Hence, from the above remark there is a point
y\in{\rm Grad}([R/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
over
\tilde{y}\in{\rm Grad}([\tilde{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
, hence over
(y_{1},y_{2})\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}_{1})\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}_{2})
. Notice that the dotted arrows give a stabilization by quadratic bundle.

∎

6.2.2.
Shifted symplectic version

We begin by discussing
T
-equivariant
-1
-shifted symplectic structures. Consider a derived algebraic space
\underline{X}
with the action of a torus
T
. We follow the discussion of
[
Cal16
, Remark 1.4, Section 2.1.1]
. From
[
CPT+15
, Remark 2.4.8]
, there is a relative version of the De Rahm complex, such that for any morphism of derived stack
\underline{\mathcal{X}}\to\underline{\mathcal{Y}}
,
\mathbf{DR}(\underline{\mathcal{X}}/\underline{\mathcal{Y}})
forms global section of a sheaf of mixed graded complexes
\mathcal{DR}(\underline{\mathcal{X}}/\underline{\mathcal{Y}})
on
\underline{\mathcal{Y}}
. In particular,
\mathcal{DR}([\underline{X}/T]/BT)
is a sheaf of mixed graded complex on
BT
, whose pullback along
{\rm Spec}(k)\to BT
is
\mathbf{DR}(\underline{X})
: it gives then an extra
\Gamma:={\rm Hom}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
-grading on the mixed graded complex
\mathcal{DR}(\underline{X})
of forms: we will then talk about forms of weight
\chi\in\mathcal{DR}(\underline{X})
. Applying the functor
NC^{w}
of
[
PTVV13
, Section 1.1]
, we obtain a
\Gamma
-grading on the complex
NC^{w}(\underline{X})
of closed forms. From the functoriality of the relative De Rahm complex, we obtain directly that pullbacks along
T
-equivariant morphisms respect this grading.

We define then a
-1
-shifted symplectic structure on
\underline{X}
of weight
\chi
to be a nondegenerate closed
2
-form
\omega
of degree
-1
in the weight
\chi
part of
NC^{w}(\underline{X})
. Given a
-1
-shifted symplectic structure on
\underline{X}

\omega
, putting an equivariant structure of weight
\chi
on it amount to give an isomorphism
\omega\sim\omega^{\chi}
, with
\omega^{\chi}
with grading
\chi
. Given two such choice, the isomorphism
\omega^{\chi}\sim\omega(\omega^{\chi})^{\prime}
does not have to have grading
\chi
, hence this is really a structure, and not a property. To avoid confusion, we denote by
\sim_{\chi}
a homotopy in the
\chi
-graded part. Putting an equivariant structure of weight
0
on
\omega
amount to descend it to a closed
2
-form of degree
-1
on
[\underline{X}/T]
(which cannot be nondegenerate from the inspection of the cotangent complex). We quickly check that the Darboux theorem
[
BBJ19
, Theorem 5.18]
admits a
T
-equivariant version:

Lemma 6.9
.

Consider a
T
-equivariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic space
(\underline{X},\omega)
of weight
\chi\in{\rm Hom}(T,\mathbb{G}_{m})
and a point
x\in X(k)=\underline{X}(k)
. There is a smooth affine scheme
U
with
T
-action, a
T
-equivariant function of weight
\chi

f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and a
T
-equivariant étale map
\phi:\underline{R}:=\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}\to\underline{X}
over
x
such that
f|_{R^{red}}=0
and
\phi^{\ast}(\omega)\sim_{\chi}\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}}
. In particular, its classical truncation
(X,s)
is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi
,
(R,U,f,i)
is a
T
-equivariant étale critical chart, and the isomorphism
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})|_{X^{red}}\simeq K_{X,s}
respects the
T
-equivariant structure from Corollary
6.7
.

Proof.

From
[
AHR20
, Theorem 4.1]
, we have at the level of the classical truncation a
T
-equivariant étale pointed map
(X^{\prime},x^{\prime})\to(X,x)
, which gives an étale map
[X^{\prime}/T]\to[X/T]
over
BT
. By descent from
[
GR17
, Lemma 2.1.5]
, there is a unique étale map
\underline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}\to[\underline{X}/T]
over
BT
extending this map. By denoting
\underline{X}^{\prime}
the pullback of
\underline{\mathcal{X}}^{\prime}
along
{\rm Spec}(k)\to BT
, one obtains that
\underline{X}^{\prime}
is affine, as its classical truncation
X^{\prime}
is affine, and it has a
T
-action such that
\underline{X}^{\prime}\to\underline{X}
is
T
-equivariant. Replacing
(\underline{X},\omega)
by
(\underline{X}^{\prime},\omega|_{\underline{X}^{\prime}})
, we can then assume that
\underline{X}=\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
is an affine derived scheme with
T
-action, i.e.
\underline{R}
is a
\Gamma
-graded cdga of finite presentation over
k
.

We can now do exactly the same operations than in
[
BBJ19
, Theorem 4.1]
, but in a way consistent with the
\mathbb{Z}
-grading of
\underline{R}
: then, up to replacing
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
by a
T
-invariant Zariski open neighborhood of
x
, we can assume that
\underline{R}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded standard form cdga, giving a minimal standard neighborhood of
x
. Then
\underline{R}(0)
is smooth of dimension
m
, and
\underline{R}
is freely generated over
\underline{R}(0)
by
m
homogeneous generators in degree
-1
. Moreover, as pullbacks by
T
-equivariant map preserves the grading,
\underline{\phi}^{\ast}(\omega)
enhance to a
T
-equivariant closed
2
-form of degree
-1
and weight
\chi
on
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
. The periodic cyclic complex and cyclic complex of
[
BBJ19
, Definition 5.5]
, and then their cohomology, admits an obvious
\Gamma
-graded enhancement, such that the exact sequence
[
BBJ19
, Proposition 5.6 a)]
respects this
\Gamma
-grading. The isomorphism
[
BBJ19
, Proposition 5.6 b)]
admits an obvious
\Gamma
-graded enhancement, such that
HP^{-4}(\underline{R})(2)
has obviously weight
0
. We can then argue as in
[
BBJ19
, Proposition 5.7 a)]
, taking a lift of
\omega
to
HC^{-2}(\underline{R})(1)
of weight
\chi
, giving
\Phi\in\underline{R}^{0}=\underline{R}(0)
and
\phi\in(\Omega^{1}_{\underline{R}})^{-1}
of weight
\chi
, such that
d\boldsymbol{\Phi}=0
,
d_{dR}\boldsymbol{\Phi}+d\Phi=0
and
\omega\sim_{\chi}(d_{dR}\Phi,0,0,...)
. We can adapt the arguments of
[
BBJ19
, Proposition 5.7 b)]
: if
\chi\neq 0
, obviously
\Phi_{X^{red}}=0
as it is constant on
X^{red}
, and, if
\chi=0
, one can adjust the value of
\Phi_{X^{red}}
to be
0
by adding an element of
HP^{-4}(\underline{R})(2)
, which automatically has weight
0
. We can now argue as in
[
BBBBJ15
, Section 5.6, Step 1]
: in
[
BBBBJ15
, Diagram 5.30]
, the morphism
(V^{-1})^{\ast}\to V^{0}
induced by
d_{dR}\Phi
has
\Gamma
-weight
\chi
, hence the open subset where it is an isomorphism is
T
-invariant, hence we can localize around
x
.

We can now argue as in
[
BBBBJ15
, Section 5.6, Step 2]
: we localize at a
T
-invariant open neighborhood of
x
, and take homogeneous étale coordinates
x_{1},...,x_{m}
for
\underline{R}(0)
. We can then choose generators
y_{1},...,y_{m}
in degree
-1
, homogeneous of
\Gamma
-weights opposite to those of the
x_{i}
, such that the
d_{dR}y_{i}
forms the dual basis of the
d_{dR}x_{i}
under
(V^{-1})^{\ast}\simeq V^{0}
. As
\boldsymbol{\Phi}
and
\Phi
have
\Gamma
-weight
\chi
, one checks directly that all the operations of
[
BBBBJ15
, Section 5.6, Step 2]
respects the
\Gamma
-grading (notice that the discussion on the master equation is irrelevant for
k=-1
). We obtain then a version with homogeneous coordinates of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 5.18 a)]
. Then, consider the smooth scheme with
T
-action
U:={\rm Spec}(\underline{R}(0))
, the
T
-invariant function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
corresponding to the element
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\in\underline{B}^{0}
of
\Gamma
-weight
\chi
. We obtain that
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})=\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)
as a derived scheme with
T
-action, and that
\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}}\sim_{\chi}\omega|_{\underline{R}}
.

From
[
BBJ19
, Theorem 6.6]
, for such a data,
(R,U,f,i)
forms a critical chart for
(X,s)
. Then
(R\times T,U\times T,f\circ\mu,i)
(resp.
(R\times T,U\times T,f\circ pr_{2},i)
) give a critical chart for
(X\times T,\mu^{\star}(s))
(resp.
(X\times T,(pr_{2})^{\star}(s))
, and, as
f\circ\mu=\chi\cdot f\circ pr_{2}
, we have that
(\mu^{\star}(s))|_{R\times T}=\chi\cdot(pr_{2})^{\star}(s)|_{R\times T}
. As such critical charts cover
R\times T
, we have
(\mu^{\star}(s))=\chi\cdot(pr_{2})^{\star}(s)
, and then
s
is
T
-equivariant of weight
\chi
. On such a critical chart, the isomorphism
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})|_{R^{red}}\simeq K_{X,s}|_{R^{red}}
is given in
[
BBJ19
, Theorem 6.6]
by the isomorphism:

(6.23)

\displaystyle\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}})|_{R^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{U}^{\otimes 2})|_{R^{red}}

obtained by writing
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}}
as
T_{U}|_{R}\overset{hess_{f}}{\to}T^{\ast}U
.
f
, and then
hess_{f}
, have weight
\chi
, so, as a
T
-equivariant complex,
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}}
can be written as
T_{U}\otimes\chi|_{R}\overset{hess_{f}}{\to}T^{\ast}U
, which gives an isomorphism:

(6.24)

\displaystyle\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}})|_{R^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}(K_{U}^{\otimes 2})|_{R^{red}}\otimes\chi^{-n}

giving that
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})|_{R^{red}}\simeq K_{X,s}|_{R^{red}}
is
T
-equivariant, hence
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})|_{X^{red}}\simeq K_{X,s}
is
T
-equivariant.
∎

The following will be the key point to compare the
{\rm Grad}
construction at the
-1
-shifted symplectic level and at the
d
-critical level:

Lemma 6.10
.

Consider a
-1
-shifted symplectic stack
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega)
and a point
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})(k)
. There is a smooth map
\bar{\underline{\phi}}:([\underline{R}/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}],y)\to(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\iota(x))
, with
\underline{R}
a derived scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
-action restricting to the map
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
classified by
x
, a smooth scheme
U
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action and a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant functions
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}
and a map
\underline{j}:\underline{R}\to\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)
inducing an isomorphism on the classical truncation, such that
f|_{R^{red}}=0
and, denoting
\underline{\phi}:\underline{R}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
,
\underline{j}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}})\sim\underline{\phi}^{\ast}(\omega)
.

Proof.

We will adapt the arguments of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 2.10, Theorem 3.18]
. Consider a point
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})(k)
, corresponding to a point
\iota(x)\in\mathcal{X}(k)
, and a cocharacter
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to G_{\iota(x)}
. Applying
[
AHLHR22
, Theorem 1.13]
to the smooth map
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to BG_{\iota(x)}
, where
BG_{\iota(x)}
is considered as a closed substack of
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, we obtain a smooth pointed map
\bar{\underline{\phi}}:([\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}],y)\to(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\iota(x))
, where
\underline{R}
is a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded cdga, and
\phi^{-1}(BG_{\iota(x)})=B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
. Then
\underline{\phi}:\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
is smooth of dimension
\dim(G_{\iota(x)}):=n
, hence
\dim(T_{\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R}),y})=\dim(T_{\underline{\mathcal{X}},\iota(x)}):=m
. We consider
\underline{\phi}^{\ast}(\omega)
, which is obtained by pullback from
\bar{\underline{\phi}}^{\ast}(\omega)
, and give then a closed
2
-form of degree
-1
and weight
0
on
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
.

As in the proof of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 2.10 a)]
, we follow closely the proof of Lemma
6.9
, by first replacing
\underline{R}
by a standard form
\mathbb{Z}
-graded cdga, but where we have
n
homogeneous generators
w_{i}
in degree
-2
that we fix. We follow then the operations of Lemma
6.9
, with which the generators
w_{i}
of degree
-2
does not interfere. We obtain then a version with homogeneous coordinates of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 2.10 a)]
. As in
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 5.18 b)]
, we denote by
\underline{B}\hookrightarrow\underline{R}
the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded sub-cdga generated by the
x_{i}
and the
y_{i}
, and consider its dual
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})\to\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{B})
, which is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. Then, consider the smooth scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action
U:={\rm Spec}(\underline{B}(0))
, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
corresponding to the element
\boldsymbol{\Phi}\in\underline{B}^{0}
of
\mathbb{Z}
-weight
0
. We obtain that
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{B})=\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)
as a derived scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, and that
\underline{j}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}(f)}})\sim\underline{\phi}^{\ast}(\omega)
. By the construction of
\Phi
, we have also
f_{R^{red}}=0
.
∎

6.3.
Hyperbolic localization on d-critical stacks

6.3.1.
D-critical stacks and graded points

Consider a stack
\mathcal{X}
. For a point
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})(k)
, corresponding to a map
g:B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to\mathcal{X}
, consider the Zariski tangent space
T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)}
, and the Lie algebra
\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})
of the stabilizer group at
\iota(x)
. Both are obtained from the first cohomologies of
g^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{X}}
, which provides them a
\mathbb{Z}
-grading. Denote:

(6.25)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}(x):=-\dim(\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})^{>0})+\dim((T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)})^{>0})-\dim((T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)})^{<0})+\dim(\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})^{<0})

When
\mathcal{X}
possesses an enhancement to a
-1
-shifted symplectic stack
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega)
,
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}(x)
gives the signed count of negative weights in the tangent complex
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{>0}
, see Lemma
6.13

ii)
.
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}
can be thought as the virtual dimension of the fibers of the strata of
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X})
flowing to a connected component of
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
. We obtain directly that, for two stacks
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
, one has
Ind_{\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}}=Ind_{\mathcal{X}}+Ind_{\mathcal{Y}}
. For a smooth morphism
\phi:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
, the exact triangle of cotangent complexes gives an exact sequence of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded vector spaces:

(6.26)

\displaystyle 0\to\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y})\to\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathfrak{Iso}_{\phi(\iota(x))}(\mathcal{Y})\to T_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y},\iota(x)}\to T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)}\to T_{\mathcal{Y},\phi(\iota(x))}\to 0

which gives the formula
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{Y}}\circ\phi+{\rm Ind}_{\phi}
.

Proposition 6.11
.

Consider
(\mathcal{X},s)
a d-critical stack which is quasi-separated, locally of finite type, with reductive stabilizers and separated diagonal.

i)

Denoting:

(6.27)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(s):=\iota^{\star}(s)\in H^{0}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),\mathcal{S}^{0}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})})

(recalling that
\iota:{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathcal{X}
is the natural morphism which ’forgets the gradation’),
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
is a d-critical stack. A natural system of critical charts covering
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
is given by the critical charts:

(6.28)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i):=({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f),{\rm Grad}(i))

for any critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
of
(\mathcal{X},s)
. Then
{\rm Grad}
enhance to a symmetric monoidal endofunctor of the 2-category of d-critical stacks (with smooth morphisms).

ii)

Ind_{\mathcal{X}}:{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})\to\mathbb{Z}
is a locally constant function such that, for each critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
of
(\mathcal{X},s)
, we have
Ind_{\mathcal{X}}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}
.

iii)

Given a d-critical stack
(\mathcal{X},s)
, there is a canonical square root
G_{\mathcal{X},s}
of
\iota^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{X},s}\otimes K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}^{\otimes-1}
, which allows to define an orientation
K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}^{1/2}
of
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
from an orientation
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}
of (
\mathcal{X},s)
by the formula:

(6.29)

\displaystyle K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}^{1/2}:=\iota^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{X},s}\otimes G_{\mathcal{X},s}^{\otimes-1}

Then
{\rm Grad}
enhance to a symmetric monoidal endofunctor of the 2-category of oriented d-critical stacks (with smooth morphisms). With this choice of orientation, for each critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, there is a natural isomorphism:

(6.30)

\displaystyle\iota^{\ast}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}\simeq Q_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}

which is compatible with smooth restriction and exterior product of critical charts. Given a stabilization by a quadratic form, the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\ast}(Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i)}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{(\mathcal{E},q)}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}})}

Q_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i))}

\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}/2\mathbb{Z}}P_{({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{E})^{0},{\rm Grad}(q)^{0})}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})^{red}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota^{\ast}Q_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i))}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the horizontal isomorphisms comes from (
5.8
) and (
6.15
).

Proof.

i)

Consider a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
of
(\mathcal{X},s)
, and the diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{R}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{U}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(f)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}

From the defining property of
\iota^{\star}
given in Definition
5.1
,
{\rm Grad}(s)|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}={\rm Grad}(f)+I_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}^{2}
, and, from Lemma
6.1
,
{\rm Crit}({\rm Grad}(f))={\rm Grad}(R)
, hence
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f),{\rm Grad}(i))
is a critical chart for
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
. Using Proposition
6.8

i)
, such charts cover
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
, hence
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
is a d-critical stack. Notice that the definition of
{\rm Grad}(s)
is obviously functorial with smooth morphisms and symmetric monoidal, as this is the case for
f\mapsto f^{\star}
.

ii)

Consider a critical chart of
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
of the form
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}),{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U}),{\rm Grad}(f),{\rm Grad}(i))
. As
\mathcal{R}\to\mathcal{X}
is smooth, we have proven above that
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{R}}-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}
. But, given
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})(k)
, and the underlying point
\iota(x)\in\mathcal{R}(k)
, the Hessian of
f
gives a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant quadratic form on
T_{\mathcal{U},\iota(x)}
, whose kernel is
T_{\mathcal{R},x^{\prime}}
. We obtain then:

(6.31)

\displaystyle\dim((T_{\mathcal{R},\iota(x)})^{>0})-\dim((T_{\mathcal{R},\iota(x)})^{<0})=\dim((T_{\mathcal{U},\iota(x)})^{>0})-\dim((T_{\mathcal{U},\iota(x)})^{<0})

As moreover
i
is a closed immersion, we have
\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{U})=\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{R})
, which gives
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{R}}(x)={\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{U},f}(x)
, inducing the result. In particular,
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})}
is locally constant. As
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
is covered by critical charts of
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
of the form
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i))
,
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}
is then locally constant on
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
.

iii)

For a smooth map
\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
and a subset
I
of
\mathbb{Z}
, we consider the line bundle on
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}
:

(6.32)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}}^{I}:=\det((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}/\mathcal{Y}})^{I})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}

Considering a critical chart
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
, we have:

\displaystyle((\iota_{\mathcal{X}^{red}})^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{X},s})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})^{red}}\simeq

\displaystyle(\iota_{\mathcal{R}^{red}})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(\iota_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}(i^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\otimes 2})\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\otimes-2})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}(\det((\iota_{\mathcal{U}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}})^{\otimes 2})\otimes\det((\iota_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})^{\otimes-2})

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}(\det(((\iota_{\mathcal{U}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}})^{0})^{\otimes 2})\otimes\det(((\iota_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})^{0})^{\otimes-2})

\displaystyle\otimes\Bigl({\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}\det((\iota_{\mathcal{U}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{U}})^{\neq 0}\otimes\det(((\iota_{\mathcal{R}})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}})^{\neq 0})^{\otimes-1}\Bigr)^{\otimes 2}

(6.33)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})^{red}}\otimes\Bigl({\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\neq 0}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\neq 0})^{\otimes-1}\Bigr)^{\otimes 2}

(where, by a slight abuse of notation, we have omitted the restrictions to the reduced locus except from the first and last line for readability).
We define then the line bundle on
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})^{red}
:

(6.34)

\displaystyle G_{(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)}:={\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\neq 0}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\neq 0})^{\otimes-1}

and we will glue these into a line bundle

on

, and glue the above isomorphisms into an isomorphism:

(6.35)

\displaystyle\iota^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{X},s}\simeq K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}\otimes G_{\mathcal{X},s}^{\otimes 2}

Given a smooth restriction of critical charts

\displaystyle:={\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{U}}^{\neq 0}|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\neq 0}|^{\otimes-1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Grad}(i^{\prime})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}}^{\neq 0}\otimes(K_{\mathcal{U}^{\prime}/\mathcal{U}}^{\neq 0})^{\otimes-1})\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\neq 0}|^{\otimes-1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{\prime})^{red}}

Given an orientation

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}):=\iota^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}\otimes G_{\mathcal{X},s}^{\otimes-1}

with the isomorphism
({\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}))^{\otimes 2}\simeq K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s)}
obtained from (
6.35
). Then (
6.43
) and (
6.45
) allows to upgrade:

(6.47)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})\to({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s),{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}))

to a symmetric monoidal endofunctor of the 2-category of oriented d-critical Artin stacks with smooth morphisms.

Given an isomorphism
K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}|_{\mathcal{R}^{red}}\simeq i^{\ast}K_{\mathcal{U}}\otimes K_{\mathcal{R}/\mathcal{X}}^{\otimes-1}
, one builds from (

⁢
i
i
i

)

) an isomorphism

(6.48)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R}^{red})}\simeq{\rm Grad}(i)^{\ast}K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{U})}\otimes K_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{R})/{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}^{\otimes-1}

which provides the natural isomorphism (
6.30
). Its compatibility with smooth restriction and exterior products of critical charts is obvious from the definitions. Considering a stabilization by a quadratic form, the isomorphism (

⁢
i
i
i

)

) used to glue
G_{\mathcal{X},s}
, hence giving the difference between the isomorphisms (
6.30
) for
(\mathcal{R},\mathcal{U},f,i)
and
(\mathcal{R},\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E}),f\circ\pi+q,s\circ i)
, is built using the perfect pairing between
K_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}^{<0}
and
K_{\mathbb{V}_{\mathcal{U}}(\mathcal{E})}^{>0}
induced by
\det(q)
, exactly as (
6.15
), hence the square of the proposition commutes as claimed.

∎

6.3.2.
Shifted symplectic version

Lemma 6.12
.

i)

Given a
-1
shifted symplectic stack
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
, the closed
2
form of degree
-1

{\rm Grad}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}):=\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
is nondegenerate, with pairing

(6.49)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}[-1]

obtained by applying
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}-)^{0}
to the nondegenerate pairing of
2
-form of degree
1
underlying
\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}
:

(6.50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}[-1]

i.e. it gives a natural
-1
-shifted symplectic structure on
\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})
. Equivalently,
{\rm Grad}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
can be obtained by applying the mapping construction
[
PTVV13
, Theorem 2.5]
using the natural
\mathcal{O}
-orientation of degree
0
(in the sense of
[
PTVV13
, Definition 2.4]
) on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
given by
\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})\simeq k
.

ii)

Given a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
shifted symplectic algebraic space
(\underline{X},\omega_{\underline{X}})
, the closed
2
form of degree
-1

\omega_{\underline{X}}^{0}:=\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
is nondegenerate, with pairing

(6.51)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}^{0}}^{\vee}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}^{0}}[-1]

obtained by applying
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}-)^{0}
to the nondegenerate pairing of
2
-form of degree
1
underlying
\omega_{\underline{X}}
, i.e. it gives a natural
-1
-shifted symplectic structure on
\underline{X}^{0}
.

Proof.

i)

We first define the
\mathcal{O}
-orientation of degree
0
on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
. For any affine derived scheme
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
, consider
B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}:=B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
. Then
{\rm QCoh}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})
is the stable category of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
\underline{R}
-complexes, hence it is obviously strictly
\mathcal{O}
-compact over
\underline{R}
following
[
PTVV13
, Definition 2.1]
, hence
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
is
\mathcal{O}
-compact from the same definition. Under this identification,
\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
is just
k
with
\mathbb{Z}
-grading
0
, and we have an obvious isomorphism
\eta:\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})\simeq k
. For an affine derived scheme
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
and a perfect complex
\mathcal{E}
on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}
, i.e. a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded perfect
\underline{R}
-complex, we have
\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}},E)=E^{0}
. Then the pairing:

(6.52)

\displaystyle\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}},E)\otimes_{A}\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}},E^{\vee})\to\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}},\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}})=\underline{R}

induced by
\eta
is the pairing
E^{0}\otimes_{\underline{R}}(E^{0})^{\vee}\to\underline{R}
, which is obviously nondegenerate. Then
\eta
is an
\mathcal{O}
-orientation of degree
0
(in the sense of
[
PTVV13
, Definition 2.4]
) on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
.

Consider an
\underline{R}
-point
x
of
\underline{Map}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\underline{\mathcal{X}})
, corresponding to a map
f:B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
. Using the identification:

(6.53)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}|_{x}\simeq\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}},f^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})=(f^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{0}

from the description given at the end of the proof of
[
PTVV13
, Theorem 2.5]
, the pairing obtained from
\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
is clearly the one described in the Lemma.

We will now explain why
Map(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})\sim\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
. The former is defined in
[
PTVV13
, Section 2.1]
from the sequence of morphisms (where
NC^{w}
denotes the graded complex of closed forms):

\displaystyle NC^{w}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})\overset{(\underline{ev})^{\ast}}{\to}NC^{w}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})\times B\mathbb{G}_{m,k})

(6.54)

\displaystyle\overset{\kappa_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}}{\to}

\displaystyle NC^{w}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})\times\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})\overset{Id\times\eta}{\to}NC^{w}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})

it suffices then to show that
(Id\times\eta)\circ\kappa_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}
is isomorphic with the pullback along
\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})\to\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})\times B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
. By left Kan extension, it suffices to build a functorial isomorphism for
\underline{\mathcal{X}}=\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
. The map
(Id\times\eta)\circ\kappa_{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}
is build in
[
PTVV13
, Page 30]
(where we denote global section by
\Gamma
) by applying
NC^{-}
to:

\displaystyle\mathbf{DR}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})\simeq\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{DR}(\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}))

(6.55)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})\simeq\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}\Gamma(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})\simeq\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})

where the arrow comes from the natural projection to the component of weight
0
. We interpret now objects on
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
as objects with an extra
\mathbb{Z}
-grading, and recall that
\Gamma
takes the part of homogeneous grading
0
, and pullbacks along
{\rm Spec}(k)\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
forgets the homogeneous grading. Then, following the discussion in
[
PTVV13
, Page 26]
, as
k\simeq k^{\vee}
is the Lie algebra of
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
,
\mathbb{L}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}}\simeq k[-1]
with homogeneous grading
0
, and so
\mathbf{DR}(\mathcal{O}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}})
is simply
{\rm Sym}_{k}^{\ast}k[0]
with homogeneous grading
0
. Then the above map is simply:

(6.56)

\displaystyle\mathbf{DR}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})\simeq\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{DR}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k})\to\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}\mathbf{DR}({\rm Spec}(k))\simeq\mathbf{DR}(\underline{R})

where the arrow comes from the pullback along
{\rm Spec}(k)\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
, which proves the result.

ii)

The data of the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant closed
2
-form
\omega_{\underline{X}}
of degree
-1
is equivalent to the data of a closed
2
-form
\bar{\omega}_{\underline{X}}
of degree
-1
on
[\underline{X}/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}]
, which is degenerate (this explains why
ii
is not just a formal consequence of
i
). Notice that we can apply the mapping construction of
[
PTVV13
, Theorem 2.5]
also to degenerate forms, only the proof of nondegeneracy will not carry on. We obtain then a degenerate
2
-form
Map(\mathbb{G}_{m,k},\bar{\omega}_{\underline{X}})
of degree
-1
on
\underline{{\rm Grad}}([\underline{X}/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}])
, and the arguments above (which do not use nondegeneracy) show that it is equivalent to
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\bar{\omega}_{\underline{X}})
: it gives then by pullback a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant closed
2
-form
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{X}})
on
\underline{X}^{0}
. The computation of the map:

(6.57)

\displaystyle\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}^{\vee}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})}[-1]

induced by
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\bar{\omega}_{\underline{X}})
in the proof of
[
PTVV13
, Theorem 2.5]
carries over in the degenerate case, giving that it is obtain by applying
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}-)^{0}
to the map induced by
\bar{\omega}_{\underline{X}}
: in particular, pulling back to
\underline{X}^{0}
, we obtain that the map induced by
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{X}})
is the one described in the Lemma, in particular it is an isomorphism, i.e.
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{X}})
is a
-1
-shifted symplectic form on
\underline{X}^{0}
.

∎

In particular, by functoriality and symmetric monoidality of the pullback of closed forms, we obtain that
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})\mapsto(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}}),{\rm Grad}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}))
enhances naturally to a symmetric monoidal endofunctor of the
(\infty,1)
-category of
-1
-shifted symplectic stacks, with étale morphisms. Notice that
-1
-shifted symplectic stacks behaves well only with étale morphisms, when d-critical stacks behaves well with smooth morphisms.

If
(\mathcal{X},s)
is the classical truncation of a
-1
-shifted symplectic stack
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
, we reinterpret the constructions of Proposition
6.11
:

Lemma 6.13
.

Consider a
-1
shifted symplectic stack
(\underline{\mathcal{X}},\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})
, with its underlying
d
-critical stack
(\mathcal{X},s)
built from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18]

i)

The
d
-critical stack
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
from Proposition

is the d-critical stack obtained from the

-shifted symplectic stack

using the Darboux theorem
[
BBBBJ15

(and the similar statement holds for
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant

-shifted symplectic algebraic spaces).

ii)

The locally constant function

is the signed dimension of
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{>0}|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}
(and the similar statement holds for
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic spaces).

iii)

Using the nondegenerate pairing induced by
\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\iota^{\ast}\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{\neq 0})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}

(6.58)

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|^{\otimes 2}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}

there is a natural isomorphism between
\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}
and the square root
G_{\mathcal{X},s}
built in Proposition
6.11

iii)
, compatible with étale maps and products of
-1
-shifted symplectic stacks, such that the orientation on
({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}),{\rm Grad}(s))
is given by:

(6.59)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}):=\iota^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}^{\otimes-1}

(and the similar statement holds for
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic spaces)

Proof.

i)

We have seen in the proof of Lemma
6.1
that, given a function
\bar{f}:\mathcal{U}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
on a smooth stack,
\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f))=\underline{{\rm Crit}}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(f))
: in particular, given a
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
-invariant function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
on a smooth scheme with
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
-action
U
, we have
(\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f))^{0}=\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f^{0})
. We will now show that the same formula holds for the
-1
-shifted symplectic structures. Given a function
f
on a smooth space, one obtains two Lagrangian
0,df:U\to T^{\ast}U
, and by definition
\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f):=U\times^{h}_{0,T^{\ast}U,df}U
. According to
[
PTVV13
, Page 25]
, the space of closed forms of degree
0
on
U
is discrete, and is just the classical space of
p
-form. So the usual symplectic structure
\omega_{T^{\ast}U}
on
T^{\ast}U
is a
0
-shifted symplectic structure. One have then a unique choice of isotropic structures
h_{1}:0^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\sim 0
and
h_{2}:(df)^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\sim 0
, such that
0,df
enhance uniquely to
0
-shifted Lagrangian in the sense of
[
PTVV13
, Definition 2.8]
. Then
\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}
is then define in
[
PTVV13
, Corollary 2.11]
by using the construction of
[
PTVV13
, Corollary 2.10]
. Namely,
\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}
is the closed
2
-form of degree
-1
given by:

(6.60)

\displaystyle 0\overset{(\underline{pr_{1}})^{\ast}(h_{1})}{\sim}(\underline{pr_{1}})^{\ast}0^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\sim(\underline{pr_{2}})^{\ast}(df)^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\overset{(\underline{pr_{2}})^{\ast}(h_{2})}{\sim}0

Consider now
f
which is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant as above: one obtains then
\iota^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})=\omega_{T^{\ast}U^{0}}
, and, as they is a single choice for
h_{i}
,
\iota^{\ast}(0^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\sim 0)=(0^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U^{0}})\sim 0)
and
\iota^{\ast}((df)^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U})\sim 0)=((df)^{\ast}(\omega_{T^{\ast}U^{0}})\sim 0)
. Then, by pulling back the above definition along
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}
, one obtains:

(6.61)

\displaystyle\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}^{0}:=\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}\sim\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f^{0})}

Consider a point
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})(k)=\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})(k)
, and a data as in Lemma
6.10
. In particular, from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18 a)]
,
(R,U,f,i)
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
, and then
([R/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}],[U/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}],\bar{f},\bar{i})
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
. As
\omega\mapsto{\rm Grad}(\omega)
and
\omega\mapsto\omega^{0}
are defined by pullback along
\underline{\iota}
, they are compatible with pullbacks. We obtain then, denoting
\underline{\phi}^{0}:\underline{R}^{0}\to[\underline{R}^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}]\to\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})
:

\displaystyle(\underline{\phi}^{0})^{\ast}\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})

\displaystyle\sim(\underline{\phi}^{\ast}\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}))^{0}

\displaystyle\sim(\underline{j}^{\ast}\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)})^{0}

\displaystyle\sim(\underline{j}^{0})^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)})^{0}

(6.62)

\displaystyle\sim(\underline{j}^{0})^{\ast}(\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f^{0})})

We have then a smooth map
\underline{\phi}^{0}:\underline{R}^{0}\to\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})
, a smooth scheme
U^{0}
, a function
f^{0}:U^{0}\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and a map
\underline{j}^{0}:\underline{R}^{0}\to\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f^{0})
such that
f^{0}_{(R^{0})^{red}}=0
and
(\underline{\phi}^{0})^{\ast}\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})\sim(\underline{j}^{0})^{\ast}(\omega_{{\rm Crit}(f^{0})})
. Then, from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18 a)]
,
(R^{0},U^{0},f^{0},i^{0})
gives a critical chart for the
d
-critical stack obtained from the classical truncation of
(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}}),\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}))
. Using this for each cocharacter of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
, we obtain that
({\rm Grad}([R/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}]),{\rm Grad}([U/\mathbb{G}_{m,k}]),{\rm Grad}(\bar{f}),{\rm Grad}(\bar{i}))
gives a critical chart for this
d
-critical structure. Then, by Proposition
6.11

i)
, this d-critical structure coincide with the d-critical structure
{\rm Grad}(s):=\iota^{\star}(s)
. Notice that d-critical structures on a stack forms a set, hence there is no need to check any compatibility with product or étale maps of d-critical stacks.

Notice that, using Lemma
6.9
, we obtain similarly that, for
(\underline{X},\omega)
a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant shifted symplectic algebraic space, with underlying
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s)
, the
d
-critical structure
s
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant, and the
d
-critical algebraic space underlying
(\underline{X}^{0},\omega^{0})
is
(X^{0},s^{0}:=\iota^{\ast}(s))
, as expected. This does not follows directly from the result of the Lemma, as
([\underline{X}/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\bar{\omega})
is not
-1
-shifted symplectic.

ii)

For
x\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})(k)=\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})(k)
, with underlying point
\iota(x)\in\mathcal{X}(k)
, we have (recalling that we use the chomological conventions, as usual):

\displaystyle H^{-1}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{x})=H^{-1}((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{x})=\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})

(6.63)

\displaystyle H^{0}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{x})=H^{0}((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\mathcal{X}})|_{x})=T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)}

with their
\mathbb{Z}
-grading. The
-1
shifted symplectic form induces moreover isomorphisms:

\displaystyle H^{2}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{-1}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{<0}_{x})^{\vee}

(6.64)

\displaystyle H^{1}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x})

\displaystyle\simeq H^{0}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{<0}_{x})^{\vee}

and the other
H^{i}
’s vanishes by duality. Then, one can rewrite from the definition (
6.25
):

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}(x)

\displaystyle:=-\dim(\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})^{>0})+\dim((T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)})^{>0})-\dim((T_{\mathcal{X},\iota(x)})^{<0})+\dim(\mathfrak{Iso}_{\iota(x)}(\mathcal{X})^{<0})

(6.65)

\displaystyle=-\dim(H^{-1}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x}))+\dim(H^{0}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x}))-\dim(H^{1}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x}))+\dim(H^{2}((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|^{>0}_{x}))

which gives the result.

We obtain similarly that, for
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic spaces
(\underline{X},\omega)
with classical truncation the
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s)
,
{\rm Ind}_{X}
is the signed dimension of
\mathbb{T}_{X}|_{X^{0}}^{>0}
(notice that it does not follows directly from this Lemma as said above).

iii)

Consider again a smooth map
\underline{\phi}:\underline{R}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}
from a derived scheme, a smooth scheme
U
, a function
f:U\to\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}
and a map
\underline{j}:\underline{R}\to\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)
inducing an isomorphism of classical schemes such that
\underline{\phi}^{\ast}\omega\sim\underline{j}^{\ast}\omega_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}
, such that
(R,U,f,i)
is a critical chart for
(\mathcal{X},s)
. Consider the two exact triangles:

\displaystyle\underline{\phi}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\phi}}

(6.66)

\displaystyle\underline{j}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{R}}\to\mathbb{L}_{\underline{j}}

We obtain an isomorphism:

\displaystyle\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{R^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)})|_{R^{red}}\otimes\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{j}})|_{R^{red}}\otimes\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\phi}})|^{\otimes-1}_{R^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{U})|_{R^{red}}^{\otimes 2}\otimes\det(\mathbb{L}_{\phi})|^{\otimes-2}_{R^{red}}

(6.67)

\displaystyle\simeq K_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{R^{red}}

where the second line comes from
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{j}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\phi}}^{\vee}[2]
(
[
BBBBJ15
, Equation 2.14]
), and from the fact that
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)}
is quasi-isomorphic to
hess^{\vee}:\mathbb{L}_{U}^{\vee}\to\mathbb{L}_{U}
. These isomorphisms are glued in the proof of
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18 b)]
into a global isomorphism
\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})|_{\mathcal{X}^{red}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{X},s}
.

Consider a data as in Lemma
6.9
. Applying the functor
\det((\iota^{\ast}-)^{<0})
to the two exact triangles (

⁢
i
i
i

)

), using the notations in the proof of Proposition
6.11

iii)
, we find moreover an isomorphism:

\displaystyle\det((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|_{(R^{0})^{red}}

\displaystyle\simeq\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{{\rm Crit}}(f)})^{<0})|_{(R^{0})^{red}}\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{j}})^{<0})|_{(R^{0})^{red}}\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\phi}}^{<0})|^{\otimes-1}_{(R^{0})^{red}}

(6.68)

\displaystyle\simeq G_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{(R^{0})^{red}}

where we have used in the second line the fact that taking the dual reverses the

-weight, hence one obtains the weights
>0
. This isomorphism is by construction compatible with (

⁢
i
i
i

)

) and (

⁢
i
i
i

)

). As those are isomorphisms of line bundles on reduced stacks, to show that they glue into an isomorphism:

\displaystyle\det((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}\simeq G_{\mathcal{X},s}

of square roots, it suffices to show that their restriction to the stalk of an intersection point of critical charts agree. Consider the composed isomorphism:

\displaystyle\det((\iota^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}})^{<0})|_{x}\simeq G_{\mathcal{X},s}|_{x}\simeq\det((\iota^{\ast}\tau^{\leq 0}\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{X}})^{\neq 0})|_{x}

We obtain a similar formula for

Proof.

The isomorphism of commutation between specialization systems (like

with special properties on the morphism

is closed, which gives a natural adjunction morphism

. The morphism is then obtained by base change. Consider now the composition of two such correspondences, and consider the following commutative diagram:

in which the squares parallel to the left plane are Cartesian. we obtain that the arrow
\pi
for the composed correspondence is the composition of base change of the arrow

for the two correspondence. As noticed in Proposition

and Lemma

, the adjunction morphisms for

are compatible with composition of
\pi

\pi
, hence we obtain by base change in the above diagram that the composition of the morphisms for the two correspondences is the morphism for the composed correspondence. Noticing that the correspondence given by the diagonal of the lower big square of diagram

it suffices to show that the diagram of the Lemma is commutative smooth locally, hence we can work on a critical chart

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(\phi)^{\ast}\{d_{{\rm Grad}(\phi)}/2\}(p_{\mathcal{X}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathcal{X}})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}}

7.
Applications

7.1.
Bialynicki-Birula decomposition

Consider now a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s)
: recall from Section
6.2.1
that we define this to be an algebraic space with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action
X
, and a
d
-critical structure
s
on
X
that descends to a d-critical structure
\bar{s}
on
[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, i.e. such that
\mu^{\star}(s)=(pr_{2})^{\star}(s)
(as
s
lives in a set,
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariance is a property and not an extra structure). From Lemma
6.9
, given a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic space
(\underline{X},\omega)
(i.e. a derived algebraic space
\underline{X}
with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action, and a nondegenerate closed
2
-form of degree
-1

\bar{\omega}
of weight
0
, or equivalently a closed
2
-form of degree
-1

\bar{\omega}
on
[\underline{X}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
whose pullback
\omega
to
\underline{X}
is nondegenerate), its classical truncation
(X,s)
is a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant d-critical algebraic space.
K_{X,s}
has then a natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant structure coming from the formula:

(7.1)

\displaystyle K_{X,s}\simeq K_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\bar{s}}|_{X^{red}}\otimes K_{X/[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}|_{X^{red}}^{\otimes 2}

(an alternative description that works also for nonzero weights is given in Corollary
6.11
). When
(X,s)
enhance to a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
-1
-shifted symplectic algebraic space
(\underline{X},\omega)
, the isomorphism
K_{X,s}\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})|_{X^{red}}
respects this
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant structure. Given
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s)
, we define a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant orientation to be a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant square root of
K_{X,s}
(i.e. a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant line bundle
K_{X,s}^{1/2}
on
X^{red}
with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant isomorphism

). Using (
7.1
), it is equivalent to the data of an orientation on
([X/\mathbb{G}_{m}],\bar{s})
.

Classically, one obtains a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant oriented d-critical algebraic space if one consider the intersection of two
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant Lagrangian with
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant spin structure in a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant algebraic symplectic variety, or the moduli space of stable objects for a Bridgeland stability conditions on a CY3 with an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
leaving the CY3 form invariant, or the moduli space of stable representations of a quiver with potential with an action of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
leaving the potential invariant.

Consider the Bialynicki-Birula correspondence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{0}:=Map^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}({\rm Spec}(k),X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{+}:=Map^{\mathbb{G}_{m}}((\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k})^{+},X)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{+}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta^{+}}

where
\mathbb{A}^{1}
is provided with its natural

. In particular, the formula

\displaystyle(K_{X,s}^{1/2})^{0}:=(\iota^{\ast}K_{X,s}^{1/2})\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{X}})^{<0})|^{\otimes-1}_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})^{red}}

Given a point

,
T_{X,x}
is provided with a
\mathbb{Z}

(7.3)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{X}(x):=\dim(T_{X}|_{X^{0}}^{>0})-\dim(T_{X}|_{X^{0}}^{<0})

Notice that, considering

as a point of

,

has weight

, which gives, using (

(7.4)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{X}(x)={\rm Ind}_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}(x)

hence this definition is consistent with the definition for stacks, and

from Proposition
6.11

ii)
. From Lemma

ii)
, when
(X,s)

,
{\rm Ind}_{X}(x)
is the signed dimension of

, and can be generally thought as the virtual dimension of the fibers of the strata of
X^{+}
flowing to a connected component of

. Consider now
X^{0}=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}}
the decomposition into connected components, and consider
X^{+}=\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}X^{+}_{\mathbf{c}}
with

the decomposition into strata flowing to them (notice that it is also a decomposition into connected components of

), and denote by

the value of

on
