.

Theorem 7.1
.

Consider a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-invariant oriented
d
-critical algebraic space
(X,s,K_{X,s}^{1/2})
(as always, assumed to be locally of finite type over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
0
). Then, considering the oriented
d
-critical algebraic space
(X^{0},s^{0},(K_{X,s}^{1/2})^{0})
, we have a canonical isomorphism:

(7.5)

\displaystyle(p^{+})_{!}(\eta^{+})^{\ast}P_{X,s,K_{X,s}^{1/2}}\simeq P_{X^{0},s^{0},(K_{X,s}^{1/2})^{0}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{X}/2\}

Suppose moreover that
X
is separated of finite type, then
\eta
is a geometrically injective, and we have the following equality in the Grothendieck ring of monodromic Nori motives (and then monodromic mixed Hodge structures):

(7.6)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}^{T}_{c}(X,P_{X})]=\sum_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}/2}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}},P_{X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}}})]+[\mathbb{H}^{T}_{c}(X-\eta(X^{+}),P_{X}|_{X-\eta(X^{+})})]

In particular, if
X
is proper,
\eta
is geometrically bijective, and we obtain the simpler formula:

(7.7)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}^{T}_{c}(X,P_{X})]=\sum_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}/2}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}},P_{X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}}})]

Proof.

As
[X^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
is a component of
{\rm Grad}([X/\mathbb{G}_{m}])
, by Theorem
6.14
, we have a canonical isomorphism:

(7.8)

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}\simeq P_{[X^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}

Considering the smooth morphisms
\phi:X\to[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
and
\phi^{0}:X^{0}\to[X^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, enhancing by definition to smooth morphisms of oriented d-critical stacks, we obtain:

\displaystyle(p^{+})_{!}(\eta^{+})^{\ast}P_{X}

\displaystyle\simeq(p^{+})_{!}(\eta^{+})^{\ast}\phi^{\ast}\{1\}P_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}

\displaystyle\simeq(\phi^{0})^{\ast}\{1\}p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}

\displaystyle\simeq(\phi^{0})^{\ast}\{1\}P_{[X^{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}\{-{\rm Ind}_{[X/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}/2\}

(7.9)

\displaystyle\simeq P_{X^{0}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{X}/2\}

where the first and last line come from Corollary
5.9
, and the second line by base change, giving the first claim.

Suppose now that
X
is separated of finite type. In particular,
X^{0}
is of finite type too, so the cohomology with compact support is constructible, and the above isomorphism gives:

(7.10)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{c}(X^{+},(\eta^{+})^{\ast}P_{X})\simeq\mathbb{H}_{c}(X^{0},(p^{+})_{!}(\eta^{+})^{\ast}P_{X})\simeq\bigoplus_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\mathbb{H}_{c}(X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}},P_{X^{0}_{\mathbf{c}}})\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}/2\}

Given a
k
point
x\in X(k)
, the orbit gives a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
\mathbb{G}_{m,k}\to X
. Consider an extension of this map to
f:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\to X
, and denote by
x_{0}\in X(k)
the image of
\{0\}
: by considering
t\cdot f:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\to X
for any
t\in\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
, one obtains by separation of
X
that
t\cdot x_{0}=x_{0}
, i.e.
x_{0}\in X^{0}
, and
f
is
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant. Then points of
X^{+}(k)
above
x\in X(k)
are in bijection with extensions
f:\mathbb{A}^{1}_{k}\to X
as above. Then, because
X
is separated,
\eta
is injective on
k
-point, and it is furthermore bijective on
k
-point when
X
is proper. We obtain then the result using Lemma
4.5
.
∎

Remark 7.2
.

Notice that, even in the smooth case treated in
[
BB73
]
, the
X^{+}_{\mathbf{c}}
do not give a stratification of
X
in the usual sense, see the counterexample of
[
Kon82
]
. However, in the smooth case, even without assuming the existence of an equivariant projectivization, the restriction of
\eta
to each connected component is a locally closed immersion as proven in
[
BB73
]
. In the singular case, it is no longer true without this assumption: consider for example the nodal curve
X
obtained by gluing
0
and
\infty
in
\mathbb{P}^{1}
, with the quotient of the canonical
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action on
\mathbb{P}^{1}
. one has
\mathbb{A}^{1}=X^{+}\to X
is not a locally closed immersion. See
[
HL14
, Remark 1.4.4, Remark 5.3.9]
,
[
DG13
, Section 1.6]
. One obtains a true stratification if one can embeds
X
in a projective space with linear action.

7.2.
Motivic formula for Theta-stratifications

We recall the definition of a
\Theta
-stratification from
[
HL14
, Definition 2.1.2]
:

Definition 7.3
.

Consider a quasi-separated, locally of finite type Artin
k
-stack
\mathcal{X}
with affine stabilizers. A
\Theta
-stratification of
\mathcal{X}
is the data of:

•

a totally ordered set
\Gamma
with a minimal element
0
and a collection of open substacks
\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}
for
{\mathbf{c}}\in\Gamma
such that
\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}\subseteq\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}}
for
{\mathbf{c}}<{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}
and
\mathcal{X}=\bigcup_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}
(We call
\mathcal{X}^{ss}:=\mathcal{X}_{\leq 0}
the semistable locus.);

•

For each
{\mathbf{c}}\in\Gamma
, a
\Theta
-stratum
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}
of
\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}
- i.e. an union of open components of
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}})
such that
\eta|_{\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}}:\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}\to\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}
is a closed immersion and:

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}-\eta(\Theta_{\mathbf{c}})=\mathcal{X}_{<{\mathbf{c}}}:=\bigcup_{{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}<{\mathbf{c}}}\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}^{\prime}}

•

for every point
x\in\mathcal{X}(k)
, the set
\{{\mathbf{c}}\in\Gamma|x\in\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}}\}
has a minimal element
{\mathbf{c}}(x)
. The unique
y\in\Theta_{{\mathbf{c}}(x)}
such that
\eta(y)=x
is called the Harder-Narasimhan filtration of
x
.

We define the center
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}:=\tilde{\iota}^{-1}(\Theta_{\mathbf{c}})
of the
\Theta
-stratum
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}
.

Notice that from the definition,
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}
is open in
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq{\mathbf{c}}})
, which is open in
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X})
, hence
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}
is open in
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
. We have moreover (it is noticed in
[
HL14
, Page 42]
:

Lemma 7.4
.

Given a
\Theta
-stratification of a quasi-separated, locally of finite type
k
Artin stack
\mathcal{X}
, we have for any stratum
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}=p^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}})
.

Proof.

Because
\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}}\to\mathcal{X}
is an open immersion we have from
[
HL14
, Corollary 1.1.7, Proposition 1.3.1 3)]
that
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}})=\tilde{\iota}^{-1}({\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}})
, and
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}})=p^{-1}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}})
. But
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}
is a connected component of
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}})
, by
[
HL14
, lemma 1.3.8]

(i_{\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}}}\circ p_{\mathcal{X}_{\leq\mathbf{c}}})^{-1}(\Theta_{\mathbf{c}})=\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}
, which means that
\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}=p^{-1}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}})
.
∎

The following theorem allows to computes the cohomological DT invariants of
\mathcal{X}
in terms of those of the center:

Theorem 7.5
.

Consider
(\mathcal{X},s,K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2})
an
d
-critical stack which is quasi-separated, of finite type with affine stabilizers over an algebraically closed field
k
of characteristic
0
, with an isomorphism class of orientation. Consider a
\Theta
-stratification on
\mathcal{X}
(in particular, because
\mathcal{X}
is of finite type, it has a finite number of nonempty strata). The center
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}
of the
\Theta
-strata have a natural oriented d-critical structure, and, up to refining the
\Theta
-stratification, we can assume that the locally constant function
{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}
have a constant value
{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}
on
\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}
. We have then the following equality in the completed Grothendieck ring of monodromic Nori motives or monodromic mixed Hodge structures:

(7.12)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{X},P_{\mathcal{X}})]=\sum_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}/2}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}},P_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}})]

Proof.

Notice that, as the Grothendieck ring form a set, and not a category, any choice of orientation in the isomorphism class will give the same object. Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{X})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{X}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathbf{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\mathbf{c}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\mathbf{c}}}

where the left square is Cartesian from Lemma
7.4
, and the vertical arrows are open immersions. We have from Theorem
6.14
a canonical isomorphism:

(7.13)

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{X}}\simeq P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}\}

Then, pulling back along the open immersion
i_{\mathbf{c}}:\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})
, we obtain:

\displaystyle(p_{\mathbf{c}})_{!}(\eta_{\mathbf{c}})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i_{\mathbf{c}})^{\ast}p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{X}}

\displaystyle\simeq(i_{\mathbf{c}})^{\ast}P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{X})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{X}}\}

(7.14)

\displaystyle\simeq P_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}\}

where the first line comes from functoriality and base change, the second from Theorem
6.14
, and the last from the compatibility of the DT construction with open immersions given in Corollary
5.9
. Using proper pushforward to a point we obtain:

(7.15)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\Theta_{\mathbf{c}},(\eta_{\mathbf{c}})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{X}})]\simeq\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Ind}_{\mathbf{c}}/2}\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}},P_{\mathcal{Z}_{\mathbf{c}}})

Notice now that, by assumption,
\bigsqcup_{\mathbf{c}\in\Gamma}\eta_{\mathbf{c}}:\Theta_{\mathbf{c}}\to\mathcal{X}
is a geometric bijection, and then Lemma
4.5
gives the result.
∎

7.3.
Recollection on moduli stacks of complexes

7.3.1.
Moduli of objects in dg categories

We follow here
[
TV05
]
,
[
BD18
]
: in particular, we adopt the conventions of
[
BD18
]
. Consider a presentable dg-category
\mathcal{C}
over
k
(i.e. a category enriched over
k
-complexes, with arbitrary colimits, generated by a set of compact objects) and denote by
\mathcal{C}_{c}
its (small) dg-subcategory of compact objects, i.e. objects
E
such that
{\rm Hom}(E,-)
commutes with filtered colimits (such objects are also sometimes called objects of finite presentation). We always assume
\mathcal{C}
to be of finite type according to
[
TV05
, Definition 2.4]
, i.e. it is equivalent to the category of module of a dg-algebra which is homotopically of finite presentation. It is in particular smooth, following
[
TV05
, Proposition 2.14]
. Recall that a continuous (i.e. , colimit preserving) functor
F
between two presentable dg-categories always has a right adjoint
F^{r}
, which is itself continous iff
F
preserves compact objects.

A dg-category is said to be proper if, for any
c,c^{\prime}
compact,
{\rm Hom}(c,c^{\prime})\in Vect_{k}
is compact, i.e. is a homotopically finite dimensional complex. A smooth and proper dg category is of finite type from
[
TV05
, Corollary 2.13]
. If
\mathcal{C}
is not necessarily proper, an object
c\in\mathcal{C}
is said to be left-proper if
{\rm Hom}(c,c^{\prime})
is homotopically of finite dimension for every
c^{\prime}\in\mathcal{C}_{c}
, and we denote by
\mathcal{C}_{lp}
the full subcategory of left-proper objects. When
\mathcal{C}
is smooth, left proper objects are in particular compact from
[
TV05
, Lemma 2.8 2)]
, and if
\mathcal{C}
is proper, then compact objects are proper by definition.

Typically, if
X
is a separated scheme of finite type and
\mathcal{C}={\rm QCoh}(X)
, we obtain that
\mathcal{C}_{c}={\rm Perf}(X)
, and
\mathcal{C}
is smooth iff
X
is smooth from
[
Lun09
, Proposition 3.13]
, in which case
\mathcal{C}_{c}={\rm Perf}(X)={\rm Coh}(X)
and
\mathcal{C}
is of finite type from
[
A.I03
, Theorem 3.1.1]
. Similarly,
\mathcal{C}
is proper iff
X
is proper from
[
Orl14
, Proposition 3.30]
. Taking now any separated scheme of finite type
X
,
\mathcal{C}={\rm IndCoh}(X)
,
\mathcal{C}_{c}=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
,
\mathcal{C}
is of finite type from
[
Efi20
]
: in particular, it is smooth, as proven earlier in
[
Lun09
, Theorem 6.3]
(even without any smoothness assumption on
X
!).

Given a
k
dg-algebra
\underline{R}
, an object
E
of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
is said to be left proper over
\underline{R}
if
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(E,-):\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\to{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
maps compact objects, i.e. objects of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})
, to compact objects, i.e. objects of
{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})
. As
\mathcal{C}
is smooth, from
[
TV05
, Lemma 2.8 2)]
, left-proper objects are compact, i.e. lies in
\mathcal{C}_{c}\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})
. As
\mathcal{C}
is smooth, from
[
BD18
, Proposition 2.4, Corollary 2.5]
, there is a natural continuous involution:

(7.16)

\displaystyle Id_{\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})/{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})}:\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\simeq\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})

such that, for
E
-left proper, there is a natural isomorphism of functors:

(7.17)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(E,-)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(-,(Id_{\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})/{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})}^{!})^{-1}(E))^{\vee}:\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\to{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})

called the relative Serre functor (it agrees with the classical Serre functor for
\mathcal{C}={\rm IndCoh}(X)
).

In
[
TV05
]
, a higher derived stack
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
classifying left-proper objects of
\mathcal{C}
was defined, and was shown in
[
TV05
, Theorem 3.6]
to be locally Artin (i.e. it is an union of Artin
n
-stacks for increasing
n
) and locally of finite presentation over
k
, when
\mathcal{C}
is of finite type (which we will always assume). In
[
BD18
, Example 3.7]
, it is described by the moduli functor:

(7.18)

\displaystyle\underline{\mathcal{M}}({\rm Spec}(\underline{R}))={\rm Map}_{dgcat}(\mathcal{C}_{c},{\rm Perf}(\underline{R}))

sending an affine derived scheme
{\rm Spec}(\underline{R})
to the space of exact functors from the small dg-category of compact objects
\mathcal{C}_{c}
to the small dg-category
{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})
of perfect
\underline{R}
-modules (i.e. , perfect complexes on
{\rm Spec}(\underline{R})
). The functoriality comes here from pullback of perfect modules. Those corresponds to continuous adjunction:

(7.19)

\displaystyle f:\mathcal{C}\rightleftharpoons{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R}):f^{r}

and so, as
\mathcal{C}
is smooth, such a data corresponds from
[
BD18
, Corollary 2.6]
to the data of a left proper objects
E
of
\mathcal{C}\otimes{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
, such that:

(7.20)

\displaystyle f={\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(E,-\boxtimes_{k}\underline{R}):\mathcal{C}\to{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})

In particular, every
k
-points
f
is then corepresented by a left proper object
E
of
\mathcal{C}
, i.e.
f\simeq{\rm Hom}(E,-)
, but the usual convention is to use the relative Serre duality functor and to identify the
k
-point with the right proper object
F
of
\mathcal{C}
representing
f
, i.e. such that
f\simeq{\rm Hom}(-,F)^{\vee}
.

By construction, one have a universal functor
F_{\mathcal{C}}:C_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}))
, which corresponds from
[
BD18
, Corollary 2.6]
to a left proper object
\mathcal{U}
of
\mathcal{C}_{c}\times{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})
, called the universal complex.
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
has a cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}
, and a tangent complex
\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}:=\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\vee}\in{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})
, given from
[
BD18
, Proposition 3.3]
by the formula:

(7.21)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}(\mathcal{U},\mathcal{U})[1]

(notice that we consider
\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}=\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\vee}
as living in
{\rm Perf}({\underline{\mathcal{M}}})
). In particular, as proven in
[
TV05
, Corollary 3.17]
, given an compact object
E
of
\mathcal{C}
, seen as a
k
-point of
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, one have a natural isomorphism:

(7.22)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}},E}\simeq\mathcal{C}(E,E)[1]

in other terms,
{\rm Ext}^{1}(E,E)
gives the Zariski tangent space,
Ext^{\geq 2}(E,E)
the obstructions,
{\rm Ext}^{0}(E,E)
the Lie algebra of the isotropy group, and
{\rm Ext}^{<0}(E,E)
the higher stacky part (which explains why one has to consider Artin
n
-stacks for
n
arbitrarily large).

Given a
n
-dimensional Calabi-Yau structure on
\mathcal{C}
(also called a left Calabi-Yau structure in
[
BD16
, Definition 3.5]
),
[
BD18
, Theorem 5.5 1)]
gives a natural
n-2
-shifted symplectic structure
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
. Informally a
n
-dimensional Calabi-Yau structure gives a functorial nondegenerate paring:

(7.23)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}(E,F)\simeq{\rm Hom}(F,E)^{\vee}[n]

giving a version of Serre duality for
\mathcal{C}
, inducing a nondegenerate pairing:

(7.24)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}},E}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}},E}[n-2]

that glue into the nondegenerate pairing induced by the
2
form of degree
n-2
underlying
\omega
. More precisely, from
[
BD18
, Corollary 2.5, Proposition 5.3]
, the class of the Hochschild homology
HH(\mathcal{C})
of degree
n
underlying the Calabi-Yau structure induces an isomorphism of functors:

(7.25)

\displaystyle{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}(\mathcal{U},-)\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}(-,\mathcal{U})^{\vee}[n]

from which one obtains the nondegenerate pairing:

(7.26)

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\simeq\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}[n-2]

from (
7.21
). Of course, one need also an extra structure, that will define the closed
2
form of degree
n-2

\omega
, we refer to
[
BD16
, Section 3.2]
,
[
BD18
, Section 5]
or the proof of Lemma
7.9
below for more details. We specialize here to the case
n=3
, where one obtains a
-1
shifted symplectic structure. From
[
BD16
, Proposition 5.11]
, for
X
a Gorenstein scheme of dimension
n
, the data of a Calabi-Yau structure of dimension
n
on
\mathcal{C}_{c}=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
is equivalent to the data of a trivialisation of the cotangent bundle
\omega_{X}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}
.

Remark 7.6
.

We will now follow and adapt the discussion of
[
JU21
, Section 3]
on orientations on CY3 categories. Remark that here that
\mathcal{C}_{c}
is not assumed to be
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
for a CY threefold
X
, so we have to adapt a bit the notations. We consider the CY3 case as in
[
JU21
, Section 4.1]
, so
m=3
and the spin structure
J
is trivial. The classical truncation
\mathcal{M}
of
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
was denoted by
\bar{\mathcal{M}}
in
[
JU21
, Section 3]
, and the authors denote there by
\mathcal{M}
the classical open
1
-substack of objects in the Abelian heart
Coh(X)
: we will below denote the analogue of this by
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
, where
\mathcal{A}
will be the Abelian heart of a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}
, restricting to a
t
-structure on
\mathcal{C}_{c}
. The perfect complexes
\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}
and
\mathcal{D}^{\bullet}
of
[
JU21
, Section 3]
will be denoted below by
\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}|_{\mathcal{M}}[-1]
and
\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}|_{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}}[-1]
, and our
K_{\mathcal{M}},L_{\mathcal{M}}
will be their
K_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}},L_{\bar{\mathcal{M}}}
. Our
\oplus_{2}
will be their
\Phi
, and we denote the projections by
pr_{i}
instead of
\pi_{i}
, as in the rest of this article. For clarity, given a morphism
f:\underline{\mathcal{X}}\to\underline{\mathcal{Y}}
, we will denote the functor
(Id_{\mathcal{C}_{c}}\otimes_{k}f^{\ast}):\mathcal{C}_{c}\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{Y}})\to\mathcal{C}_{c}\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{Y}})
simply by
f^{\ast}
, when no confusion can happen.

Direct sum gives a natural structure of commutative monoid
\underline{\oplus_{n}}:\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{n}\to\underline{\mathcal{M}}
,
0:{\rm Spec}(k)\to\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, given on
\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})
points by:

\displaystyle(f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R}))_{1\leq i\leq n}\mapsto(\bigoplus f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R}))

(7.27)

\displaystyle(\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})\to\underline{{\rm Spec}}(k))\mapsto(0:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R}))

As the support of a perfect complex of
\underline{R}
-modules is open,
0:{\rm Spec}(k)\to\underline{\mathcal{M}_{Vect_{k}}}
is an open immersion, hence, choosing an affine generator homotopically of finite type of
\mathcal{C}
, an considering the map
\underline{\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{\mathcal{M}_{Vect_{k}}}
of
[
TV05
, Section 3.2]
, we find that
0:{\rm Spec}(k)\to\underline{\mathcal{M}}
is an open immersion. One obtains from the definition a coherent system of isomorphisms:

(7.28)

\displaystyle(\underline{\oplus_{n}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U})\simeq\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}(\underline{pr_{i}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U})

Denote by:

(7.29)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}:={\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}}}((pr_{1})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}),(pr_{2})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}))[1]\in{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}\times\underline{\mathcal{M}})

and
\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}:=\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\vee}
, such that
\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\simeq(\Delta_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{\ast}(\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})
. Notice that, considering the commutativity isomorphism
\Sigma_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}:\underline{\mathcal{X}}\times\underline{\mathcal{X}}\to\underline{\mathcal{X}}\times\underline{\mathcal{X}}
, one obtains from (
7.25
) the nondegenerate pairing:

(7.30)

\displaystyle\Sigma_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}^{\ast}(\tilde{\mathbb{T}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})\simeq\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}[n-2]

Using (
7.21
), we find a coherent system of isomorphisms:

\displaystyle(\underline{\oplus_{n}})^{\ast}(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{n}}((\underline{\oplus_{n}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}),(\underline{\oplus_{n}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}))^{\vee}[-1]

\displaystyle\simeq{\rm Hom}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{n}}(\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}(\underline{pr_{i}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}),\bigoplus_{1\leq j\leq n}(\underline{pr_{j}})^{\ast}(\mathcal{U}))^{\vee}[-1]

(7.31)

\displaystyle\simeq\bigoplus_{1\leq i\leq n}(\underline{pr_{i}})^{\ast}(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})\oplus\bigoplus_{1\leq i\neq j\leq n}(\underline{pr}_{ij})^{\ast}(\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})

We denote now:

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle:=\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})|_{\mathcal{M}}

(7.32)

\displaystyle L_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle:=\det(\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})|_{\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}}

Such that we obtain
K_{\mathcal{M}}\simeq(\Delta_{L_{\mathcal{M}}})^{\ast}L_{\mathcal{M}}
, and we obtain from (
7.30
) an isomorphism:

(7.33)

\displaystyle(\Sigma_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq L_{\mathcal{M}}

whose restriction along the diagonal is
Id:K_{\mathcal{M}}\simeq K_{\mathcal{M}}
. Combining this with the determinant of (
7.3.1
), we obtain as in
[
JU21
, (3.16)]
an isomorphism:

(7.34)

\displaystyle(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2}

There are moreover from
[
JU21
, (3.17),(3.18)]
natural isomorphisms, derived with the same kinds of computation:

\displaystyle(\oplus_{2}\times Id_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}L_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\simeq(pr_{13})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus(pr_{23})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})

(7.35)

\displaystyle(Id_{\mathcal{M}}\times\oplus_{2})^{\ast}L_{\mathcal{M}}

\displaystyle\simeq(pr_{12})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus(pr_{13})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})

Considering the square giving the associativity of the monoid structure
\oplus_{n}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{2}\times Id_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id_{\mathcal{M}}\times\oplus_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{2}}

There is from
[
JU21
, (3.19)]
a commutative square of isomorphisms:

(7.36)

(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{3})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})

\otimes(pr_{12})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})\otimes(pr_{13})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})\otimes(pr_{23})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\oplus_{2}\times Id_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}((pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Id_{\mathcal{M}}\times\oplus_{2})^{\ast}((pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})}

(\oplus_{2}\times Id_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})

\simeq(Id_{\mathcal{M}}\times\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the arrows come from (
7.34
) and (
7.3.1
).

We give the definition of orientation data (resp. strong orientation data) from
[
JU21
, Definition 4.2]
, extended straightforwardly from
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
to any CY3 category
\mathcal{C}_{c}
. More precisely, we give the definition of spin structure compatible with direct sum (resp. strong spin structure compatible with direct sum) from
[
JU21
, Definition 3.4]
(resp.
[
JU21
, Definition 3.7]
), which give are equivalent data from
[
JU21
, Proposition 4.3]
(the strong case follows from the same
\mathbb{A}^{1}
-contractibility arguments). Notice that we will work with strong spin structure because our strategy to prove Kontsevich-Soibelman wall crossing formula and build the CoHA is different from the one sketched in
[
JU21
, Section 4.3]
, and do not use Joyce’s conjecture from
[
JS19
, Conjecture 1.1]
.

Definition 7.7
.

(
[
JU21
, Definition 3.4, Definition 3.7]
)
Consider a presentable CY3 category
\mathcal{C}
of finite type:

i)

An orientation data on
\mathcal{C}_{c}
is the data of an isomorphism class
[K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}]
of squares root of
K_{\mathcal{M}}
, such that, for one (and then, any) representative
K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}
, there is an isomorphism:

(7.37)

\displaystyle(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\simeq(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes L_{\mathcal{M}}

which is a square root of (
7.34
).

ii)

A strong orientation data on
\mathcal{C}_{c}
is the data of a square root
K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}
of
K_{\mathcal{M}}
, with the data of a square root of (
7.34
) as above, such that the diagram:

(7.38)

(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{3})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

\otimes(pr_{12})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{13})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{23})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\oplus_{2}\times Id_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}((pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes L_{\mathcal{M}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Id_{\mathcal{M}}\times\oplus_{2})^{\ast}((pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2})}

obtained from it and (
7.3.1
), which is a square root of (

), commutes.

Notice that this definition depends only on the nondegenerate pairing induced by the CY3 structure.

Remark 7.8
.

Canonical orientation data for
\mathcal{C}_{c}=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, for
X
a smooth projective Calabi-Yau threefold, were build in
[
JU21
, Theorem 4.4]
, and for
X
a smooth quasiprojective Calabi-Yau threefold with the data of a spin compactification in
[
JU21
, Theorem 4.8]
(but it is, to our knowledge, not known to be independent of this compactification). In
[
JU21
, Theorem 3.10]
, the authors describes the topological obstruction to lift this orientation data to a strong orientation data. From the arguments of
[
JU21
, Section 5]
(Proposition below), giving a (strong) orientation data on
\mathcal{C}
is equivalent to giving a (strong) orientation data on the Abelian heart of a
t
-structure. Also, one can define a strong orientation data when
\mathcal{C}_{c}
is equivalent to the dg-category of a quiver with potential (indeed, in this case,
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
is a global critical chart, hence one has a trivial orientation), but, to our knowledge, one does not known if it is independent of the choice of quiver with potential, nor if the underlying orientation data coincide with the one of
[
JU21
, Theorem 4.8]
in the presence of a spin compactification.

Consider
E,F\in\mathcal{C}_{lp}
, and consider:

(7.39)

\displaystyle\langle E,F\rangle:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i+1}\dim({\rm Ext}^{i}(E,F))

which is finite, as
E
is left proper and
F
is in particular compact. The CY3 pairing gives
\langle E,F\rangle=-\langle F,E\rangle
.

Denote by
(\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathbb{Z})},\omega_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\mathbb{Z})})
the
n-2
-shifted symplectic stack obtained by taking the colimit of the products
\prod_{i\in\{-n,n\}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}})
, where the maps are defined by:

(7.40)

\displaystyle 0\times Id\times 0:\prod_{i\in\{-n,n\}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}})\hookrightarrow\prod_{i\in\{-(n+1),n+1\}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}},\omega_{\mathcal{C}})

which are open and closed immersions, as
0
is isolated. It is the stack of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded left-proper objects of
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
: notice that it is a locally Artin stack, locally of finite presentation. Given a line bundle
L
on
\mathcal{M}
, denote by
L^{(\mathbb{Z})}
the line bundle on
\mathcal{M}^{(\mathbb{Z})}
obtained by the colimit of
\boxtimes_{-n\leq i\leq n}L
.

The point
i)
of the following is standard for classical stacks without shifted symplectic structure (see
[
HL14
, Section 6.3]
,
[
AHLH18
, Section 7.2]
, and it will be the only place where we will have to unwrap the construction of
[
BD18
, Theorem 5.5]
. The discussion about
\tau
, following the one of Section
6.3.4
, will be important for the proof of the associativity of the CoHA.

Lemma 7.9
.

Consider a dg-category of finite type
\mathcal{C}
with a Calabi-Yau structure of dimension
3
.

i)

There is a natural isomorphism of
-1
-shifted symplectic stacks:

(7.41)

\displaystyle(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}),{\rm Grad}(\omega_{\mathcal{C}}))\simeq(\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathbb{Z})},\omega_{\mathcal{C}}^{(\mathbb{Z})})

(in particular, the left hand side is locally Artin, and locally of finite presentation), such that
\underline{\iota}
is identified with the colimit of the
\underline{\oplus_{n}}
. Under this identification, the involution:

(7.42)

\displaystyle\underline{\tau}:\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}))\simeq\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}}))

obtained by swapping the two
B\mathbb{G}_{m,k}
factors
is identified with the involution
(\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}\simeq(\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}
obtained by swapping the indices.

ii)

Under the identification of
i)
, the isomorphism (
7.3.1
) gives the weight decomposition:

\displaystyle\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}=

\displaystyle(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{0}\oplus(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{<0}\oplus(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{>0}\simeq(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{0}\oplus((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{<0})^{\otimes 2}

(7.43)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(pr_{i})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i<j\in\mathbb{Z}}(pr_{ij})^{\ast}\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i>j\in\mathbb{Z}}(pr_{ij})^{\ast}\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\simeq\bigoplus_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(pr_{i})^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\oplus\bigoplus_{i<j\in\mathbb{Z}}((pr_{ij})^{\ast}\tilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{\otimes 2}

where the term corresponding to
i,j
has weight
i-j
, and the last isomorphism comes from (
7.33
). In particular, the signed dimension of
(\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{>0}
at the point
(E_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
is given by the classical formula
\sum_{i<j}\langle E_{i},E_{j}\rangle
.

iii)

Suppose now that
\mathcal{C}_{c}
has an orientation data
[K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}]
(resp. a strong orientation data
K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}
). Extending straightforwardly the definitions of Lemma
6.13

ii)
and Lemma
6.15

iii)
and using the identification of
i)
, there is a (resp. canonical) isomorphism:

(7.44)

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\simeq(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})}

(resp. such that the isomorphism:

(7.45)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}{\rm Grad}({\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}))\simeq{\rm Grad}({\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{X},s}^{1/2}))

and the isomorphism:

(7.46)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}((K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}\simeq((K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}

obtained by swapping the indices agree when restricted on the open substack:

(7.47)

\displaystyle(\mathcal{M})_{(0,0)}\times(\mathcal{M})_{(1,0)}\times(\mathcal{M})_{(1,1)}

)

Proof.

i)

By definition,
\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})
is defined as the prestack:

(7.48)

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})\mapsto\underline{\mathcal{M}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})

by descent, the latter is identified with the space of objects of:

(7.49)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})\simeq\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k})

whose pullback to
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
are left-proper. From
[
AHLH18
, Section 7.2]
,
{\rm QCoh}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,k})
is the dg-category of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded complexes, hence the latter is identified as the dg-category of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded objects
(E_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
, where the pullbacks takes the direct sum. If a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded object
\bigoplus_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}E_{n}
is left proper, it is in particular compact, hence a finite number of
E_{n}
must be nonzero. But a finite sum of objects is left proper iff each object is left proper. It gives then the equivalence of spaces:

(7.50)

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R}))\simeq({\rm colim}_{n\to\infty}\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\underline{\mathcal{M}}(\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})))

which is obviously functorial in
\underline{R}
, and gives then a natural isomorphism:

(7.51)

\displaystyle\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})\simeq(\underline{\mathcal{M}}^{(\mathbb{Z})})

such that
\underline{\iota}
is the colimit of the
\underline{\oplus_{n}}
. Notice that the map:

(7.52)

\displaystyle i_{n}:\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\underline{\mathcal{M}}\to\underline{{\rm Grad}}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})

is simply identified with the functor that sends
2n+1
continuous adjunctions:

(7.53)

\displaystyle f_{i}:\mathcal{C}\rightleftharpoons{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R}):(f_{i})^{r}

to the continuous adjunction:

(7.54)

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}\rightleftharpoons{\rm QCoh}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})\simeq{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})^{\mathbb{Z}}

obteined by taking the
i
-th component to be
(f_{i},(f_{i})^{r})
for
-n\leq i\leq n
, and the other one to be
(0,0)
.

Using the above description, and the fact that
{\rm QCoh}((B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m})_{\underline{R}})
is the dg-category of
\mathbb{Z}\times\mathbb{Z}
-graded objects of
{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
, we find the claim about
\underline{\tau}
.

We identify now the shifted symplectic structures. Fortunately, the definition of
\omega_{\mathcal{C}}
in
[
BD18
, Proposition 5.2]
is pretty transparent, the hard part of
[
BD18
, Theorem 5.5]
being the fact that it is nondegenerate. In
[
BD18
, Section 4.2]
, the authors define, for any presentable dg-category
\mathcal{C}^{\prime}
, a graded mixed complex
HH(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
(a
S^{1}
-equivariant version of the Hochschild homology), which is functorial with respect to continuous adjunctions, i.e. given a continuous adjunction
f:\mathcal{C}^{\prime}\rightleftharpoons\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime}
, there is a functorial morphism
HH(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})\to HH(\mathcal{C}^{\prime\prime})
. It is moreover symmetric monoidal functor, as it is defined in
[
BD18
, Section 4.2]
by applying the symmetric monoidal functor of
S^{1}
equivariant traces from
[
HSS17
, Theorem 2.14]
to the symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{C}\to(\mathcal{C},Id_{\mathcal{C}})
. Applying the negative cycle complex functor
HC^{-}
from graded mixed complexes to complexes described in
[
BD18
, Section 5.1]
(which is also symmetric monoidal, as can be seen using its definition as a pushforward), one obtains a graded complex, denoted by the shorthand notation
HC^{-}(\mathcal{C}^{\prime})
, functorial with continuous adjunctions and symmetric monoidal.

Using the naturality of the symmetric monoidal functor of
[
HSS17
, Theorem 2.14]
applied to the symmetric monoidal functor of
2
-categories:

(7.55)

\displaystyle QCoh:Corr(Aff)\to(DGCat^{2}_{con})^{2-op}

from
[
GR17
, 5.5.3]
, the authors obtain in
[
BD18
, Theorem 4.6]
an isomorphism:

(7.56)

\displaystyle\Gamma(L\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R}),\mathcal{O}_{L\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})})\simeq HH({\rm QCoh}(\underline{R}))

which is functorial and symmetric monoidal in
\underline{R}
. The space of closed
p
-form of degree
n
on an affine scheme is defined by:

(7.57)

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{p,cl}(\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R}),n):=|HC_{w}^{-}(\Gamma(L\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R}),\mathcal{O}_{L\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})}))(p)[n-p]|

Using descent from the affine case, the authors define in
[
BD18
, Section 5.2]
, for any stack
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
and any
p\in\mathbb{Z}
, a morphism:

(7.58)

\displaystyle\chi_{\underline{\mathcal{X}}}:|HC^{-}({\rm Ind}({\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{X}})))[-n]|\to\mathcal{A}^{p,cl}(\underline{\mathcal{X}},p-n)

which is functorial, and symmetric monoidal in
\underline{\mathcal{X}}
(this is a derived version of the Hochschild-Kostant-Rosenberg theorem).

A Calabi-Yau structure of dimension
n
is defined to be a map
k[n]\to HC^{-}(\mathcal{C})
, i.e. the space of Calabi-Yau structure of dimension
n
is given by
|HC^{-}(\mathcal{C})[-n]|
. Using the universal continuous adjunction:

(7.59)

\displaystyle\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}:\mathcal{C}\rightleftharpoons{\rm Ind}({\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})):\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}}^{r}

the map from the space of Calabi-Yau structure of dimension
n
to the space of closed
2
form of degree
n-2

\omega
is then defined in
[
BD18
, Proposition 5.2]
as the composition:

(7.60)

\displaystyle|HC^{-}(\mathcal{C})[-n]|\overset{HC^{-}(\mathcal{F}_{\mathcal{C}})}{\to}|HC^{-}({\rm Ind}({\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})))[-n]|\overset{\chi_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}}{\to}\mathcal{A}^{2,cl}_{k}(\underline{\mathcal{M}},2-n)

and its image is shown in
[
BD18
, Theorem 5.5]
. to lie in the subspace of nondegenerate closed
2
forms of degree
2-n
, i.e.
2-n
-shifted symplectic structures.

Consider the continuous adjunction of smooth dg-categories:

(7.61)

\displaystyle\pi_{n}:\mathcal{C}\rightleftharpoons\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\mathcal{C}:(\pi_{n})^{r}

where
\pi_{n}
sends an objects to
2n+1
copies of it, and
(\pi_{n})^{r}
sends
2n+1
objects of
\mathcal{C}
to their direct sum. For a functor
G
on the category of stacks to a category of objects with an additive structure
\oplus
and a finite set
I
, we denote:

(7.62)

\displaystyle\boxplus:=((g_{i})_{i\in I}\mapsto\bigoplus_{i\in I}(pr_{i})^{\ast}g_{i}):\prod_{i\in I}G(\underline{\mathcal{X}}_{i})\to G(\prod_{i\in I}\underline{\mathcal{X}}_{i})

We consider now the following diagram of spaces:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|HC^{-}(\mathcal{C})[-n]|}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{|HC^{-}({\rm Ind}({\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})))[-n]|}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(F_{\mathcal{C}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces HC^{-}(\pi_{n})}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\iota}^{\ast}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxplus}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\boxplus}

We have to prove that it is commutative: it will build an equivalence
(\underline{i_{n}})^{\ast}\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\sim\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\omega_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}
which is exactly what we want to prove. Notice that this equivalence will be obviously a compatible system of equivalence for the system
i_{n}
, but we will be at the end only interested in comparing the d-critical structures, which lives in a set, so we are not so interested in these questions. The four little squares are commutative from fact that
HC^{-}
and (
7.58
) are functorial with continuous adjunction and symmetric monoidal. To show that the left rectangle commutes, it suffices to prove:

(7.63)

\displaystyle(i_{n})^{\ast}\iota^{\ast}\circ F_{\mathcal{C}}=\boxplus\circ(\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}F_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\pi_{n}

we will do this by identifying the prestacks. Beware that we will work with the restriction of these functors for the small dg-categories of compact objects. Consider a point
f=(f_{-n},...,f_{n}):\underline{{\rm Spec}}(\underline{R})\to\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, which gives
(2n+1)
exact continuous maps

with continuous right inverse. Then

is the map:

(7.64)

\displaystyle(...,0,f_{-n},...,f_{n},...,0):\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}})

and so

is the map:

(7.65)

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{-n\leq i\leq n}f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})

such that by definition:

(7.66)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}((i_{n})^{\ast}\iota^{\ast}\circ F_{c}):=\bigoplus_{-n\leq i\leq n}f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})

On the other hand, we have by definition:

(7.67)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\boxplus:=((g_{-n},...,g_{n})\mapsto\bigoplus_{-n\leq i\leq n}(f_{i})^{\ast}g_{i}:\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}{\rm Perf}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})

such that, still by definition:

(7.68)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\boxplus\circ(\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}F_{\mathcal{C}}):=((g_{-n},...,g_{n})\mapsto\bigoplus_{-n\leq i\leq n}f_{i}(g_{i})):\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})

and finally, by definition of
\pi_{n}

(7.69)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}(\boxplus\circ(\prod_{-n\leq i\leq n}F_{\mathcal{C}})\circ\pi_{n}):=\bigoplus_{-n\leq i\leq n}f_{i}:\mathcal{C}_{c}\to{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})

So (
7.66
) and (

) gives (

), and then the compatibility of the big left rectangle of the above diagram, which finishes the proof.

ii)

To prove the claim about
\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}
, we need to consider a point

, i.e. a functor

corepresented by

, and describe the

-grading on

. But, by (

(7.70)

\displaystyle f^{\ast}\mathbb{T}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}}\simeq{\rm Hom}_{B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}}((g_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}},(g_{j})_{j\in\mathbb{Z}})[1]=\bigoplus_{i,j}{\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(g_{i},g_{j})[1]

: passing to the dual, the

has weight

, which proves the claim. The last claim follows straightforwardly from the definition of
\langle-,-\rangle
.

iii)

Consider first the case of an orientation data
[K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}]
, and fix an orientation

and a (noncanonical) isomorphism:

\displaystyle\zeta_{2}:(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\simeq(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus L_{\mathcal{M}}

which is a square root of:

\displaystyle\phi_{2}:(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\simeq(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}})\oplus L_{\mathcal{M}}^{\otimes 2}

Denote by

for

. Writing

and using the isomorphisms (

of

. Working iteratively, we write an inductive system

of square roots

\displaystyle\iota^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\simeq\bigotimes_{(i,i^{\prime})\in\mathbb{Z}^{2}}(pr_{i})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes\bigotimes_{i<j\in\mathbb{Z}}(pr_{ij})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})

which is a square root of the determinant of (

⁢
i
i

)

). From the description of the weights in

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}):=\iota^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes\det((\underline{\iota}^{\ast}\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})^{<0})^{\otimes-1}\simeq\bigotimes(pr_{i})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})=:(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})}

Which proves the claim. Notice that this construction depends on the initial choice of
\zeta_{2}
, but also on the choices that we made to write
\oplus_{n}
in terms of
\oplus_{2}
.

Consider now a strong orientation data
K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2}
, with a fixed choice of isomorphism (
7.71
). We do the same construction to define an isomorphism

, i.e. this isomorphism is canonical. Consider now the open substack

, one obtains then from the construction of (
