) a commutative diagram:

\tau^{\ast}\bigl((pr_{(0,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(0,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

\otimes(pr_{(0,0),(0,1)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{(0,0),(1,1)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{(0,1),(1,1)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\bigr)

\Bigl(\tau^{\ast}{\rm Grad}(\iota)^{\ast}\bigl((pr_{0})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

\otimes(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{0,1})^{\ast}L_{\mathcal{M}})\Bigr)|_{\mathcal{X}}

(pr_{(0,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

\otimes(pr_{(0,0),(1,0)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{(0,0),(1,1)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})\otimes(pr_{(1,0),(1,1)})^{\ast}(L_{\mathcal{M}})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bigl(\tau^{\ast}{\rm Grad}(\iota)^{\ast}\iota^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\Bigr)|_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bigl({\rm Grad}(\iota)^{\ast}\bigl((pr_{0})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{0,1})^{\ast}L_{\mathcal{M}})\Bigr)|_{\mathcal{X}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Bigl({\rm Grad}(\iota)^{\ast}\iota^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\Bigr)|_{\mathcal{X}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

From the definition of the isomorphism:

(7.75)

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}((K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}\simeq((K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})^{(\mathbb{Z})})^{(\mathbb{Z})}

in Lemma
6.15

ii)
, one finds then directly that, under the isomorphism (
7.74
), its restriction to
\mathcal{X}
is simply the natural isomorphism:

\displaystyle\tau^{\ast}\bigl((pr_{(0,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(0,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

(7.76)

\displaystyle\simeq

\displaystyle(pr_{(0,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,0)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\otimes(pr_{(1,1)})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})

as claimed. Indeed, in this case, the
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
-weights of the
L
factors are respectively
(-1,0)
,
(-1,-1)
and
(0,-1)
, which are both in
H_{1}\cap H_{2}
, hence there is no need to use (
7.33
) to do the step (
6.77
) in Lemma
6.15

ii)
.

By similar arguments, one can show that the result of
iii)
holds without having to restrict to
\mathcal{X}
if one further assumes that
\zeta_{2}
is symmetric under
\Sigma_{\mathcal{M}}
, i.e. that the following square of isomorphisms is commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}\bigl((pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus L_{\mathcal{M}}\bigr)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\oplus_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(pr_{1})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus(pr_{2})^{\ast}(K_{\mathcal{M}}^{1/2})\oplus L_{\mathcal{M}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Sigma_{\mathcal{M}})^{\ast}(\zeta_{2})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\zeta_{2}}

where the left vertical arrow uses the commutativity isomorphism of
\oplus_{2}
, and the right vertical arrow uses the isomorphism (
7.33
). Notice that, as this is the square root of a commutative diagram, this is always true up to a locally constant sign on
\mathcal{M}\times\mathcal{M}
. As this will not be needed to prove the associativity of the CoHA, and this is not part of the initial definition of strong orientation data in
[
JU21
]
, we didn’t want to impose this condition.

∎

We give now a description of
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})
, adapted from
[
AHLH18
, Corollary 7.13]
:

Lemma 7.10
.

Given a dg-category of finite type
\mathcal{C}
,
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})
is the stack classifying finite extensions of left-proper objects of
\mathcal{C}
. More precisely, given a
k
dg-algebra
\underline{R}
,
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{R})
is the space of objects
(E_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
with
E_{n}\in(\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R}))_{lp}
with a map of degree
1

x:E_{n+1}\to E_{n}
, which stabilize to some left-proper object
E
when
n\ll 0
, and to
0
when
n\gg 0
. Then
\eta
takes the total object
E
, and
p
the
\mathbb{Z}
-graded object
({\rm Cofib}(E_{n+1}\overset{x}{\to}E_{n}))_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
.

Proof.

By descent, objects of
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{R})
are objects of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\Theta_{k})
whose pullback along
{\rm Spec}(R[x])\to\Theta_{\underline{R}}
is left proper. By Rees construction,
{\rm QCoh}(\Theta_{k})
is the dg-category of graded complexes with a map
x
of degree
1
, i.e.
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\Theta_{k})
is the dg-category of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded objects
(E_{n})
of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
with a map
x
of degree
1
. The same arguments that
[
AHLH18
, Corollary 7.13]
gives that the restriction along
B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}\to\Theta_{\underline{R}}
is
((E_{n})_{n},x)\mapsto({\rm Cofib}(x:E_{n+1}\to E_{n}))_{n}
, and the restriction along
{\rm Spec}(\underline{R})\to\Theta_{\underline{R}}
is
((E_{n})_{n},x)\mapsto{\rm colim}(\cdots E_{n+1}\overset{x}{\to}E_{n}\cdots)
.

If
(E_{n})_{n},x)\in{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{R})
, its restriction along
B\mathbb{G}_{m,\underline{R}}\to\Theta_{\underline{R}}
is in
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M})(\underline{R})
, i.e. the
{\rm Cofib}(x:E_{n+1}\to E_{n})
are left-proper, and only a finite number of them is nonvanishing: in particular
E_{n}
stabilize for
n\ll 0
to
E=\eta(((E_{n})_{n},x))
, and it stabilize for
n\gg 0
to some object
F
, that we claim to be zero. Indeed, consider a compact generator
K
of
\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
, and consider the sequence
K_{r}\in\mathcal{C}\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R}[x])
of objects:
\cdots K\overset{Id}{\to}K\overset{0}{\to}0\overset{0}{\to}0\cdots
where the last
K
is in degree
r
, such that
{\rm colim}_{r}K\simeq 0
. As
((E_{n})_{n},x)
is compact, we have
{\rm colim}_{r}{\rm Hom}_{R[x]}(((E_{n})_{n},x),K_{r})\simeq 0
, but, for
n\gg 0
, the former if
{\rm Hom}_{\underline{R}}(F,K)
, hence, as
K
is a compact generator,
F\simeq 0
. In particular, each
E_{n}
is left-proper, as it is a finite extension of left-proper objects. Hence each object of
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{R})
is of the claimed form.

On the other hand, an object as in the Lemma is obtained by a finite number of extensions from the left-proper objects
{\rm Cofib}(x:E_{n+1}\to E_{n})\otimes_{\underline{R}}\underline{R}[x]\langle-n\rangle
(where
\langle-n\rangle
denote a shift of the grading), hence it is left proper, i.e. is an object of
{\rm Filt}(\underline{\mathcal{M}})(\underline{R})
.

∎

7.3.2.
Moduli of object in the heart of a
t
-structure

We consider now the case where
X
is a smooth and projective scheme, and
\mathcal{C}={\rm QCoh}(X)
,
\mathcal{C}_{c}={\rm Perf}(X)=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
. The results presented here could surely be obtained for smooth and proper dg-category
\mathcal{C}
, by generalizing existing techniques, but we stick to this case to keep this section short. In the non-proper case, there are some difficulties linked with the fact that left-proper objects do not generates
\mathcal{C}
, an the fact that one cannot extend a left-proper object along an open immersion (look at the diagonal family
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\in D^{b}{\rm Coh}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
, that cannot be extended to a left-proper object of
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\mathbb{A}^{1})
). We leave in particular the case of a smooth quasi-projective variety
X
for future works. As
{\rm QCoh}(X)
is smooth and proper, the small dg-subcategory
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
of compact objects is also the subcategory of left-proper objects. Similarly, from
[
TV05
, Lemma 2.8]
, for any dg-algebra
\underline{R}
,
\mathcal{M}(\underline{R})
classifies left-proper objects of
{\rm QCoh}(X_{\underline{R}})={\rm QCoh}(X)\otimes_{k}{\rm QCoh}(\underline{R})
, which are exactly the compact objects, i.e. objects of
{\rm Perf}(X_{\underline{R}})=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)\otimes_{k}{\rm Perf}(\underline{R})
.

Consider now a
t
-structure
(D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\leq 0},D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\geq 0})
on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, with Abelian heart denoted by
\mathcal{A}_{c}
, which is assumed to be nondegenerate, i.e. :

(7.77)

\displaystyle\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\leq n}=\bigcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\geq-n}=D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)

(by definition, this condition is always satisfied for any heart from a Bridgeland stability condition). We denote by
({\rm QCoh}(X)^{\leq 0},{\rm QCoh}(X)^{\geq 0})
its extension to a an accessible
t
-structure on
{\rm QCoh}(X)
built from
[
Lur12
, Prop. 1.4.11]
, i.e.
{\rm QCoh}(X)^{\leq 0}
is the presentable subcategory of
{\rm QCoh}(X)
generated under colimits and extensions by
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\leq 0}
. From
[
Pol07
, Lemma 2.1.1]
, we have:

(7.78)

\displaystyle D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{[a,b]}={\rm QCoh}(X)^{[a,b]}\cap D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)

hence the truncation functors preserves
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, and preserves filtered colimits by
[
Lur12
, Prop. 1.4.13]
, and
\mathcal{A}_{c}=\mathcal{A}\cap D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
. The objects of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
are compacts in
\mathcal{C}
, and then furthermore compacts in
\mathcal{A}
, they are stable by extensions and retracts in
\mathcal{A}
, and, as the truncation functors preserve the compact objects, they generates
\mathcal{A}
: it gives that
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is the Abelian subcategory of compact objects of
\mathcal{A}
. In particular, we will use the results of
[
AHLH18
, Section 7.2]
(where compact objects are called finitely presented).

A small Abelian category is said to be Noetherian if every object is Noetherian (i.e. any sequence of subobjects stablilize). A presentable Abelian category
\mathcal{A}
is said to be locally Noetherian if it has a set of Noetherian generators: in this case, from
[
AZ01
, Proposition B1.3]
, Noetherian and compact objects of
\mathcal{A}
coincide. In particular,
\mathcal{A}
is locally Noetherian iff
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is Noetherian. From
[
AP06
, Proposition 5.0.1]
, this is satisfies for any heart of a Bridgeland stability condition for which
Z(\Gamma)\in\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}
, i.e. for a dense subset in the space of stability conditions.

We will now explain how to define a substack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
of
\mathcal{M}
classifying objects of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
, following and slightly adapting
[
HL14
, Section 6.2]
and
[
AHLH18
, Section 7]
. Namely, in
[
HL14
, Section 6]
, Halpern-Leistner, build
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
as a substack of the classical 1-stack of perfectly gluable complexes from
[
Lie05
]
: we will consider instead their stack as a substack of the stack
\mathcal{M}
from
[
TV05
]
which will gives us a derived enhancement. In
[
AHLH18
, Section 7]
, the authors works with locally Noetherian Abelian categories and build a functor classifying compact objects in them, which is proven in
[
HL14
, Proposition 6.2.7]
to agree with
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

For
R
a
k
-algebra, we have that
{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})
is naturally identified with the
R
-algebra objects of
{\rm QCoh}(X)
. In
[
HL14
, Definition 6.1.1]
, Halpern-Leistner defines a
t
-structure on
{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})
whose heart corresponds from
[
HL14
, Proposition 6.1.7]
to the Abelian subcategory
\mathcal{A}_{R}
of
R
-algebra objects of
\mathcal{A}
. An object
E\in{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})
is said to be flat if
E\otimes_{R}-:{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})\to{\rm QCoh}(R)
is exact with respect to this
t
-structure.

Definition 7.11
.

(
[
HL14
, Definition 6.2.4]
,
[
AP06
, Prob. 3.5.1]
)
Given a smooth and projective scheme
X
, a
t
-structure on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
is said to satisfy generic flatness if, given a domain
R
of finite type over
k
with fraction field
K
and an object
E\in D^{b}(X_{R})
such that
E|_{K}\in\mathcal{A}_{K}
, there is an
f\in R
such that
E|_{R_{f}}
is flat.

We define then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)\subset\mathcal{M}
to be the subspace of flat objects of
{\rm Perf}(X_{R})
. We will then use this result, using the results of
[
AP06
]
,
[
Pol07
]
in a fundamental way:

Proposition 7.12
.

(
[
HL14
, Proposition 6.2.7]
)
Consider a smooth and projective scheme
X
, and a nondegenerate
t
-structure on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, satisfying generic flatness, with Noetherian heart
\mathcal{A}_{c}
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}
: in particular, it is an Artin
1
-stack, locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
coincide with the stack of
[
AHLH18
, Definition 7.8]
, i.e. the stack of compacts objects
E
of
\mathcal{A}_{R}
such that
H^{0}(E\boxtimes-):R-Mod\to\mathcal{A}_{R}
is exact. When
R
is of finite type,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)
coincide with the objects
E\in\mathcal{M}
such that
E|_{x}\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
for each closed point
x\in{\rm Spec}(R)
.

Indeed,
[
HL14
, Proposition 6.2.7]
establish that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an open substack of the
1
-stack of universally gluable relative perfect complexes from
[
Lie05
]
, which from
[
TV05
, Remark 3.30]
, coincide with the open substack of
\mathcal{M}
of complexes
E\in{\rm Perf}(X_{R})
such that
Ext^{i}_{R}(E,E)=0
for
i<0
. The statement about affineness of the diagonal follows from
[
AHLH18
, Lemma 7.20]
. The condition that the
t
structure is bounded with respect to the usual
t
-structure is automatic, thanks to the following lemma (which is maybe known to experts):

Lemma 7.13
.

Given a smooth and projective scheme
X
, any nondegenerate
t
-structure on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
satisfying generic flatness, with Noetherian heart, is bounded with respect to the usual
t
-structure.

Proof.

Consider the substack
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X)^{[a,b]}}
of
\mathcal{M}
such that, for
R
of finite type,
\mathcal{M}_{[a,b]}
is the subspace of
E\in{\rm Perf}(X_{R})
such that
E|_{x}\in{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})^{[a,b]}
for any closed point
x
(one can define it, as
\mathcal{M}
is locally of finite presentation, hence it is determined by its value on
k
-agebras of finite type). Given a domain
R
of finite type over
k
with fraction field
K
and an object
E\in D^{b}(X_{R})
such that
E|_{K}\in D^{b}(X_{R})^{[a,b]}
, using generic flatness and the fact that restriction to an open subset is
t
-exact, there is an
f\in R
such that
H^{i}(E)|_{R_{f}}=H^{i}(E|_{R_{f}})
is flat for
a\leq i\leq b
. Then, for each closed point
x\in{\rm Spec}(R_{f})
,
H^{i}(E|_{R_{f}})|_{x}\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
: but
E|_{R_{f}}
is generated by the
H^{i}(E|_{R_{f}})[i]
under extensions, hence
E|_{x}
is generated by the
H^{i}(E|_{R_{f}})|_{x}[i]
for
a\leq i\leq b
, then
E|_{x}\in{\rm QCoh}(X_{R})^{[a,b]}
. Using the fact that the truncation functors preserves
D^{b}(X_{R})
for
R
of finite type from
[
Pol07
, Theorem 3.3.6]
, we find as in
[
HL14
, Corollary 6.2.3]
that, for any
R
of finite type and
E\in\mathcal{M}(R)
, the set:

(7.79)

\displaystyle U=\{\mathfrak{p}\in{\rm Spec}(R)|E|_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}\in}D^{b}(X_{R_{\mathfrak{p}}})^{[a,b]}\}

is open in
{\rm Spec}(R)
. Then, one argue by Noetherian induction as in
[
HL14
, Proposition 6.2.7]
, giving that
\mathcal{M}_{[a,b]}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}
.

Consider the diagonal family
\mathcal{O}_{\Delta}\in{\rm Perf}(X\times X)
, seen as an object of
\mathcal{M}(X)
. As
X
is of finite type, from the above, the set of closed points
x\in X(k)
such that
\mathcal{O}_{x}\in D^{b}(X)^{[a,b]}
is open. As the
t
-structure is nondegenerate, and
X
is quasi-compact, there is some
a,b
such that
\mathcal{O}_{x}\in D^{b}(X)^{[a,b]}
for any
x\in X(k)
. We use then a well-known argument from
[
Bri03
, Lemma 10.1]
. Consider
E\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
and
x\in X(k)
: from the orthogonality of the
t
-structure, for any
i<a
,
{\rm Ext}^{i}(E,\mathcal{O}_{x})=0)
, and, for any
i>b+n
,
{\rm Ext}^{i}(E,\mathcal{O}_{x})={\rm Ext}^{n-i}(\mathcal{O}_{x},E)^{\vee}=0
by Serre duality. Then, from
[
BM99
, Proposition 5.4]
,
E
has perfect amplitude in
[a,b+n]
with respect to the usual
t
-structure, and this range does not depend on
E\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
, i.e. the
t
structure
(D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\leq 0},D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)^{\geq 0})
is bounded with respect to the usual
t
-structure.
∎

We have moreover:

Lemma 7.14
.

(
[
HL14
, Lemma 6.3.1]
,
[
AHLH18
, Proposition 7.12, Corollary 7.13,Lemma 7.17]
)
Consider a smooth and projective scheme
X
, and a nondegenerate
t
-structure on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, with Noetherian heart
\mathcal{A}
, satisfying generic flatness. Then
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
is the stack of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded points of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
(i.e.
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})(R)
is the stack of
\mathbb{Z}
-graded objects
(E_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{R}
, with each
E_{n}
compact and
E_{n}=0
unless for a finite number of
n
), and the map
\iota:{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
takes the direct sum.
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
is the stack of
\mathbb{Z}
-filtered points of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
(i.e.
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})(R)
is the stack of
\mathbb{Z}
-filtered objects
(E_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
of
\mathcal{A}_{R}
, with each
E_{n}
compact and
E_{n}/E_{n+1}=0
unless for a finite number of
n
), the map
\eta:{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
takes the total object, and the map
p:{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
takes the associated graded
(E_{n}/E_{n+1})_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}
. Moreover,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is
\Theta
-reductive, i.e.
\eta:{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the valuative criterion for properness with respects to any DVR.

Proof.

Except for
\Theta
-reductivity, this is
[
HL14
, Lemma 6.3.1]
,
[
AHLH18
, Proposition 7.12, Corollary 7.13]
. In
[
AHLH18
, Lemma 7.17]
, the author shows that
\eta
satisfies the valuative criterion for properness with respects to any DVR which is essentially of finite type. As
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is locally of finite presentation, we will argue as in
[
AHLH18
, Proposition 3.18]
to show that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
is
\Theta
-reductive. Consider any DVR
R
with fraction field
K
, and a map
f:{\rm Spec}(R)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and
g:{\rm Spec}(K)\to{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
, with
\eta\circ g\simeq f|_{K}
. As
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is locally of finite presentation,
f
factorize through an affine scheme of finite type
{\rm Spec}(A)
: the base change of
\eta
along
{\rm Spec}(A)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
is then a morphism algebraic space as
\eta
is representable, which implies from
[
AHLH18
, Lemma A.11]
that it satisfies the valuative criterion with respect to any DVR, which implies that
f,g
extends uniquely to a morphism
{\rm Spec}(R)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
, which proves the claim.
∎

Remark 7.15
.

From
[
STV11
, Proposition 2.1]
, there is a unique open immersion
\underline{j}:\underline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}\to\underline{\mathcal{M}}
with classical truncation
j
, giving a derived enhancement
\underline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
. In particular, given a Calabi-Yau structure
\omega_{X}\simeq\mathcal{O}_{X}
on
X
of dimension
n
on
X
, there is a canonical Calabi-You structure of dimension
n
on
{\rm QCoh}(X)
from
[
BD16
, Proposition 5.12]
, which gives from
[
BD18
, Theorem 5.5 1)]
a canonical
n-2
-shifted symplectic form on
\underline{\mathcal{M}}
, and then on
\underline{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}
. When
n=3
, we obtain from
[
BBBBJ15
, Theorem 3.18]
a canonical
d
-critical structure
s
on
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
, with canonical bundle:

(7.80)

\displaystyle K_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},s}\simeq\det(\mathbb{L}_{\underline{\mathcal{M}}})|_{(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})^{red}}=:K_{\mathcal{M}}|_{(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})^{red}}

with the preceding notation. In particular, an the orientation data on
\mathcal{M}
from
[
JU21
]
gives an isomorphism class of orientation on
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
compatible with direct sum, and a strong orientation data on
\mathcal{M}
would give a canonical orientation on
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}},s)
, compatible with direct sum.

For
E,F\in D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, recall the pairing:

(7.81)

\displaystyle\langle E,F\rangle:=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}(-1)^{i+1}\dim({\rm Ext}^{i}(E,F))

which is antisymmetric when
X
is CY3, by Serre duality. This pairing descends to an pairing on the Grothendieck group
K(X)=K(D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X))
. As in
[
Bri07
]
, we denote by
K^{num}(X)
the quotient of
K(X)
by the kernel of this pairing: by Grothendieck-Hirzebruch-Riemann-Roch, this is a finite dimensional lattice. We argue now as in
[
HL14
, Lemma 6.4.1]
. For
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)
, and
R
is of finite type, we consider the subspace
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X),\gamma}(R)\subset\mathcal{M}(R)
of objects
E\in{\rm Perf}(X_{R})
such that
[E|_{x}]=\gamma
for every closed point of
{\rm Spec}(R)
. Consider now
E\in\mathcal{M}(R)
, and
F\in D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
: as
E
is left-proper,
Hom_{R}(E,F\otimes_{k}R)\in{\rm Perf}(R)
, i.e.
\langle E|_{x},F\rangle
is locally constant on
{\rm Spec}(R)
, i.e. the class
[E|_{x}]\in K^{num}(X)
is locally constant on
{\rm Spec}(R)
. As
\mathcal{M}
is locally of finite presentation, it defines an open and closed substack
\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X),\gamma}
of
\mathcal{M}
, such that
\mathcal{M}=\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in K^{num}(X)}\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X),\gamma}
, and
\oplus_{n}
restricts to:

(7.82)

\displaystyle\oplus_{n}:\prod_{1\leq i\leq n}\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X),\gamma_{i}}\to\mathcal{M}_{{\rm QCoh}(X),\sum_{1\leq i\leq n}\gamma_{i}}

Now, the intersection with the open substack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
gives open and closed substacks
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma}
with the same properties.

7.4.
Kontsevich-Soibelman wall crossing formula

We consider now the space of Bridgeland stability condition
{\rm Stab}(X)
on a smooth and projective scheme
X
, from
[
Bri07
]
. More precisely, we consider the space of numerical Bridgeland stability conditions on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
satisfying the support property from
[
KS08
]
.

Definition 7.16
.

A stability condition
\sigma=(Z,(\mathcal{P}(\phi)))
in
{\rm Stab}(X)
is the data of a group homomorphism
Z:K^{num}(X)\to\mathbb{C}
called a central charge, and full additive subcategories
\mathcal{P}(\phi)\subset D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
for each
\phi\in\mathbb{R}
, whose objects (resp simple objects) are called semistable(resp stable) of phase
\phi
, satisfying:

i)

If
E\in\mathcal{P}(\phi)
, then
Z(E)=m(E)e^{i\pi\phi}
for some
m(E)\in\mathbb{R}_{>0}
(we write
Z(E):=Z([E])
).

ii)

For all
\phi\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{P}(\phi+1)=\mathcal{P}(\phi)[1]
.

iii)

If
\phi_{1}>\phi_{2}
and
A_{j}\in\mathcal{P}(\phi_{j})
then
{\rm Ext}^{0}_{\mathcal{D}}(A_{1},A_{2})=0
.

iv)

For each nonzero object
E\in D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
there are a finite sequence of real numbers
\phi_{1}>\phi_{2}>...>\phi_{n}
and a collection of triangles:

(7.83)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0=E_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces...}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{n-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{n}=E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{n}}

with
A_{j}\in\mathcal{P}(\phi_{j})
for all
j
. This decomposition (which is unique from
iii)
) is called the Harder-Narasimhan decomposition.

v)

(support property) For
\|\cdot\|
a norm on
K^{num}(X)
, there is a constant
C>0
such that
\|\gamma\|\leq C|Z(\gamma)|
for
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)
such that there is a semistable object of classs
\gamma
(this condition is independent from the norm, as
K^{num}(X)
is finite dimensional). Equivalently, there is quadratic form
Q
on
K^{num}(X)
such that
Q|_{\ker(Z)}=0
, and, if there is an object
E\in\mathcal{P}(\phi)
of numerical class
\gamma
, then
Q(\gamma)\geq 0
(it suffices to take
Q(\gamma)=-\|\gamma\|^{2}+C^{2}|Z(\gamma)|^{2}
).

Recall that, from
[
Bri07
]
,
{\rm Stab}(X)
has a natural structure of metric space (where the metric is given by comparing the extremal slopes of Harder-Narasimhan filtrations), such that
Z:{\rm Stab}(X)\to Hom(K^{num}(X),\mathbb{C})
is a local homeomorphism, giving furthermore a structure of complex analytic space on
{\rm Stab}(X)
.

For any interval
I
, we denote as usual by
\mathcal{P}(I)
the full subcategory of objects
E\in D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
whose direct factors have slope in
I
(equivalently, it is the full subcategory generated by extensions from the
\mathcal{P}(\phi)
for
\phi\in I
. In particular, for
\phi\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathcal{P}([\phi,\phi+1))
and
\mathcal{P}((\phi,\phi+1])
are the heart of nondegenerate
t
-structures on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
. We consider then
\mathcal{M}_{I}^{\sigma}
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}^{\sigma}
), the substack of
\mathcal{M}
such that, for
R
of finite type,
\mathcal{M}_{I}^{\sigma}(R)
(resp.
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}^{\sigma}(R)
) is the subspace of
E\in\mathcal{M}(R)
such that, for any closed point
x\in{\rm Spec}(R)
,
E|_{x}\in\mathcal{P}(I)
(resp. and
[E|_{x}]=\gamma
). Notice that
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}^{\sigma}
is an open and closed substack of
\mathcal{M}_{I}^{\sigma}
. For an interval of
I
length
<1
, we consider the associated strict sector
V:=\{me^{i\pi\phi}|m>0\;\rm{and}\;\}
of
\mathbb{C}
. Notice that, given a strict sector
V
, the corresponding interval
I
is only well defined up to a shift of
2
: in particular, the corresponding stacks
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I+2k}
are isomorphic as stacks, but different as substacks of
\mathcal{M}
(the embedding differ by a shift of
[2k]
). For
\phi\in\mathbb{R}
, consider the half line
l_{\phi}:=\{me^{i\pi\phi}|m\geq 0\}
, such that
\mathcal{M}_{\phi}^{\sigma}=\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in Z^{-1}(l_{\phi})}\mathcal{M}_{\phi,\gamma}^{\sigma}
, and, for
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)-\{0\}
, we denote also by
l_{\gamma}
the half-line containing
Z(\gamma)
.

We recall the terminology of
[
Tod07
]
,
[
PT19
]
:

Definition 7.17
.

Consider a smooth and projective scheme
X
, and a stability condition
\sigma\in{\rm Stab}(X)
.

•

We say that
\sigma
is algebraic if
Z(K^{num}(X))\in\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}
. As
K^{num}(X)
is finite dimensional, such stability conditions are dense in
{\rm Stab}(X)
, and, by
[
AP06
, Proposition 5.0.1]
, for an algebraic stability condition,
\mathcal{P}((0,1])
is Noetherian.

•

We say that
\sigma
is bounded if, for any
\phi\in(0,1]
and
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)
,
\mathcal{M}_{\phi,\gamma}^{\sigma}
is bounded, i.e. admits a surjection from a scheme of finite type.

•

We say that
\sigma
satisfies generic flatness if the
t
-structure defining the heart
\mathcal{P}((0,1])
satisfies it (see Definition
7.11
).

The main result about this is the following:

Proposition 7.18
.

(
[
PT19
, Proposition 4.12, Corollary 4.21]
)
Consider a connected component
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
of
{\rm Stab}(X)
. Then, if an algebraic stability condition of
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
satisfies boundedness and generic flatness, then any algebraic stability condition of
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
satisfies boundedness and generic flatness (we will call such a component a good component). When
X
is a Calabi-Yau threefold, the connected components containing the stability conditions from
[
BMT11
]
(using strong Bogomolov-Gieseker inequalities) are good.

We follow now the discussion of
[
KS08
, Section 2]
,
[
KS13
, Section 2.3]
. Consider the ring
N_{mon}
of monodromic Nori motives completed at
\mathbb{L}^{-1/2}
from Section
4.3
, and the motivic quantum torus:

(7.84)

\displaystyle G:=N_{mon}\langle(x^{\gamma})_{\gamma\in K^{num}(X)}\rangle/((x^{\gamma}\cdot x^{\gamma^{\prime}}-\mathbb{L}^{\langle\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle/2}x^{\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}})_{\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\in K^{num}(X)},x^{0}-1)

We see
G
as a multiplicative group, with graded Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}:=\bigoplus_{\gamma\in\Gamma}
, with Lie bracket
[x^{\gamma},x^{\gamma^{\prime}}]=(\mathbb{L}^{\langle\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle/2}-\mathbb{L}^{-\langle\gamma,\gamma^{\prime}\rangle/2})x^{\gamma+\gamma^{\prime}}
.

Consider a quadratic form
Q
on
K^{num}(X)
as in
v)
of Definition
7.16
. For a strict sector
V
of
\mathbb{C}
, consider then the convex cone of
K^{num}(X)
:

(7.85)

\displaystyle C(V,Z,Q)=\{\gamma\in K^{num}(X)|Z(\gamma)\in V,m>0,\phi\in I)\;\rm{and}\;Q(\gamma)\geq 0\}

it is a strict convex cone, i.e. its closure does not contain any line through the origin. By the support property, we have:

(7.86)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}_{I}^{\sigma}=\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in C(V,Z,Q)}\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}^{\sigma}

We consider then
\bigoplus_{\gamma\in C(V,Z,Q)}\mathfrak{g}_{\gamma}
, and its completion
\mathfrak{g}_{V,Z,Q}:=\prod_{\gamma\in C(V,Z,Q)}\mathfrak{g}_{\gamma}
, which is a pro-nilpotent Lie algebra, as
C(V,Z,Q)
is a strict cone. One consider then the pro-nilpotent group
G_{V,Z,Q}:=\exp(\mathfrak{g}_{V,Z,Q})
. Consider triangles
\Delta
cut out from the strict sector
V
by a line,
\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta}:=\oplus_{\gamma\in Z^{-1}(\Delta)}\mathfrak{g}_{\gamma}
(which contains a finite number of elements as
C(V,Z,Q)
is strict). The pro-nilpotent topology is given explicitly by
\mathfrak{g}_{V,Z,Q}=\lim_{\Delta}\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta}
, and, denoting
G_{\Delta}:=\exp(\mathfrak{g}_{\Delta})
, we have
G_{V,Z,Q}=\lim_{\Delta}G_{\Delta}
.

We prove then the Kontsevich-Soibelman wall crossing formula from
[
KS08
]
, applying Theorem
7.5
to the
\Theta
-stratification built by Halpern-Leistner in
[
HL14
, Theorem 5.3]
:

Theorem 7.19
.

Consider a smooth and projective Calabi-Yau threefold
X
, and a good connected component
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
of the space of stability condition.

i)

Consider
\sigma\in{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
and an interval
I
of length
<1
with associated strict sector
V
. Then
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}
,
\mathcal{M}_{\phi,\gamma}
are oriented
d
-critical Artin
1
-stacks of finite type with affine diagonals, for any choice of quadratic form
Q
in the support property,
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}=\emptyset
if
\gamma\not\in C(V,Z,Q)
, and we have the following equality in
G(V,Z,Q)
(the Kontsevich-Soibelman wall crossing formula):

(7.87)

\displaystyle A^{\sigma}_{V,Q}:=\sum_{\gamma\in C(V,Z,Q)}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma},P_{\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}})]x^{\gamma}=\prod_{\phi\in I}^{\to}\sum_{\gamma\in C(l_{\phi},Z,Q)}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\phi,\gamma},P_{\mathcal{M}_{\phi,\gamma}})]x^{\gamma}=:\prod_{l\subset V}^{\to}A^{\sigma}_{l}

where the symbol
\prod_{\phi\in I}^{\to}
(resp.
\prod_{l\in V}^{\to}
) denotes an oriented product on increasing
\phi\in I
(resp on the half-lines
l\subset V
in the trigonometric order).

ii)

The family of stability data
(Z,(\log(A^{\sigma}_{l_{\gamma}})_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in K^{num}_{Z}}))
defines a continuous family of stability data on
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
in the sense of
[
KS08
, Definition 3]
(in particular, it defines a wall crossing structure on
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
in the sense of
[
KS13
, Definition 2.2.1]
).

Stability structures from
[
KS08
]
, and their generalization, the wall-crossing structures from
[
KS13
]
, are very versatile structures, that allows in particular to build scattering diagrams on
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
encoding the Donaldson-Thomas invariants. We refer to
[
KS08
]
and
[
KS13
]
for more details.

Proof.

i)

Consider
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)
and a closed point
E\in\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}(k)
: by definition, the Harder-Narasimhan factors are semistable objects of class
\gamma_{i}
and phase
\phi_{i}\in I
such that
\gamma=\sum_{i}\gamma_{i}
. From the support property,
Q(\gamma_{i})\geq 0
, which implies that
\gamma_{i}\in C(I,Z,Q)
, and then, as
C(I,Z,Q)
is a cone,
\gamma\in C(I,Z,Q)
, i.e.
\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}=\emptyset
if
\gamma\not\in C(I,Z,Q)
.

Consider any triangle
\Delta
in
V
cut out from a line, and the set
S:=Z^{-1}(\Delta)\cap C(V,Z,Q)
: as
C(V,Z,Q)
is a strict cone, it is finite. Moreover, from
[
Tod07
, Proposition 2.8]
, there is a locally finite real wall and chamber structure on
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
such that the set of semistable object of class
\gamma\in S
are constant on the interior of each component of the intersection of walls. Notice that those walls are necessarily pulled back from rational walls of
Hom(K^{num}(X),\mathbb{C})
, hence algebraic stability conditions are dense in any intersection of walls. Then we can find an algebraic stability condition
\sigma^{\prime}
in
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
such that the
\gamma
-semistable objects of
\sigma
and
\sigma^{\prime}
are the same for any
\gamma\in S
. Then, as any
\gamma_{i}
appearing in the Harder-Narasimhan decomposition of a closed point of
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}
is in
\Delta
if
\gamma\in\Delta
, we have that
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}=\mathcal{M}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{I,\gamma}
for any
\gamma\in\Delta
. Then, by the definition of the profinite completion, we can suppose that
\sigma
is an algebraic stability condition, in particular it is Noetherian. From Piyaratne-Toda’s Proposition
7.18
,
\sigma
satisfies moreover boundedness and generic flatness. From
[
Tod07
, Lemma 3.15]
, it implies that
\sigma
boundedness of quotients as in
[
HL14
, Definition 6.5.2]
.

As
\Delta
contains a finite number of class, one can find
m_{1}e^{i\pi\phi_{1}},m_{2}e^{i\pi\phi_{2}}\in\mathbb{Q}+i\mathbb{Q}
with
\phi^{\prime}-\phi\leq 1
such that, for
\gamma\in\Delta
,
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}=\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}],\gamma}
. But, by definition,
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}],\gamma}=\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1],\gamma}\times_{\mathcal{M}}\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{2},\phi_{2}+1],\gamma}
. From Halpern-Leistner’s Proposition,
7.12
applied to stability conditions obtained from
\sigma
by the action of
\tilde{Gl}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
,
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}
is an open substack of
\mathcal{M}
, which is an Artin 1-stack, locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal, which inherits as in Remark
7.15
a natural
d
-critical structure, and a natural isomorphism class of orientation, compatible with direct sum (notice that these does not depends of any choices made here). The same reasoning applies to
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{\phi,\gamma}
for any
\gamma\in S
: moreover, those are bounded by assumption, hence
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{\phi,\gamma}
is quasi-compact and locally of finite presentation, i.e. it is of finite type. Then the set of
\sigma
-semistable objects of slope
\phi\in I
and class
\gamma\in S
is bounded, and there is some
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that any object of
\mathcal{P}(I)
of class
\gamma\in S
is obtained from an extension of at most
N
such elements: then, applying
N-1
times
[
Tod07
, Lemma 3.16]
, we find that
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}
is also bounded, hence of finite type, for any
\gamma\in S
.

We apply now
[
HL14
, Theorem 6.5.3]
to a shift of
\sigma
under the action of
\tilde{Gl}_{2}(\mathbb{Q})
, which is still algebraic. It gives a
\Theta
-stratification on
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1),\gamma}
, which gives on closed points the Harder-Narasimhan decomposition of objects of
\mathcal{P}(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1)
of class
\gamma
. More precisely, the totally ordered set
\Gamma
is the set of vectors
(\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r})
such that
\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{r}=\gamma
, with a total order extending the partial order
(\sum_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}_{1}}\gamma_{1,j},\cdots,\sum_{j=1}^{r^{\prime}_{r}}\gamma_{r,j})\leq(\gamma_{1,1},\cdots,\gamma_{r,r^{\prime}_{r}})
. The center
\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r})}
of the corresponding strata is the open substack
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}}
of
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1),\gamma})
, and
\Theta_{(\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{n})}
is the open substack of
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1),\gamma})
of filtrations
(E_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
E_{i}/E_{i+1}
is semistable of class
\gamma_{i}
for
1\leq i\leq r
, and is
0
otherwise. In particular,
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}
is by definition the union of the open strata
(\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{(\phi_{1},\phi_{1}+1),\gamma})_{\leq(\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r})}
for
\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{r}\in C(V,Z,Q)
, then from
[
HL14
, Lemma 2.3.2]
, there is an induced
\Theta
-stratification on it, with the same description than above.

We apply now Theorem
7.5
to the
d
-critical Artin
1
-stack with affine diagonal, locally of finite type
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I,\gamma}
, with its isomorphism class of orientation from
[
JU21
]
, which gives:

(7.88)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma},P_{\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}})]=\sum_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})\in C(I,Z,Q)^{r}}\mathbb{L}^{{\rm Ind}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})}/2}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})},P_{\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})}})]

But, from Lemma
7.9

i)
,
iii)
, given a choice of orientation in the isomorphism class, the oriented
d
-critical stack
\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})}
is isomorphic to
\prod_{i=1}^{n}\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}}
, then Corollary
5.9
gives:

(7.89)

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})},P_{\mathcal{Z}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})}})]=\prod_{i=1}^{r}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}},P_{\mathcal{M}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}}})]

and Lemma
7.9

ii)
gives:

(7.90)

\displaystyle{\rm Ind}_{(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})}=\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq r}\langle\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j}\rangle

hence by the definition of the quantum affine torus (
7.84
) we have:

\displaystyle[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma},P_{\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}})]x^{\gamma}=\sum_{r\geq 1}\sum_{\begin{tabular}[]{c}$(\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r})\in C(V,Z,Q)^{r}$\\
$\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{r}=\gamma$\end{tabular}}\prod_{i=1}^{r}[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}},P_{\mathcal{M}_{\phi_{i},\gamma_{i}}})]x^{\gamma_{i}}

By definition of the profinite completion, this finishes the proof of
i)
. Notice that
A^{\sigma}_{V},A^{\sigma}_{l}
are independent from the choice of
I,\phi
, as the stacks
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{I+2k,\gamma},\mathcal{M}^{\sigma}_{\phi+2k,\gamma}
are independent from
k
as oriented
d
-critical stacks.

ii)

We check the conditions
a),b),c)
of
[
KS08
, Definition 3]
. By definition of the topology on
{\rm Stab}(X)
,
Z
is continuous, hence
a)
is satisfied. Consider
\sigma\in{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
: literally, the condition of
b)
is equivalent to the fact that, if a quadratic form
Q
gives the support property for
\sigma
, then there is an open neighborhood
U
of
\sigma
such that
Q
gives the support property for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
. This condition is too strong, and has no chance to hold in general. We replace it by the slightly weaker condition that, for any
\sigma
, there is a neighborhood
U
of
\sigma
and a quadratic form
Q
which gives the support property for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
: one checks directly that all the arguments of
[
KS08
, Section 2]
can be proven also in this setting. From
[
BM11
, Lemma 5.5.4]
, for a choice of norm
\|\cdot\|
on
K^{num}(X)
, there is an open neighborhood
U
of
\sigma
and
C<\infty
such that, for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
, one can take the constant to be
C
in the support property, hence the quadratic form
Q(\gamma)=-\|\gamma\|^{2}+C^{2}|Z(\gamma)|^{2}
works for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
.

We check now
c)
. Consider a closed interval
I=[\phi_{1},\phi_{2}]
and
\sigma\in{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(U)
. For any
\gamma
,
\log(A^{\sigma}_{l_{\gamma}})_{\gamma}
depends only on the restriction of
A^{\sigma}_{l_{\gamma}}
to a finite subset of
C(l,Z,Q)
, hence is independent from the choice of
Q
. We have to show that, given
\sigma\in{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
and a choice of
Q
as above, given
I=[\phi_{1},\phi_{2}]
such that, for
\sigma
,
\mathcal{P}(\phi_{1}),\mathcal{P}(\phi_{2})
are empty, then
A^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{V,Q}
is continuous at
\sigma
. Consider a triangle
\Delta\subset V
, and the associated bounded mass subset
S:=Z^{-1}(\Delta)\cap C(V,Z,Q)
. From
[
Tod07
, Proposition 2.8]
, there is
\epsilon>0
and an open neighborhood
U
of
\sigma
(which we takes to be connected, and on which
Q
gives the support property) such that, for any
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
, there are no
\sigma^{\prime}
-semistable objects of phase
\phi\in[\phi_{1}-\epsilon,\phi_{1}+\epsilon]\cup[\phi_{2}-\epsilon,\phi_{2}+\epsilon]
and class
\gamma\in S
. As
U
is connected, by the definition of the topology of
{\rm Stab}(X)
, the Harder-Narasimhan factors of any
\sigma^{\prime}
-semistable object of phase
\phi\in I
and class
\gamma\in S
for any other
\sigma^{\prime\prime}\in U
have also their phase in
I
. It means then that, for any
\gamma\in S
,
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{I,\gamma}
, and then
[\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma},P_{\mathcal{M}_{I,\gamma}})]
is independent from
\sigma^{\prime}\in U
. By definition of the profinite completion, it means exactly that
A^{\sigma^{\prime}}_{V,Q}
is continuous at
\sigma
.

∎

7.5.
Construction of the Cohomological Hall algebra

Cohomological Hall algebras were first introduced in
[
KS10
]
, with inspiration from the classical theory of Ringel-Hall algebras
[
Rin90
]
. In
[
KS10
]
, Kontsevich and Soibelman have defined a Cohomological Hall algebra for quiver with potentials (see also
[
Dav17
]
,
[
DM16
]
for latter development in this case). They use crucially the fact that the moduli stacks are in this case global critical loci, hence there is no need to use the gluing formalism from
[
BBD+15
]
, inspired from
[
KS08
]
. The case of compact Calabi-Yau has remained opened during a long time, due to the difficulties in gluing. The major approach to this problem was through the so-called Joyce conjecture from Joyce and Safronov
[
JS19
, Conjecture 1.1]
, using the fact that the map from the stack of short exact sequence
0\to E\to F\to G\to
to
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a
-1
Lagrangian in the
-1
-shifted symplectic stack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
. However, proving the Joyce conjectures amount to glue morphisms in the derived category of constructible sheaves (or monodromic mixed Hodge modules), which is require to do homotopy coherent constructions. In fact, it is possible to do that more easily, using the hyperbolic localization isomorphism from Theorem
6.14
, which is an isomorphism in an Abelian category, hence easier to build. A said in the introduction, this idea was also recently used by Kinjo, Park and Safronov in
[
KPS24
]
, in an independent work.

Consider a smooth and projective Calabi-Yau threefold
X
, and a nondegenerate
t
-structure with Noetherian Abelian heart
\mathcal{A}_{c}
on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
satisfying generic flatness. Then, from Halpern-Leistner’s Proposition
7.12
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an Artin
1
-stack locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal. Consider the correspondence:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}(\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}

obtained by restricting the
\Theta
-correspondence to the open subset of
{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
of graded objects
(E_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
E_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}
,
E_{1}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}
and
E_{i}=0
for
i\neq 0,1
. According to Lemma
7.14
, the
k
-points of
{\rm Filt}_{2}(\mathcal{A})
are the short exact sequences
0\to E\to F\to G\to 0
of objects of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
such that
[E]=\gamma_{1}
,
[G]=\gamma_{2}
, and then
[F]=\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}
. Similarly, consider the following commutative diagram:

(7.93)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times p_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times\eta_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}

where the upper right and lower left squares are Cartesian, which is obtained by restricting the diagram
6.90
to the open subset of
{\rm Grad}({\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}))
of
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
-graded objects
(E_{(i,j)})_{i,j\in\mathbb{Z}}
such that
E_{(0,0)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}
,
E_{(1,0)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}
and
E_{(1,1)}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{3}}
and
E_{(i,j)}=0
for the remaining
(i,j)
. In particular,
{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}
is the stacks of two-steps filtrations of objects of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
, whose graded components have respectively class
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}
.

A Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is a full subcategory
\mathcal{S}
such that, given any short exact sequence
0\to E\to F\to F/E\to 0
in
\mathcal{A}_{c}
,
F\in\mathcal{S}
iff
E,F/E\in\mathcal{S}
. We define then the substack
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
such that, when
R
is of finite type,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(R)
is the full subspace of
E\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(R)
such that
E|_{x}\in\mathcal{S}
for any closed point
x\in{\rm Spec}(R)
. We say that
\mathcal{S}
is locally closed if
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is a locally closed immersion: in particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
is then an Artin
1
-stack locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal. Typical examples are given as follows:

Lemma 7.20
.

Consider
\mathcal{A}_{c}:=\mathcal{P}((\phi,\phi+1])
an Abelian heart from a Bridgeland stability condition, and consider a subinterval
I\subset(\phi,\phi+1]
. Then
\mathcal{S}:=\mathcal{P}(I)
is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
.

Proof.

Consider first the case where
(\phi,\phi+1]
is the ordered union of two intervals
I_{1},I_{2}
. From Definition
7.16

iii)
:

(7.94)

\displaystyle Hom_{\mathcal{A}_{c}}(\mathcal{P}(I_{2}),\mathcal{P}(I_{1}))=Ext^{0}_{D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)}(\mathcal{P}(I_{2}),\mathcal{P}(I_{1}))=0

and, from Definition
7.16

v)
, for each
F\in\mathcal{A}_{c}
, there is a (necessarily unique) exact sequence
0\to E\to F\to G\to 0
with
E\in\mathcal{P}(I_{2})
and
E\in\mathcal{P}(I_{1})
. Then
(\mathcal{P}(I_{1}),\mathcal{P}(I_{2}))
forms a tilting pair of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
, hence both
\mathcal{P}(I_{1}),\mathcal{P}(I_{2})
are Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
. Writing
(\phi,\phi+1]
as an ordered union of the intervals
I_{1},I,I_{2}
, we have
\mathcal{P}(I)=\mathcal{P}(I_{1}\cup I)\cap\mathcal{P}(I\cup I_{2})
, hence it is a Serre subcategory of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
.
∎

Notice that, for any
R
of finite type, and
E\in{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})(R)=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Theta_{R})
(resp.
E\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})(R)=\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(B\mathbb{G}_{m,R})
, by definition,
E\in{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})(R)
(resp
E\in{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})(R)
iff its restriction to every closed point of
\mathbb{A}^{1}_{R}\to\Theta_{R}
(resp.
{\rm Spec}(R)\to B\mathbb{G}_{m,R}
is in
\mathcal{S}
, i.e. iff, for each closed point
x\in{\rm Spec}(R)
, the total object and the associated graded of
E|_{x}
is in
\mathcal{S}
(resp. the total object of
E|_{x}
is in
\mathcal{S}
). As
\mathcal{S}
is a Serre subcategory, an object is in
\mathcal{S}
iff its associated graded is in
\mathcal{S}
, which gives:

\displaystyle{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})

\displaystyle={\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}

(7.95)

\displaystyle{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})

\displaystyle={\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{S}={\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\times_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})}{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})

For
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in K^{num}(X)
, we obtain then a Cartesian diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}

And similarly, we obtain a diagram similar to
7.93
above for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
, defining
{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}
, Cartesian over the diagram for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proposition 7.21
.

Consider a smooth and projective Calabi-Yau threefold
X
, a nondegenerate
t
-structure with Noetherian Abelian heart
\mathcal{A}_{c}
on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
satisfying generic flatness, and a locally closed Serre subcategory
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathcal{A}_{c}

i)

(
[
AHLH18
, Theorem 7.23]
) If
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is bounded, then it admits a separated good moduli space
JH_{\gamma}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}\to M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
, whose
k
-points represents semisimple objects of
\mathcal{S}
. Moreover,
M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is proper if
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is closed.

ii)

If this is the case for any
\gamma\in K^{num}(X)
, there is a canonical
2
-isomorphism making the following diagram commutative:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}}

where, on
k
-points,
\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}
takes the direct sum of semisimple objects, which extends
JH:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to M_{\mathcal{S}}
to a morphism of
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoids in the
2
-category of stacks. This
2
-isomorphism is compatible with the diagram
7.93
.

Proof.

i)

If
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is closed, this is exactly
[
AHLH18
, Theorem 7.23]
. We slightly adapt the proof of
[
AHLH18
, Theorem 7.23]
, accounting for the restriction to a Serre subcategory, which is not necessarily closed. By Halpern-Leistner’s Proposition
7.12
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an Artin
1
-stack locally finite presentation with affine diagonal: as
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is locally closed by assumption, then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is an Artin
1
-stack of finite type (as it is locally of finite presentation and bounded), with affine diagonal. By (
7.5
),
{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma})={\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})\times_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
, and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is
\Theta
-reductive by Lemma
7.14
, i.e. one obtains by base change that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is
\Theta
-reductive.

Consider a discrete valuation ring
R
(DVR) with fraction field
K
and residual field
\kappa
, and a choice of uniformizer
\pi
. As in
[
AHLH18
, Section 3.5]
, consider
\overline{ST}_{R}:=[{\rm Spec}(R[s,t]/(st-\pi))/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, where
s,t
have
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-weights
1,-1
(a different choice of uniformizer
\pi
gives an isomorphic stack). Consider the closed immersion
0:{\rm Spec}(\kappa)\to\overline{ST}_{R}
given by
s=t=0
, such that
\overline{ST}_{R}-0={\rm Spec}(R)\cup_{{\rm Spec}(K)}{\rm Spec}(R)
, i.e. a morphism
\overline{ST}_{R}-0\to\mathcal{X}
is the data of two morphisms
\zeta,\zeta^{\prime}:{\rm Spec}(R)\to\mathcal{X}
, and an isomorphism
\zeta_{{\rm Spec}(K)}\simeq\zeta^{\prime}_{{\rm Spec}(K)}
. In
[
AHLH18
, Definition 3.38]
, a
1
-stack
\mathcal{X}
is said to be
S
-complete if, for any DVR
R
, any morphism
\overline{ST}_{R}-0\to\mathcal{X}
can be uniquely extended to a morphism
\overline{ST}_{R}\to\mathcal{X}
. From
[
AHLH18
, Lemma 7.16]
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies this property with respect to any DVR essentially of finite type. Consider
R
essentially of finite type, and
E\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\overline{ST}_{R}-0)
: in particular, one have
E\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\overline{ST}_{R}-0)
, hence there is a unique
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\overline{ST}_{R})
such that
F|_{\overline{ST}_{R}-0}\simeq E
: it suffices then to show that
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\overline{ST}_{R})
. The restriction along
\Theta_{\kappa}\overset{s=0}{\to}\overline{ST}_{R}
is an objects of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\Theta_{\kappa})
, whose restriction to the open point is in
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\kappa)
, hence, from (
7.5
), we have also
F|_{0}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\kappa)
. As
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is locally of finite presentation, there is by definition a subring of finite type
R^{\prime}
of
R
containing
\pi
, and an object
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}(\overline{ST}_{R^{\prime}})
such that
F^{\prime}|_{\overline{ST}_{R}}\simeq F
,
F^{\prime}|_{\overline{ST}_{R^{\prime}}-0^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\overline{ST}_{R^{\prime}}-0^{\prime})
and
F^{\prime}|_{0^{\prime}}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\kappa)
(where
0^{\prime}:{\rm Spec}(R^{\prime}/\pi)\to\overline{ST}_{R^{\prime}}
). Then, by definition,
F^{\prime}\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\overline{ST}_{R^{\prime}})
, and then
F\in\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}(\overline{ST}_{R})
. We obtain then that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
, and furthermore
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
, are
S
-complete with respect to any DVR essentially of finite type, and
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is of finite type, with affine diagonal: then by
[
AHLH18
, Proposition 3.42]
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
is
S
-complete.

Then
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
, is
\Theta
-reductive and
S
-complete, and moreover of finite type and with affine diagonal. Then
[
AHLH18
, Theorem A]
gives that it admits a separated good moduli space
JH_{\gamma}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}\to M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
, and
[
AHLH18
, Lemma 7.19]
gives that closed points of
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
are the semisimple objects of
\mathcal{S}
(as
\mathcal{S}
is a Serre subcategory, an object of finite type of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is in
\mathcal{S}
iff each of its Jordan-Hölder factors is in
\mathcal{S}
). From
[
AHLH18
, Lemma 7.16]
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
satisfies the existence part of the valuative criteria for properness, hence if
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is closed,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
does it too, hence from
[
AHLH18
, Proposition 3.48]

M_{\mathcal{S}}
is proper.

ii)

By the universal property of good moduli space, the map to an algebraic space:

(7.96)

\displaystyle JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\circ\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\to M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}

factorize uniquely through a map:

(7.97)

\displaystyle\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\to M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}

the same argument for
\oplus_{\gamma_{1},...,\gamma_{r}}
gives a monoidal structure on
M_{\mathcal{S}}
, such that, by construction
JH:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to M_{\mathcal{S}}
enhance to a morphism of monoids. On closed points,
\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}(k)\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}(k)\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}(k)
is obtained by taking the direct sum, hence it is also the case at the level of the good moduli space.

At the level closed points, the diagram commutes because
JH
takes the polystable object associated to an object of finite length, hence commutes with taking the associated graded. Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(M_{\mathcal{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(M_{\mathcal{S}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Grad}(JH)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(JH)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where we have used the fact that
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
is an algebraic space, hence the hyperbolic localization diagram for it is trivial, which gives a natural isomorphism
JH\circ\eta\simeq JH\circ\iota\circ p
. one obtains then by base change a natural isomorphism:

(7.98)

\displaystyle JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\circ\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\simeq JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\circ\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\circ p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\simeq\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\circ(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}})\circ p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}

making that the diagram of the proposition commutes.

Consider the following
2
-commutative diagrams:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}

and:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm Filt}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{3}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times p_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times\eta_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Id\times\oplus_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\oplus_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}}}

Applying the commutative diagram (
6.90
) to
JH
, and using the monoidality of
JH
, we obtain that the
2
-isomorphism making these two diagram commutes are the same, which is the claimed compatibility with diagram
7.93
.

∎

We follow here the discussion of
[
DM16
, Section 3.2]
. For any
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoid object in the
2
-category of stacks
(\mathcal{X}_{\gamma})_{\gamma\in K^{num}(X)}
, we consider the monoidal structure on the triangulated category
\mathcal{D}_{mon}(\mathcal{X})
given by
\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus}=(\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in K^{num}(X)}
:

(7.99)

\displaystyle\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus,\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:=(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(-\boxtimes-)\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

formally, it is obtained by applying the monoidal functor
\mathcal{D}_{!}
to the monoidal object
\mathcal{X}
, and then twisting by the formula:

(7.100)

\displaystyle\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus}(F_{\gamma_{1}},\cdots,F_{\gamma_{n}}):=\boxtimes_{\oplus}(F_{\gamma_{1}},\cdots,F_{\gamma_{n}})\{-\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\langle\gamma_{i},\gamma_{j}\rangle/2\}

We denote by
K^{num}(X)
the scheme
\bigsqcup_{\gamma\in K^{num}(X)}{\rm Spec}(k)
, with the monoidal product
\oplus_{n}:(\gamma_{1},\cdots,\gamma_{n})\mapsto\gamma_{1}+\cdots+\gamma_{n}
, such that a
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoid stack is equivalent to a monoid stack with a monoidal morphism to
K^{num}(X)
. A monoidal morphism
f:\mathcal{X}\to\mathcal{Y}
of
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoid stack induces a monoidal morphism
f_{!}:(\mathcal{D}_{mon}(\mathcal{X}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})\to(\mathcal{D}_{mon}(\mathcal{X}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})
; if
f
is moreover of finite type, it restricts to a monoidal morphism
f_{!}:(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{mon,c,-}(\mathcal{X}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})\to(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{mon,c,-}(\mathcal{X}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})
(recalling the notations from (
4.3
)).

We give now the construction of the CoHA. As we work with cohomology with compact support, we obtain a coalgebra: in general, one consider the Borel-Moore homology, which is obtained by taking the dual of the cohomology with compact support, hence one obtains an algebra.

Theorem 7.22
.

Consider a smooth and projective Calabi-Yau threefold
X
, with a strong orientation data on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
, and a
t
-structure with Noetherian Abelian heart
\mathcal{A}_{c}
on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
satisfying generic flatness. In particular,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is an oriented
d
-critical Artin
1
-stack, locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal. Consider a locally closed Serre subcategory
\mathcal{S}
of
\mathcal{A}_{c}
, and suppose that, for any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in K^{num}(X)
, the map
\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:{\rm Filt}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}
is of finite type.

i)

Then there is a natural coassociative coproduct:

(7.101)

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}},P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}})\to\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}},P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}})\otimes_{k}\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}},P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}})\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

defined from the extension correspondence, giving to
\mathbb{H}_{c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}},P_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}})
a natural structure of comonoid in
(\mathcal{D}(k),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})
(the absolute CoHA)

ii)

If moreover the
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
are of finite type, there is a natural coassociative coproduct:

(7.102)

\displaystyle(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}})\to(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}((JH_{\gamma_{1}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}})\boxtimes(JH_{\gamma_{2}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}}))\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

defined from the extension correspondence, giving to
(JH_{\gamma})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{A}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}})
a natural structure of comonoid in
(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{-}(M_{\mathcal{S}}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})
(the relative CoHA), whose hypercohomology with compact support is the absolute CoHA.

Remark 7.23
.

Consider a smooth projective CY3
X
such that
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
admits a strong orientation data compatible with direct sum (the orientation data of
[
JU21
]
is not known to upgrades to a strong orientation data, but it is plausible that it is the case). The construction of the the absolute CoHa works in particular in the following situation:

•

When
\mathcal{A}_{c}=Coh(X)
is the heart of the classical
t
-structure on
D^{b}{\rm Coh}(X)
: indeed, the corresponding heart is known to be Noetherian, and satisfying generic flatness. The map
{\rm Filt}_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}
is then bounded by the Grothendieck Quot construction, see
[
Joy03
, Proposition 9.5]
. One can then take various Serre subcategory, giving a locally closed substacks: torsion or torsion free sheaves, semistable sheaves for a Gieseker stability conditions, sheaves with support on a locally closed subvariety, sheaves with support of dimension
\leq i
,… In general, the
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma}
will not be bounded, so there will be no relative CoHA, but there is a relative CoHA for locally closed substacks of finite type (e.g., for Gieseker-semistable sheaves).

•

When
\mathcal{A}_{c}
is the heart of a
t
-structure coming from an algebraic stability condition in a good connected component
{\rm Stab}^{\ast}(X)
(e.g., a component containing a stability condition from
[
BMT11
]
), from
[
PT19
, Proposition 4.21]
(Proposition
7.18
here), the heart
\mathcal{A}_{c}=\mathcal{P}((0,1])
is Noetherian and satisfy generic flatness, and by
[
Tod07
, Lemma 3.15]
, this heart satisfies boundedness of quotients, i.e. in particular the maps
\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}
are of finite type. One obtains then an absolute CoHA for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
, and a restriction of it for any subinterval
I\subset(0,1]
(indeed,
\mathcal{P}(I)
is a Serre subcategory from Lemma
7.20
, and it forms an open substack by arguing as in the proof of Theorem
7.19
). When
I
is of length
<1
, the
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma}
are bounded from Theorem
7.19
, and then there is moreover a relative CoHA.

Proof.

From Halpern-Leistner’s Proposition
7.12
and Remark
7.15
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is
d
-critical Artin
1
-stack, with an orientation compatible with direct sum, locally of finite presentation, with affine diagonal. From Theorem
6.14
, we have a canonical isomorphism:

(7.103)

\displaystyle p_{!}\eta^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}\simeq P_{{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})}\{-{\rm Ind}_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}/2\}

From Lemma
7.9
and Corollary
5.9
, the restriction of this isomorphism along the open and closed immersion
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}\to{\rm Grad}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
gives a canonical isomorphism in
\mathcal{A}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}})
:

(7.104)

\displaystyle(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}}\simeq P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}}\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}\simeq P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}}\boxtimes P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}}\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

Consider now
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}\in K^{num}(X)
, and look at
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{2}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma_{3}}
as an open and closed substack of
Map(B\mathbb{G}_{m}\times B\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}})
, where the factors have
\mathbb{Z}^{2}
grading respect
(0,0),(1,0),(1,1)
. Then, by Lemma
6.16
,
7.9

is compatible with products, the following square of isomorphisms in

is commutative (where we denote
P_{\gamma}:=P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma}}
for readability):

(7.105)

(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id)_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id)^{\ast}

(p_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}P_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}}

(P_{\gamma_{1}}\boxtimes P_{\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}\rangle/2\}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

where the vertical arrows come from (

), and the upper horizontal one by base change in the diagram
7.93
. Restricting along
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{i}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A},\gamma}
, and using the fact that the diagrams of filtrations for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
are Cartesian over those for
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
as
\mathcal{S}
is a Serre subcategory, we obtain natural isomorphisms in
\mathcal{D}^{b}_{mon,c}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}})
:

(7.106)

\displaystyle(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}})\simeq(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}})\boxtimes(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}})\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

which fits into a similar commutative diagram.

We describe now how to build the relative CoHA of
ii)
, using the morphism
JH:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to M_{\mathcal{S}}
to the
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoid
M_{\mathcal{S}}
: the absolute CoHA is built exactly in the same way, using instead the morphism
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to K^{num}(X)
to the
K^{num}(X)
-graded monoid
K^{num}(X)
, and the fact that the hypercohomology with compact support of the relative CoHA is the abosolute CoHA is obtained by applying the monoidal map
(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}\to K^{num}(X))_{!}:(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{-}(\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})\to(\mathcal{D}^{-}_{-}(K^{num}(X)),\boxtimes^{tw}_{\oplus})
to the relative CoHA will then be automatic.

From Lemma
7.14
,
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}
is
\Theta
-reductive, and then from (
7.5
),
\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S}}
is
\Theta
-reductive too, i.e. the maps
\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}:\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\times\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}\to\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}
satisfies the valuative criterion for properness with respect to any DVR. Then, as they are moreover assumed to be of finite type, they are then proper, and representable by algebraic space: in particular, the natural morphism
(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}\to(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{\ast}
is an isomorphism, i.e. we get a natural morphism:

(7.107)

\displaystyle Id\to(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{\ast}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}\simeq(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}

which will be the key point, as it is generally the case to build CoHA. Notice that the morphism
\eta_{!}\to\eta_{\ast}
is compatible with base change and composition by Lemma
2.9
and
2.11
, and the adjunction morphisms are also compatible with base change and composition, so we obtain that the morphism
Id\to\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}
, for a proper morphism
\eta
, is compatible with base change and composition. Using the commutation of the diagram of Proposition
7.21

ii)
, we obtain a natural morphism:

\displaystyle(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}})_{!}

\displaystyle\to(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}

(7.108)

\displaystyle\simeq(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}})_{!}(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}

Using the fact that
Id\to\eta_{!}\eta^{\ast}
is compatible with composition and base change in the commutative diagrams at the end of the proof of Proposition
7.21

ii)
, we obtain the following commutative diagram:

(7.109)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}}

(\oplus_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(p_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

(\oplus_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id)_{!}

(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id)_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}}\times Id)^{\ast}

(p_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}(Id\times\oplus_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}}\times JH_{\gamma_{3}})_{!}

(Id\times p_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})_{!}(Id\times\eta_{\gamma_{2},\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}+\gamma_{3}})^{\ast}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}

We obtain finally the comultiplication:

\displaystyle(JH_{\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}})

\displaystyle\to(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(JH_{\gamma_{1}}\times JH_{\gamma_{2}})_{!}(p_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}(\eta_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})^{\ast}(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}+\gamma_{2}}})

(7.110)

\displaystyle\simeq(\oplus_{\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}})_{!}\Bigl((JH_{\gamma_{1}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{1}}})\boxtimes(JH_{\gamma_{1}})_{!}(P_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{A}}}|_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{S},\gamma_{2}}})\Bigr)\{-\langle\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\rangle/2\}

And the two commutative diagrams
7.105
and
7.109
give the associativity of the comultiplication.
∎

Remark 7.24
.

We have built here the CoHA as a comonoidal object in a triangulated category. Using the formalism of
2
-segal spaces and the Waldhausen construction from
[
DK12
]
, and the homotopy coherent version of six functor formalisms, one could probably write it as a comonoid in a stable
(\infty,1)
-category. Indeed, the only part in the above construction which uses the gluing technology from
[
BBD+15
]
is the construction of the isomorphism (
7.104
), and the check that the diagram (
7.105
) is commutative, which is done in an Abelian category. The remaining part use the six operations, and do not need any gluing. As this paper is written mostly in the
2
-categorical language, we have not tried to do such a homotopy coherent construction. Moreover, there seems to be no substantial need to give a homotopy coherent version of this construction, as the CoHA is defined globally, and one do not have to glue it from local presentations using quiver with potential.
